# Findings

Dated investigations that belong to no single arc. Row format, states and the
rules for adding, changing and retiring a row are in [[records/README]]. Prefix
is `FD`.

Every row here was a `.planning/FINDING-*.md` file, written into a gitignored
directory where a second reader could not find it. The investigation itself is
long-form and stays where it is written; the row is the part that survives a
fresh clone. `evidence:` names the investigation file **and** the live tree
spans, because the investigation is untracked and the tree is not.

⚑ Two of these investigations cite `scaffold/` paths and Python modules that the
migration evicted. Where that is so, the row says it, and the `measured:` line
carries a span in the live tree instead. A citation nobody can open is not
evidence.

## Compiler defects found while building tools

### FD-01 `str-sub` clamps, says a load-bearing comment

- state:    RETIRED
- claim:    `lib/prelude/string.chiral:14` says "str-sub clamps, so a too-long prefix is just false", and `str-starts-with` is written to depend on it.
- measured: MOVED to PRB-47 in records/lenses/problems.md (2026-09-05): the lens row carries the live state, this row is the history.  two range cases were unguarded. `end < start` computed a negative length, reached the allocator and killed the process (`(str-len (str-sub "abc" 2 1))`, SIGSEGV, exit 139). `end > len` did not clamp: `(str-len (str-sub "abc" 0 99))` returned 99, a `Str` of length 99 over a 3-byte buffer, 96 bytes of adjacent memory, no error and no truncation. The inverted-range half was FIXED 2026-08-31: `nb-bslice-t` clamps an inverted range to the empty slice, fixpoint held at gen2==gen3 at 1,102,200 B and the promoted binary rebuilt byte-identically. **The `end > len` half is untouched**, so the cited comment is still false and `str-starts-with` still depends on a read past the buffer returning differing bytes. That is why this row is OPEN and not FIXED.
- evidence: `lib/prelude/string.chiral:14-17`, `lib/lowering/tal/erase.chiral:114`, `lib/lowering/tal/bytes.chiral` (`nb-bslice`, `nb-bslice-t`), `.planning/FINDING-str-sub-range-2026-08-31.md`
- checked:  2026-09-01
- element:  UNASSIGNED

### FD-02 a `let` over a `case` on a computed comparison cannot be proven

- state:    RETIRED
- claim:    refinement checking accepts a two-line minimum function. Found porting `tools/paren-audit/paren-audit.py` to `prog/paren-audit.prog`, which needed one and could not have one.
- measured: MOVED to PRB-48 in records/lenses/problems.md (2026-09-05): the lens row carries the live state, this row is the history.  `(let (m (case (<i a b) (true a) (false b))) m)` fails with "cannot prove refinement". The trigger needs all four at once: a `case`, a **computed** comparison as scrutinee rather than a `Bool` that arrived as a variable, arms returning **bound variables** rather than literals, and a `let`-bound result rather than a direct return. Any one of the four removed and it passes. `cond` fails identically, as expected, since it desugars to `case`. The first hypothesis in the investigation was REFUTED by its own diagnosis section; the mechanism is recorded there. Three coherent fixes exist and they differ in what they preserve, so this needs a blueprint rather than a patch.
- measured (diagnostic half): the *message* cannot be improved cheaply either. No `Reason` arm fits, and adding one costs an arm in all seven exhaustive `case`s over `Reason` in `diag.chiral` (there is deliberately no `_`). Nothing in the tree can print a surface `Term`: `dg-mismatch-msg` is `(lam (w) "type mismatch")`, E158 has zero code in `lib/` or `prog/`, and `lib/typing/pretty.chiral` declares its own private 5-former `Term` and has no importers. Binder names are gone from `Ctx`. The one cheap real improvement is splitting `jg-refine-unproved` into two arms, because `kernel.chiral:1439-1440` collapses two different refusals into one string.
- evidence: `lib/typing/kernel.chiral:1439-1440`, `lib/typing/diag.chiral:20-25`, `:314-315`, `:346`, `lib/typing/pretty.chiral:15-20`, `prog/paren-audit.prog`, `.planning/FINDING-let-bound-case-refinement-2026-08-31.md`
- checked:  2026-09-01
- element:  UNASSIGNED

## Refuted premises

### FD-03 the closure refusal is about a bare lambda, not about capture

- state:    RETIRED
- claim:    `.planning/USER-LAYER-GAP.md` §10 records "k>=1, real capture -> REFUSED" and reads it as a language limitation: captured closures in a record do not lower.
- measured: MOVED to PRB-49 in records/lenses/problems.md (2026-09-05): the lens row carries the live state, this row is the history. REFUTED in both directions by a single fixture pair. A fixture with **zero** captures and a bare `(lam ...)` written directly in a data-constructor argument position is refused; the same lambda with the same captures wrapped in `(the (-> ...) ...)` lowers, runs and exits 0, as does a call that returns a closure. The discriminator is purely syntactic: a bare `(lam ...)` in constructor position is never registered as a closure-conversion site. A second, independent limitation is real and separate: the closure-returning projector shape works at one instance and fails at two, exactly as `specialize-singleton.chiral` documents. **Nothing has been applied.** The run's write surface was the finding plus fixtures under a `scaffold/tests/samples/_wip/` path the migration has since evicted, so the fixtures are gone and §10 still carries the wrong table.
- evidence: `lib/lowering/upper/closconv.chiral`, `lib/lowering/upper/specialize-singleton.chiral`, `.planning/FINDING-captured-closures-2026-08-30.md` (§0, §1.3, §6), `.planning/USER-LAYER-GAP.md` §10
- checked:  2026-09-01
- element:  UNASSIGNED

### FD-04 mutually-recursive `data` types are built, not missing

- state:    FIXED
- claim:    the 2026-07-31 finding says chirality's surface elaborator cannot express mutually-recursive `data` types and calls it an unbuilt prerequisite for the self-hosted checker.
- measured: built. It landed 2026-08-01 as E79 and it is chirality today, not the Python it was written against: `lib/surface/parse.chiral:1319-1446` is the data-group phase, registering every data name before any field is elaborated, and `diag.chiral` carries the `rl-parse` reason arm for it. Two deltas from the recommendation as written are recorded in the finding: plain forward (DAG) references are supported too, subsumed by SCC-topo processing at no cost, and termination needed no change because the structural token chain is type-agnostic. Strict positivity is still refused, by design, with its own judgment (`jg-not-positive`). The finding's own citations (`surface.py`, `data.py`, `scaffold/`) are all dead paths.
- evidence: `lib/surface/parse.chiral:632`, `:1319-1322`, `:1446`, `lib/typing/diag.chiral:73`, `:111`, `:469`, `.planning/archive/FINDING-mutual-data-2026-07-31.md`

### FD-07 the secure-datum model's threat split is drawn in the wrong place

- state:    RETIRED
- claim:    `docs/definitions/secure-datum-model.md` §2 puts peripheral DMA **write** (tamper, replay/rollback) in scope and CPU code execution out of scope, and §1 says the register root "is unreachable by the threat".
- measured: MOVED to PRB-51 in records/lenses/problems.md (2026-09-05): the lens row carries the live state, this row is the history. on the stated hardware (no TPM, no IOMMU relied on) DMA write is arbitrary physical memory write, which is a routine path to CPU code execution: overwrite kernel text, a function pointer or a page-table entry. PCILeech, which §2 names as the in-scope tool, ships kernel implants that do exactly this. So the out-of-scope list is a consequence of a power the model grants rather than a power the attacker lacks, and the document's own §1 supplies the verdict, that N layers sharing one dependency collapse to one. What survives intact is the whole model against a **read-only** DMA adversary, the evil-maid and Thunderbolt-snapshot case: register root, derive-not-store, the interrupt-disabled window and every §4 confidentiality multiplier are sound there. Three edits are owed and none applied: split §2 into A_read and A_write with a guarantee per half, move write/tamper/rollback out of the confidentiality stack into an integrity section whose verb is *detect*, and say that A_write requires the IOMMU the document lists as optional.
- evidence: `docs/definitions/secure-datum-model.md` §1, §2, §4, `.planning/FINDING-datum-model-write-adversary-2026-08-31.md`
- checked:  2026-09-01
- element:  none. Document-tier, not an element. If the split becomes a language obligation, that mints a row.

⚑ FD-07 is on the ownership-and-trust track, which is [[goals/ownership-and-trust]],
deferred out of scope for *work* on 2026-08-31. The row is here because the
document is in the reader-facing tier and is wrong today. Deferred is not deleted:
`.planning/FINDING-datum-model-write-adversary-2026-08-31.md` stays where it is.

## The committed compiler

### FD-08 the shipped binary trails its sources by one generation

- state:    FIXED
- claim:    the build rule requires `build-new -> test -> promote`, and `docs/decisions/decision-lane-split.md:112-116` makes a precondition of it: on the unmodified tree `B1(blob)` must already equal `bin/chirality-bin`, so a staleness inherited from a merge is caught as a merge's and not blamed on the element promoting next. The arc file records that precondition passing for E181 on 2026-09-01.
- measured: it does not hold on master at `3be8915`, and the reason is ordinary rather than a defect. The tree reproduces itself at generation two. Blob 804,277 B. `bin/chirality-bin` is 1,147,256 B and was last promoted at `58603c3` (E181, 2026-09-01). `B1 = bin/chirality-bin(blob)` is 1,184,120 B and differs from the shipped binary at char 98. `B1 != B2`, differing at char 1,180,606; `B2 == B3`, so the tree reaches a fixpoint at generation two rather than generation one. Every artifact was checked non-empty before its `cmp`. 13 commits have touched `lib/` or `prog/` since that promotion, spanning three arcs (`aedf555` E11, `13a1362`-`e882568` E173, `7341ddf` ports, `d8bcec5` the C backend drop), so the lag belongs to no one element. `B1 != B2` is the expected two-generation bootstrap: `7341ddf` made the arena counter readable by a compiled program, which changes emitted code, so the old binary does not emit that body and `B1` does. Convergence at `B2 == B3` is the fixpoint. Nothing here is broken. The one consequence that survives is attribution: an element promoting from this base reports blob and binary deltas that carry 13 commits of other arcs' work, so it either promotes once beforehand or reports the two deltas separately. E182 is the next such element. ⚑ **FIXED 2026-09-02 at `4047a66`, and the side that moved is the tree.** E182 entered the closure and promoted. Its commit 3 (`e42d3af`) shipped generation one while measuring the fixpoint at generation two, which would have re-created this condition deliberately rather than inheriting it; commit 3b promoted the fixpoint instead. Verified independently on the unmodified tree afterwards: blob 806,827 B, `B1` 1,188,216 B, `cmp B1 bin/chirality-bin` **EQUAL**. The precondition at `docs/decisions/decision-lane-split.md:112-116` holds for the first time since E181. The inherited `1,147,256 → 1,184,120` is now folded into a shipped binary that reproduces itself, so the next element in the closure separates its own delta with a single `cmp`.
- evidence: `docs/definitions/working-discipline.md:19-42`, `docs/decisions/decision-lane-split.md:105-119`, `docs/arcs/diagnostics-arc.md:68`, `records/diagnostics-arc-record.md:162-164`, `git log 58603c3..HEAD -- lib prog`
- checked:  2026-09-02
- element:  E182, whose promotion at `4047a66` fixed it

## The judgment

### FD-09 the demanded statement is prose, and the judgment's decomposition is unreached

- state:    RETIRED
- claim:    [[certificate-discipline]] states that a certificate's one surviving vacuity is whether the demanded statement is meaningful, and pins it by fixing that statement at the socket as part of the small audited spec, so vacuity "lives in exactly one place ... one small thing audited once". [[decisions/decision-split-checker]] names `kernel-spec` as "the type theory as a small, human-audited requirement type" and one of the three parts the checker splits into.
- measured: MOVED to PRB-52 in records/lenses/problems.md (2026-09-05): the lens row carries the live state, this row is the history. the statement is a `Str`. `(data SpecRule () (spec-rule (form JForm) (name Str) (statement Str)))`, so the artifact the whole scheme's non-vacuity rests on is unstructured prose, and nothing can check it says anything. The decomposition beside it is unreached: the six `JForm` constructors (`j-check`, `j-infer`, `j-conv`, `j-usage`, `j-data`, `j-membrane`) have **one use each**, their own declaration line; `JForm` 2, `SpecRule` 2, `Spec` 3. No file imports `typing/kernel-core`; the only mention of it anywhere in `lib`, `prog`, `tools` or `bin` is a comment at `reflect-floor.chiral:16` calling it work the port "must carry". It compiles clean standalone (`chirality check` exits 0) and no suite phase runs it, so `recheck` has never been executed against a populated `Spec`. Two live checking concerns have no `JForm` at all: totality (`typing/totality.chiral`, `typing/totality-check.chiral`) is presumably folded into `j-data` and nothing says so, and refinement (`typing/refine.chiral`, `t-refine` at 29 uses across 7 modules) has no form. `docs/arcs/independent-judgment-arc.md` already records the module as "written, unreached" and row `J2` owns wiring it; what is new here is that the statement is prose and that the form set is both inert and incomplete.
- evidence: `lib/typing/kernel-core.chiral:27-29`, `:53-60`, `lib/typing/reflect-floor.chiral:16`, `docs/definitions/certificate-discipline.md`, `docs/decisions/decision-split-checker.md`, `docs/arcs/independent-judgment-arc.md`
- checked:  2026-09-02
- element:  UNASSIGNED

### FD-14 the checkers that collect read their continuation state out of the artifact, and every checker that derives it stops at the first refusal

- state:    OPEN
- claim:    The question, for `PRB-73`: when a type checker or a typed-assembly checker refuses a term, do published implementations stop at the first refusal or collect several, and where do the systems that do both draw the line between the judgment and the instrument that measures it? **Both are published practice, and the split follows one structural fact instead of a policy preference.** A checker collects several refusals when the type of every value and the state at every block boundary is already written in the artifact under check, so a refusal costs the walk nothing. A checker whose environment is produced by the check itself takes one of three shapes: it stops at the first refusal, it is made total so that no term is ever refused, or it poisons the continuation and then suppresses what the poisoned continuation reports. `ck-instrs` threads a derived `REnv` (`lib/lowering/tal/check.chiral:225-230`, `(tck-ok env2)` feeds the next instruction), which puts this tree in the second family, and the prior art offers it three shapes. None of the three is plain collect-all with the rules unchanged.
- measured: **Fail-fast is what a specified judgment produces, in four independent specifications.** TAL states its static semantics as inference rules with no error object anywhere in them: TALTOPLAS:4744 "The purpose of the static semantics is to specify when programs are well formed" and TALTOPLAS:4746 "this is expressed by the judgment", TALTOPLAS:4753 "consists of 13 judgments". A program either has a derivation or it has none, and the specification carries no count of failing rules. CompCert is the same shape with the algorithm attached and machine-checked: the type system is a `Prop` (`wt_instr`, `wt_function`, COMPCERTRTL:170 "Record wt_function (f: function) (env: regenv): Prop :="), the checker is a function into the error monad (COMPCERTERR:45 "or [Error msg] to indicate failure.", COMPCERTERR:56 "Definition bind (A B: Type) (f: res A) (g: A -> res B) : res B :=", whose `Error` case short-circuits), and the instruction fold **explicitly keeps the first error and skips every later instruction**: COMPCERTRTL:334 "Definition type_code (e: S.typenv): res S.typenv :=" folds over `f.(fn_code)` with COMPCERTRTL:337 "| Error _ => re". That is `ck-instrs`, in a verified compiler, on purpose. The reason it can be on purpose is the correctness statement: COMPCERTRTL:580 "forall env, type_function = OK env -> wt_function f env." constrains the accept side alone, and COMPCERTRTL:757 "Theorem type_function_complete:" fixes accept against reject, so **nothing in either theorem gives a second error any verified meaning**. The JVM specifies verification as a Prolog predicate: JVMS4:12255 "A class is type safe if all its methods are type" safe, and JVMS4:12289 "predicate <code class=\"literal\">classIsTypeSafe</code> is not true, the type" then JVMS4:12290 "checker must throw the exception". A predicate holds or fails. One `VerifyError` is the whole output the specification defines. WebAssembly draws the judgment/instrument line in the document itself: WASMVALALG:7 "The specification of WebAssembly :ref:`validation <valid>` is purely *declarative*." and the appendix then WASMVALALG:10 "sketches the skeleton of a sound and complete *algorithm*" that WASMVALALG:13 "performs only a single pass over it." Its errors are bare `error_if` calls with no accumulator and no defined continuation; WASMVALALG:16 "The algorithm is expressed in typed pseudo code whose semantics is intended to be self-explanatory." is as far as the spec goes on what `error_if` does. **Collect-all is what a checker over an explicitly typed artifact produces, and both instances found are typed IRs.** LLVM's verifier accumulates: the check macro reports and returns from the enclosing check function, LLVMVERIFY:513 "/// We know that cond should be true, if not print an error message." and LLVMVERIFY:514 "#define Check(C, ...)"; `CheckFailed` sets a sticky flag, LLVMVERIFYH:179 "  void CheckFailed(const Twine &Message) {" and LLVMVERIFYH:182 "    Broken = true;"; and the walk runs to the end of the function before the flag is read, LLVMVERIFY:233 "  bool verify(const Function &F) {", LLVMVERIFY:269 "    // FIXME: We strip const here because the inst visitor strips const." and LLVMVERIFY:270 "    visit(const_cast<Function &>(F));". So LLVM is **fail-fast within one instruction and collect-all across the function**, which is exactly the granularity `enforcement/N12` needs and exactly the granularity `ck-instrs` lacks. It is affordable there because LLVM IR carries a type on every value, so a refused instruction leaves the next instruction's inputs fully typed. Cranelift is the same, and it is the one system found that **draws the line inside a single walk and documents where**: CRANEVERIFY:178 "pub struct VerifierErrors(pub Vec<VerifierError>);" is the accumulator, CRANEVERIFY:215 "    /// Report a fatal error and return `Err`." and CRANEVERIFY:221 "    /// Report a non-fatal error and return `Ok`." are the two severities, and CRANEVERIFY:165 "meaning that the verification process may continue. However, other (non-fatal)" errors are reported without stopping. The instruction loop propagates only the fatal kind (CRANEVERIFY:2128 "            for inst in self.func.layout.block_insts(block) {", CRANEVERIFY:2132 "                self.typecheck(inst, errors)?;"), and structural damage the rest of the walk depends on is fatal (CRANEVERIFY:2126 "                return errors.fatal((block, format!(\"{block} cannot be empty\")));"). The placement of type errors is stated in one comment: CRANEVERIFY:1325 "        // Typechecking instructions is never fatal", and the five type checks below it run unconditionally with their step results discarded, CRANEVERIFY:1326 "        let _ = self.typecheck_results(inst, ctrl_type, errors);". **The JVM's own answer to the derived-environment problem is to stop deriving it.** The type-checking verifier requires declared frames: JVMS4:12243 "The type checker requires a" list of stack map frames, and each frame JVMS4:12249 "specifies the verification type of each operand stack entry and of" each local at the start of each basic block. The inference verifier of JVMS4:12207 "type checking may fail even though the bytecode is in principle valid" is the older dataflow one. Making the continuation state part of the artifact is the same move LLVM and Cranelift make by typing every value. **Surface typers collect and then suppress, and both say the suppression is the point.** rustc poisons: RUSTCTYERR:303 "There is a `TyKind::Error` that is produced when the user makes a type error." with the stated intent RUSTCTYERR:305 "we would propagate this type and suppress other errors that come up due to it so as not to overwhelm" the user, guarded by RUSTCTYERR:309 "The compiler should **never** produce `Error` unless we **know** that an error has already been" reported, because RUSTCTYERR:315 "It's important to maintain this invariant because the whole point of the `Error` type is to suppress" other errors. GHC does the same at the constraint solver: GHCTCERR:327 "The cec_suppress flag says \"don't report any errors\".  Instead, just create" evidence bindings, applied when GHCTCERR:331 "  * If there are insoluble Givens, then we are in unreachable code and all bets" GHCTCERR:332 "    are off.  So don't report any further errors." and because GHCTCERR:335 "    simple-constraint errors are a knock-on effect of the insolubles." Ramsey names the class and gives the discipline: RAMSEYSPUR:12 "error messages in which only " the first represents a true error, fixed by an extended error monad where RAMSEYSPUR:29 "The type constructor distinguishes intermediate results that are" unavailable, at a measured cost of RAMSEYSPUR:41 "the discipline adds at most&nbsp;5--10% to" total compile time. So the two mature typers that continue past a refusal **report the continuation's findings as lower confidence by not reporting them at all**. That is a direct hit on the census: a suppressed class is invisible to an instrument in the same way a skipped class is. **The third shape is to make the judgment total.** MARKEDLAM:13 "leaving ill-typedexpressions formally meaningless" is named as the defect, answered by a marked calculus MARKEDLAM:13 "together with a total procedure for marking arbitrary unmarked expressions", where errors are non-empty holes and the marked term is well-typed by construction, so the continuation environment is defined without a guess. That paper also states the field's baseline in one sentence: MARKEDLAM:30 "is to localize the rst error that causes type-checking to fail and emit an explanatory error message before terminating." is described there as common even in production type checkers, with MARKEDLAM:32 "An implementation with support for type error recovery, like Elm's compiler or OCaml's" merlin as the other camp. It also states the cost the tree's fail-fast argument predicts: MARKEDLAM:36 "one localization decision can inuence downstream localization decisions", and MARKEDLAM:36 "this error mightmislead the programmer if the earlier localization guess was incorrect". **One rule set under two policies exists, and it is a switched sink instead of a second walk.** `go/types` publishes it in the API: GOTYPESAPI:159 "	// If Error != nil, it is called with each error found" during type checking, and GOTYPESAPI:164 "	// If Error == nil, type-checking stops with the first" error found. The two policies stay in agreement because there is one rule set and the strict policy is an unwind out of the same walk: GOTYPESERR:219 "		panic(bailout{}) // record first error and exit". `go/types` also marks the secondary findings it emits, GOTYPESAPI:161 "	// Secondary errors (for instance, to enumerate all types" involved in an invalid recursive type declaration) carry a leading tab. **How the surface typers differ from this tree.** rustc, GHC, OCaml and `go/types` check an artifact that declares almost nothing, so the environment is inferred everywhere and there is no declared state to fall back to. Their reader is a person who discards a bad message at no cost. `ck-prog` reads a lowered IR whose functions already carry signatures, and its reader is `enforcement/N12`, which counts. A suppressed or speculative class changes the number a census prints, which is the difference that makes the surface-typer answer non-transferable. **Not settled, and searched for.** No source found publishes a measured cascade rate for type checking. The one measured cascade number located is for LR parser error recovery, out of this question's scope and not pinned. No system was found that runs one rule set twice as two separate walks and publishes an argument that the two stay in agreement; `go/types` is the closest and it is one walk with a switched sink. Proof-Carrying Code is missing from the pins: the POPL 1997 paper at `people.eecs.berkeley.edu/~necula/Papers/pcc_popl97.pdf` returned HTTP 404 from this sandbox and no other copy was pinned, so nothing here rests on it. The TAL and marked-lambda pins are PDF text extractions and carry the transcriber's reading: this sandbox has no `pdftotext` and no PDF library, the extractor is a Flate-plus-`TJ` walk written for this run, and both sources lose their `fi` and `ff` ligatures, which is why MARKEDLAM:30 reads "rst" and MARKEDLAM:36 reads "inuence". Word spacing at line joins is lost in MARKEDLAM, which is why MARKEDLAM:13 reads "ill-typedexpressions". Line numbers in both are extraction lines and do not correspond to the printed pages.
- evidence: `lib/lowering/tal/check.chiral:225-230`, `:300-312`, `docs/arcs/enforcement-arc.md` roster row `enforcement/N12`, `records/lenses/problems.md` `PRB-73`. Pins added 2026-09-08, all in `.planning/sources/`: `TALTOPLAS` (Morrisett, Walker, Crary and Glew, "From System F to Typed Assembly Language", TOPLAS 1999, PDF at cs.princeton.edu, origin `transcribed`), `COMPCERTRTL` and `COMPCERTERR` (CompCert `backend/RTLtyping.v` and `common/Errors.v`, raw), `JVMS4` (Java Virtual Machine Specification SE 21 chapter 4, docs.oracle.com, raw HTML), `WASMVALALG` (WebAssembly core spec `appendix/algorithm.rst`, raw), `LLVMVERIFY` and `LLVMVERIFYH` (`llvm/lib/IR/Verifier.cpp` and `VerifierInternal.h`, raw), `CRANEVERIFY` (Cranelift `codegen/src/verifier/mod.rs`, raw), `GOTYPESAPI` and `GOTYPESERR` (`go/types` `api.go` and `errors.go`, raw), `RUSTCTYERR` (rustc-dev-guide `src/ty.md`, raw), `GHCTCERR` (`compiler/GHC/Tc/Errors.hs`, raw), `RAMSEYSPUR` (Ramsey, "Eliminating Spurious Error Messages", abstract page at cs.tufts.edu, raw), `MARKEDLAM` (Zhao, Maroof, Dukkipati, Blinn, Pan and Omar, "Total Type Error Localization and Recovery with Holes", POPL 2024, PDF at hazel.org, origin `transcribed`). The dates are the UTC stamps `xlat pin` writes.

### FD-15 the published theorem is five lemmas each naming a type translation, and the tree has no type translation to name

- state:    OPEN
- claim:    The question, for `.planning/TAL-CONFORMANCE-QUEUE.md` slice R1: what does the type-preservation theorem state, phase by phase, in the System F to Typed Assembly Language translation, and what obligation does each phase carry? **The theorem is five lemmas of one shape, `⊢ e : τ` implies `⊢ [[e]] : [[τ]]`, one per phase, and every one of them names a total function on TYPES that the phase's term translation is stated against.** Three obligations ride with the five: each translation is total on the well-typed programs of its input calculus, each intermediate form is an independently typed calculus with its own static semantics, and the target carries subject reduction and progress so that the composite means something. The tree satisfies the fifth phase's instruction-level obligation and none of the three riders. Its type translation is `ttype` at `lib/lowering/upper/lower.chiral:35`, typed `(-> UT (Maybe TalTy))`, which is partial and non-injective, and a lemma of the published shape cannot be stated over it. The divergence begins in `lower.chiral`, upstream of `tal-ty=?`; the checker's word-compatibility is a consequence of the type translation already being non-injective, and not an independent choice.
- measured: **The compiler is five translations and each one is proved separately.** TALTR:76 "Our compiler is structured as five translations between six typed calculi:" and TALTR:15 "Our translation to TAL is specified as a sequence of type-preserving transformations, includ-" ing CPS and closure conversion. The five phases, their type translations and their lemmas, in order. **CPS conversion**, λF to λK, translates an arrow into a continuation-taking function returning void: TALTR:286 "K[[t->t]]=(K[[t]],N[[t->void" and the polymorphic case `K[[∀a.t]] = ∀[a].(¬K[[t]])->void`, proved by TALTR:537 "Lemma 3.2 (CPS Conversion Type Correctness)". **Closure conversion**, λK to λC, translates a code type into an existential package pairing instantiated code with a value environment whose type is hidden: TALTOPLAS:178 "ofthecode). Weuseanexistentialtypetohidethetypeoftheenvironmentdata" structure, and TALTOPLAS:1953 "for the closure. The closure itself is a pair consisting of a piece of code instantiated" with types for its free type variables. Proved by TALTR:710 "Lemma 4.2 (Closure Conversion Type Correctness)". **Hoisting**, λC to λH, is the one phase whose type translation is the identity: TALTR:737 "No translation is required for types." The judgment still moves, because heap types are added to every value and term typing judgment. Proved by TALTR:744 "Lemma 4.4 (Hoisting Type Correctness)". **Allocation**, λH to λA, puts an initialization flag on every tuple field so that a projection of an uninitialized field has no derivation: TALTR:1043 "All that happens is that initialization flags are added to each field of tuple types:". Proved by TALTR:1064 "Lemma 5.2 (Allocation Type Correctness)". **Code generation**, λA to TAL, turns a function type into a register-file type: TALTR:1910 "translation of function types, which must assign registers to value arguments:" giving TALTR:1912 "T[[forall[a~](t,...,t)->void]]=forall[a~]{r1:T[[t]],...,rn:T[[t]]}". Proved by TALTR:2016 "Lemma 6.5 (TAL Conversion Type Correctness)". The composite is a corollary of the five: TALTR:2274 "Lemma 7.2 (Compiler Type Correctness)", TALTOPLAS:5909 "Corollary (Compiler Type Correctness)." **Three obligations ride beside the five lemmas and none of them is about types travelling.** First, each phase carries a companion existence lemma making the translation TOTAL on well-typed input, stated as an iff: TALTR:533 "Lemma 3.1emptyset;emptyset|-e:tif and only if there existsesuch that|-e:te." with one such lemma per phase. A phase that skips an input it was handed has no theorem, because the antecedent of the type-correctness lemma is satisfied and the consequent has nothing to name. Second, every intermediate form is a language in its own right: TALTR:80 "Each of these calculi is used as a first-class programming calculus in the sense that each translation" TALTR:81 "accepts any well-typed program of its input calculus; it does not assume that the input is the" output from the preceding translation, which is what licenses optimization between any two phases. Third, the target judgment has to be worth arriving at: TALTR:1664 "Lemma 6.1 (Sub ject Reduction)" plus progress and the type-safety corollary. **The obligation each pass carries is stated in the sources phase by phase.** The CPS pass must read the source DERIVATION and not the source term: CPSFOMEGA:720 "The CPS transforms are defined by induction on typing" derivations, which then owes a coherence argument because two derivations of one term must agree, CPSFOMEGA:732 "forms determined by each of these typing derivations are" equivalent up to constructor equality. Its statement is CPSFOMEGA:748 "Theorem 5.5 (CBV CPS Typing)", and the type-level half is separate: CPSFOMEGA:688 "Theorem 5.2 (Constructor Well-formedness Preservation)". The closure-conversion pass owes an abstraction boundary: TYPEDCLOS:559 "The translation of an arrowtype is a pair consisting of code" and an environment whose type is TYPEDCLOS:562 "abstract using an existential.", and the phase is split into stages each of which is TYPEDCLOS:221 "Each stage is defined as a type-directed" translation with correctness TYPEDCLOS:223 "lished using logical relations." The code-generation pass owes a register assignment and a word discipline: TALTR:1218 "Another technical point is that registers may contain onlywordvalues, which are integers, pointers" into the heap, so tuples and code blocks are heap allocated and every code block acquires a type in the heap type. **The stack variant moves the obligation without dropping it.** STAL:17 "tween CPS-based compilation and stack-based compilation. The latter is seen as a continuation-" passing style whose continuation closures are unboxed by the caller, and STAL:20 "conventions by specifying them as formal translations of source function types to STAL types." Dropping CPS costs a new type former: STAL:451 "The solution to both problems is to abstract the type of the stack using a stack type variable:" giving STAL:452 "forall[r].{r1:int,sp:r,ra:forall[].{r1:int,sp:r}}", and the abstraction is what does the work, STAL:456 "because the type checker treatsras an abstract stack type while checking the body of the code." **Erasure in the published system is erasure from EXECUTION, and the artifact under check keeps its types.** TALTR:586 "In our approach, we assume atype-erasureinterpretation of polymorphism as inThe Definition" of Standard ML, and the same paper is explicit that the check does not inherit that: TALTR:1247 "a type-erasure interpretation, we do not erase the types from the operational semantics presented" here, so that subject reduction can be stated, repeated at TALTOPLAS:4352 "cussed above, we ultimately intend a type-erasure interpretation, we do not erase" the types. What the theorem buys at the assembler is stated plainly: TALX86:151 "If the Popcorn compiler is implemented correctly," type-checking the files it produces will never fail, TALX86:153 "duces will never fail. By runningtalc, however, we" TALX86:154 "are no longer assuming that the Popcorn compiler" produces safe code. **The tree's pipeline, and which published phase each pass answers to.** The passes in order are `surface` to `Core` (kernel-checked), `specialize-singletons`, `closconv-sig`, the peel to `NDef` (`lib/lowering/compile-front.chiral:5-9`), `lower-defs`/`compile-fn` through `LCore` to typed SSA `TFn`, `erase-fn` to the erased `NFn`, then x64. **CPS conversion has no counterpart.** The nearest thing is the non-tail-`case` outlining at `lower.chiral:111` and `:313-319`, which splits one function into `name$k` blocks passing the enclosing live registers plus the scrutinee. That is the stack-based alternative STAL:17 names, and STAL's price for it is a stack type former that `TalTy` does not have. **Closure conversion is answered by a different published translation.** `lib/lowering/upper/closconv.chiral` is defunctionalization: a `$clo` sum with one constructor per closure site and an `$apply` dispatcher over it. Its family key erases, at `closconv.chiral:343` `shape-eq` (every non-arrow type is one word, so `(-> A B)`, `(-> I64 I64)` and `(-> Str Bool)` share a family) and `closconv.chiral:382` `arrow-key-eq`. The published phase hides the environment type behind `∃`; this one hides it behind a closed sum, and `TalTy` at `lib/lowering/tal/ssa.chiral:21-22` has neither. **Hoisting is answered.** The `$apply`/`$k` synthesis lifts every site to a top-level def, and the published phase's type translation there is the identity, so nothing is owed. **Allocation is answered on representation and not on typing.** `lib/lowering/tal/erase.chiral` decides immediate versus boxed and resolves constructor names to tags. The published phase's whole content is the initialization flag, and the tree's byte-cell floor carries no type at all: `ssa.chiral:31-33`, `(i-bnew (dst I64) (len I64))`, `(i-bget (dst I64) (ptr I64) (idx I64))` and `(i-bput (ptr I64) (idx I64) (val I64))` are the three instruction forms with no `ty` field, and `check.chiral:215` types every `i-bnew` result as `tt-bytes` regardless of what is stored into it. **Code generation is answered structurally and not at the type level.** `compile-fn` emits `TFn`, and `ssa.chiral:40` is `(tfn (name Str) (params (List TalTy)) (ret TalTy) (body Block) (nregs I64))`: a positional parameter list and a register count where T[[·]] produces a register-file type under a quantifier. **What the erasure discharges.** The instruction-level obligation is met and it is met the way LLVM and Cranelift meet it, which FD-14 measured: every value-defining form in `ssa.chiral:26-30` carries its result type, so a refusal leaves the next instruction's inputs typed. A target judgment exists (`ck-instrs`, `ck-block`, `ck-fn`, `ck-prog` at `check.chiral:225`, `:231`, `:290`, `:309`), and `CEnv` at `ssa.chiral:50` is the heap-type analogue that hoisting's obligation asks for. **What the erasure makes unstatable, in the order the lemmas would need it.** The lemma shape needs a total function on types. `ttype` at `lower.chiral:35` is `(-> UT (Maybe TalTy))` and returns `none` at `lower.chiral:50` for `u-other`, which is every function type and every universe, so the existence half of TALTR:533's iff fails by construction and `LowRes`'s `low-skip` at `lower.chiral:32` is the pass recording that failure as a value. The same function is non-injective at `lower.chiral:49`, `((u-erased) (some (tt-word)))`, so `⊢ [[e]] : [[τ]]` cannot separate τ from any other erased type and the conclusion degrades to representation compatibility. `∀` and `∃` have no target spelling, so neither CPS's `K[[∀a.t]]` nor closure conversion's existential has a `TalTy` to be, and the register-file type T[[·]] produces has no `TFn` field to live in. The code-block half of the heap type is incomplete by the tree's own measurement: `lib/lowering/compile-back.chiral:241-242` records that all 32 checker refusals under the removed `chk-ok` guard were one shape, "a TFn calling its own outlined `<name>$0` block whose signature `lower-defs` never puts in the `CEnv`". `LCore` at `lower.chiral:116-123` carries no types, so the segment between the peel and the SSA emission has no static semantics, which is TALTR:80-81's obligation failing at the one place the tree could most cheaply have met it. **Where the divergence begins.** It does not begin at closure conversion. `shape-eq` erases the family KEY and its output is still `Core`, which the kernel checks, so a preservation statement over that phase is still available. It begins in `lower.chiral`, in two halves that arrive together: `ttype` (`:35-50`) makes the type translation partial and non-injective, and `LCore` (`:116-123`) drops types from terms. `tal-ty=?`'s word-compatibility at `check.chiral:68-77` is downstream of both. An equality relation cannot be nominal when the translation that produced its two sides already sent distinct source types to one target type, so the checker's relation is the shape `ttype` forces and not a separate concession. **The published literature names the price of dropping the annotations.** TALT is the machine-checked foundational typed assembly language, TALT:185 "checkable proofs of type preservation and progress. The" safety of garbage collection established at the same level, and it drops TAL's annotations on purpose: TALT:273 "is a type assignment (or Curry-style) system; values contain" TALT:275 "none of the type annotations they carry in explicitly typed" systems such as TAL. The cost is stated in the next two sentences. Checking then needs inference, and TALT:283 "means that typability checking is almost certainly undecid" able, so TALT:286 "Consequently, the safety certificate for a TALT pro-" TALT:288 "gram is an entire typing derivation" where TALx86 ships a collection of annotations from which a derivation can be reconstructed. Removing the annotations from the artifact moves the certificate, and the tree's `LCore` removes them without putting a derivation in their place. **Not settled, and searched for.** No source was pinned that treats DEFUNCTIONALIZATION as a type-preserving compiler phase with a per-phase preservation lemma; the five-phase literature uses existential closure conversion throughout, and that gap is R4's subject. No pinned source relates an SSA block-argument discipline to TAL's register-file type, so the correspondence between the tree's `Block`/`Branch` floor and TALTR:1912's `{r1:...,rn:...}` rests on nothing published here; that is R5's subject. Whether the tree's divergence is a defect or a costed choice is the author's call and R2 and R3 price it; nothing here rules on it. Four URLs were tried and returned HTTP 404 from this sandbox: `cs.princeton.edu/~dpw/papers/stack.pdf`, `cs.cmu.edu/~rwh/papers/cps/popl93.pdf`, `cs.cornell.edu/talc/papers/talx86-tr.pdf` and `cs.cornell.edu/talc/papers/tali-tr.pdf`; each was replaced by a reachable copy named in `evidence`. The POPL 1998 conference version at `dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/268946.268954` was not fetched, and the December 1998 extended technical report covering the same five phases was pinned as `TALTR` instead. All six new pins are PDF text extractions and carry the transcriber's reading. The extractor was written for this run, is stdlib-only, and decodes OT1, CMMI and CMSY code points plus `/Differences` vectors, so ligatures survive where FD-14's pins lost them; math notation does not survive, subscripts and superscripts land inline, which is why TALTR:533 reads "Lemma 3.1emptyset;emptyset|-e:t" where the page carries subscripted turnstiles, and why the type-translation lines lose their subscripts. `TYPEDCLOS` is a Type3-bitmap PDF whose word spacing and commas are unreliable across most of the file; the two spans quoted from it read cleanly and much of the rest of that pin does not. Line numbers in all six are extraction lines and do not correspond to printed pages.
- evidence: `lib/lowering/upper/lower.chiral:32`, `:35`, `:49-50`, `:111`, `:116-123`, `:313-319`; `lib/lowering/upper/closconv.chiral:343`, `:382`; `lib/lowering/tal/ssa.chiral:21-22`, `:26-30`, `:31-33`, `:40`, `:50`; `lib/lowering/tal/check.chiral:68-77`, `:215`, `:225`, `:231`, `:290`, `:309`; `lib/lowering/tal/erase.chiral:1-9`; `lib/lowering/compile-front.chiral:5-9`; `lib/lowering/compile-back.chiral:235-245`; `.planning/TAL-CONFORMANCE-QUEUE.md` slice R1; `docs/arcs/enforcement-arc.md` requirement 2. Pins added 2026-09-08, all in `.planning/sources/`: `TALTR` (Morrisett, Walker, Crary and Glew, "From System F to Typed Assembly Language (Extended Version)", December 7 1998 technical report, PDF at glew.org, origin `transcribed`), `STAL` (Morrisett, Crary, Glew and Walker, "Stack-Based Typed Assembly Language", PDF at cs.cornell.edu/talc, origin `transcribed`), `TYPEDCLOS` (Minamide, Morrisett and Harper, "Typed Closure Conversion", POPL 1996, PDF at cs.cmu.edu, origin `transcribed`), `CPSFOMEGA` (Harper and Lillibridge, "Explicit Polymorphism and CPS Conversion", POPL 1993, PDF at cs.cmu.edu, origin `transcribed`), `TALX86` (Morrisett, Crary, Glew, Grossman, Samuels, Smith, Walker, Weirich and Zdancewic, "TALx86: A Realistic Typed Assembly Language", WCSSS 1999, PDF at cs.cornell.edu/talc, origin `transcribed`), `TALT` (Crary, "Toward a Foundational Typed Assembly Language", PDF at cs.cmu.edu, origin `transcribed`). `TALTOPLAS` was already pinned by FD-14 and is quoted here. The dates are the UTC stamps `xlat pin` writes.

### FD-16 production compilers coarsen the type on the way down, and every surveyed translation stays total

- state:    OPEN
- claim:    The question, for `.planning/TAL-CONFORMANCE-QUEUE.md` slice R2: where do production compilers lose source types on the way down, and what do they give up by doing it? **Seven were surveyed at their own sources. All seven stop carrying source types at a named IR and put a representation classification in its place, and where a lower-IR checker exists its relation is deliberately coarser than nominal equality on source types.** GHC and CompCert ship the exact shape of `tt-word`: a relation in which any word-sized value matches any other. The tree's non-injective type translation is therefore ordinary production practice and the survey found it everywhere. **The other half of the tree's position had no occupant in the survey.** Every one of the seven translations is total on its input, or it aborts the whole compilation and emits nothing; none of them drops one definition and hands the remainder to its own checker. `ttype`'s `(none)` at `lib/lowering/upper/lower.chiral:50` and the skip at `lib/lowering/compile-back.chiral:270` have no counterpart here. Two further differences are measurable rather than stylistic: the surveyed coarse relations are equivalences and `tal-ty=?` is not transitive, and the two systems that keep a checked target with a coarser type language, the JVM and WebAssembly, pay for the collapse with a runtime cast at every use site.
- measured: **Where each toolchain stops carrying source types, and what it carries after.** *GHC.* Core is System FC, GHCCORE:149 "GHC uses System FC" and Core Lint is its type-checker with a stated guarantee, GHCCORELINT:115 "Core Lint is the type-checker for Core. Using it, we get the following guarantee:". Types leave at Core-to-STG, in two named acts: GHCCORETOSTG:206 "In coreToStgArgs, we drop type arguments completely, but we replace" coercions with a token, and GHCCORETOSTG:857 "--   * Dropping type lambdas". STG alternatives are GHCSTGSYN:476 "Very like in Core syntax (except no type-world stuff).". What STG carries instead is a kind: GHCSTGLINT:75 "STG is still a statically typed language, but the type system" GHCSTGLINT:76 "is much coarser. In particular, STG programs must be /well-kinded/." with the distinction drawn explicitly, GHCSTGLINT:80 "So the STG type system does not distinguish between Int and Bool," GHCSTGLINT:81 "but it /does/ distinguish between Int and Int#, because they have" different kinds. Below STG, Cmm carries a category and a width, GHCCMMTYPE:57 "data CmmType    -- The important one!" GHCCMMTYPE:58 "  = CmmType CmmCat !Width" with GHCCMMTYPE:63 "   | BitsCat               -- ^ Integer (including non-GC pointer addresses)". *CompCert.* C types are consumed at the Clight to Csharpminor pass, COMPCERTCSHM:16 "- Resolution of all type-dependent behaviours: overloaded operators" are resolved and address computations made explicit. RTL then carries a machine type algebra, COMPCERTRTL:36 "is very simple, consisting of the four types [Tint] (for integers" COMPCERTRTL:37 "and pointers), [Tfloat] (for double-precision floats), [Tlong]" and Tsingle. It is inferred and then proved sound, COMPCERTRTL:579 "Theorem type_function_correct:". *LLVM.* The IR is typed and its types were never the source's, LLVMOPAQUE:47 "Historically LLVM was some sort of type-safe subset of C." and the pointee type was removed on the ground that it carried nothing, LLVMOPAQUE:44 "the pointee type carries no real" semantics, LLVMOPAQUE:56 "that the pointee type system design does not effectively support" optimization. Source-level type information moved out of the type system into metadata, LLVMOPAQUE:61 "have evolved into [TBAA]" because of limitations with LLVMOPAQUE:62 "representing higher-level language information directly via SSA values.". *MLton.* MLTONMONO:8 "Monomorphisation eliminates polymorphic values and datatype" declarations by duplicating them per use type. What follows stays typed for two more IRs: MLTONSSADOC:9 "<<SSA#>> is a <<FirstOrder#>>, <<SimplyTyped#>> <<IntermediateLanguage#>>." whose checker verifies MLTONSSADOC:36 ""traditional" well-typedness", then MLTONRSSA:23 "The big change to the type system is that type checking is now" MLTONRSSA:24 "based on subtyping, not type equality." with singleton types for constants. Untypedness arrives only at the last IR, MLTONMACHINE:8 "<<Machine#>> is an <<Untyped#>> <<IntermediateLanguage#>>, corresponding" to an abstract machine, whose checker verifies MLTONMACHINE:21 "some liveness properties." *OCaml.* OCAMLLAMBDA:16 "(* The "lambda" intermediate code *)" is where types stop. What binders and function results carry is a four-way representation class, OCAMLLAMBDA:190 "Pgenval | Pfloatval | Pboxedintval of boxed_integer | Pintval", where Pgenval is the uniform boxed-or-immediate case. *JVM and Java.* Erasure is a total function on types, JLS4:2224 "is a mapping from types (possibly including parameterized types and" type variables to types that are never either, and it is non-injective by its own definition, JLS4:2249 "erasure of a type variable" is JLS4:2250 "erasure of its leftmost bound.". The erased descriptors are what the target checks, JVMS4:12135 "Verification by type checking must be used to verify <code class="literal">class</code>" files at version 50.0 and above. The unerased source type rides along in an attribute the machine does not read: JVMS4:6353 "A <code class="literal">Signature</code> attribute stores a signature" for a declaration that JVMS4:6356 "uses type variables or parameterized types.", it is one of the JVMS4:3980 "Ten attributes are not critical to correct interpretation of" the class file by the JVM, and JVMS4:6413 "well-formedness of <code class="literal">Signature</code> attributes during class loading or" linking is not checked by Oracle's implementation. *WebAssembly.* The target check is a precondition of running at all, WASMVALCONV:7 "Validation checks that a WebAssembly module is well-formed." WASMVALCONV:8 "Only valid modules can be :ref:`instantiated <exec-instantiation>`.". The GC proposal adds structured types with a top, WASMGCMVP:40 "  - the common supertype (a.k.a. top) of all internal types", and states the producer's price for anything the type system cannot express, WASMGCMVP:8 "will require a substantial number of runtime casts" that later extensions may remove, spent through WASMGCMVP:666 "`ref.cast <reftype>` tries to convert a reference to a given type". **The coarse equality relation is shipped, and GHC ships it twice.** At STG, GHCSTGLINT:49 "Before unarisation, we must check that each r_i is compatible with s_i." where GHCSTGLINT:50 "Compatibility is weaker than on-the-nose equality: for example," GHCSTGLINT:51 "IntRep and WordRep are compatible.". At Cmm, GHCCMMTYPE:94 "A weaker notion of equality of 'CmmType's than 'cmmEqType'," is defined for the lint alone, and its word case is GHCCMMTYPE:114 "_word1      `weak_eq` _word2      = True  -- Ignores GcPtr", justified by GHCCMMTYPE:100 "are not yet well-typed wrt pointers,". CompCert's Tint covers integers and pointers together by construction, COMPCERTRTL:36-37 above. That is `tal-ty=?`'s relation at `lib/lowering/tal/check.chiral:66-77` in three other compilers. **What the coarsening gives up, stated by the compilers themselves.** GHC names both sides in one sentence: GHCSTGLINT:85 "This coarser type system makes fewer distinctions, and that allows" GHCSTGLINT:86 "many nonsensical programs (such as ('x' && "foo")) -- but all type" systems accept buggy programs, and GHCSTGLINT:88 "some optimisations that are ill-typed in Core." become available. The type check does not survive the boundary at all: GHCSTGLINT:15 "Because we don't have types and coercions in STG we can't really check types" here, GHCSTGLINT:31 "It's finally decided that we remove all type checking and only look for" the four basic properties at GHCSTGLINT:4 "A lint pass to check basic STG invariants:". LLVM gave up the frontend's confusion check, LLVMOPAQUE:64 "Pointee types provide some value to frontends because the IR verifier uses types" LLVMOPAQUE:65 "to detect straightforward type confusion bugs." and judged the cost higher than the benefit. Java gave up run-time availability of the source type, JLS4:2287 "Because some type information" is erased, not all types are available at run time. **CompCert gives up nothing at the type level because it never claims anything there.** COMPCERTDRV:13 "The whole compiler and its proof of semantic preservation" states the property, and the property is a simulation: COMPCERTDRV:446 "Theorem transf_c_program_correct:" concludes that COMPCERTDRV:443 "exists a backward simulation of the dynamic semantics of [p]" by the target's. The RTL type system is a means to register allocation and to the simulation proofs, and no lemma of the form `⊢ e : τ` implies `⊢ [[e]] : [[τ]]` appears in the pinned driver. This is the sharpest contrast with FD-15's five lemmas: end-to-end guarantees are obtainable without carrying source types down, at the price of proving every pass instead of checking every artifact. **Totality, which is the half with no occupant.** CompCert's theorem is conditioned on the whole pipeline succeeding, COMPCERTDRV:446 above with `transf_c_program p = OK tp` as its antecedent; a pass that refuses produces no Asm program, so there is no partly-lowered artifact for a checker to read. GHC's `CoreToStg` has no skip outcome in the pinned module; the one skip in the GHC survey is in the checker and it is named as a defect workaround, GHCSTGLINT:67 "we simply skip the Lint check when we detect such" representation-polymorphic situations. MLton's monomorphiser duplicates every polymorphic declaration at every use type, MLTONMONO:8 above, so the input is exhausted. The nearest production analogue of a partial lowering is a JIT bailout: HOTSPOTC2:197 "// Note that serious, irreversible failures are already logged" and HOTSPOTC2:199 "env->record_failure(C.failure_reason());" leave a method uncompiled. The disposition is the opposite of the tree's. A method C2 refuses keeps running from the class file, and the class file is the artifact the target checker already verified at link time, JVMS4:12135 above. In the tree the refused definition is dropped from the artifact the target checker reads, and `lib/lowering/compile-back.chiral:268-269` records what that costs, "a reachable skipped def surfaces as a" "missing label at emit". **Where the tree sits on the spectrum, by coordinate.** `ttype` at `lib/lowering/upper/lower.chiral:35` is `(-> UT (Maybe TalTy))`, and its erased case at `:49` sends every erased type variable to `(tt-word)`. That is Java's `|T|` at JLS4:2249-2250 and GHC's LiftedRep collapse at GHCSTGLINT:80-81, and on this axis the tree is where production is. `LCore` at `:116-123` is type-erased, which is OCaml's Lambda at OCAMLLAMBDA:16 and GHC's STG at GHCSTGSYN:476, except that Lambda and STG both keep a representation annotation on every binder and `LCore` keeps none. `TalTy` at `lib/lowering/tal/ssa.chiral:21-22` is five constructors, against CompCert RTL's four at COMPCERTRTL:36-37 and Cmm's category-plus-width at GHCCMMTYPE:57-58; the difference is that `TalTy` keeps nominal data types, `(tt-data dn targs)`, beside the uniform word. `tal-ty=?` at `lib/lowering/tal/check.chiral:66-77` then makes `tt-word` match those nominal types, and that is where the tree leaves the surveyed practice. **Measured on the pinned code and on `check.chiral:68-77`: Cmm's `weak_eq` is an equivalence and `tal-ty=?` is not.** GHCCMMTYPE:114's rule partitions `CmmCat` into three classes, float, vector and everything else, and `cmmCompatType` also demands equal width, so compatibility is reflexive, symmetric and transitive. `tal-ty=?` gives `(tt-i64)` against `(tt-word)` true, `(tt-word)` against `(tt-str)` true, and `(tt-i64)` against `(tt-str)` false. A checker relation that is not transitive accepts or refuses depending on which side an erased value passes through, and no surveyed system ships one. **What a checked target with a coarser type language costs its producer, where anyone charges for it.** The JVM and WebAssembly both keep the collapse and both make the producer pay at the use site: WASMGCMVP:8 names the runtime casts as the expected cost of the MVP type system, spent at WASMGCMVP:666, and the JVM's descriptor-level check at JVMS4:12135 is what forces the compiler to emit a cast where the erased type is wider than the source type. Neither of them makes the target checker's equality relation admit the collapse silently. **Not settled, and searched for.** No pinned source describes a production compiler that keeps a checked target type system and a type translation that is partial on its input. That combination was the specific thing this slice went looking for and the survey did not find it; the finding is a negative result and the search covered GHC, CompCert, LLVM, MLton, OCaml, WebAssembly, the JVM and HotSpot's C2. No primary source was pinned stating that OCaml's Lambda is unchecked. That claim rests on OCAMLLAMBDA:190's grammar, which carries representation classes and no types, and on the `ocaml/lambda` directory listing fetched from the GitHub contents API on 2026-09-08, which holds translation, pattern-match, simplification and printing modules and no checker; the listing is not pinned. `FLAMBDA`, pinned by FD-14, is the manual's optimisation chapter and states nothing about types or typing, so the claim that Flambda works below the type system is unpinned here. GHC's users guide gives `-dstg-lint` as GHCDEBUGFLAGS:1112 "    Ditto for STG level." and carries no statement of relative strength; the asymmetry with Core Lint is stated only in the pass source, GHCSTGLINT:15 and :31 against GHCCORELINT:115. JVMS chapter 4 never uses the word "erasure", measured by grep over the pin returning zero hits, which is why the erasure claim is cited to JLS4 and not to JVMS4. `primRepCompatible`, the function GHCSTGLINT:51 relies on, lives in `GHC.Core.TyCon` and was not fetched, so the transitivity comparison is drawn against Cmm's `weak_eq`, which is pinned in full. The WebAssembly core specification's soundness theorem was not pinned, only the validation chapter's conventions. Rust MIR, Swift SIL and Go's SSA were not surveyed. Whether the tree's position is a defect or a costed choice is the author's call and this row does not rule on it. **Reachability.** One URL was tried and returned HTTP 404 from this sandbox: `raw.githubusercontent.com/llvm/llvm-project/main/llvm/docs/OpaquePointers.rst`; the file is `OpaquePointers.md` and was pinned under that name. `mlton/backend/rssa.sig` fetched at HTTP 200 and is a ten-line signature re-export carrying no type language, so the RSSA statement is taken from the guide page instead. All sixteen pins added by this run are `raw`, the bytes as served.
- evidence: `lib/lowering/upper/lower.chiral:32`, `:35`, `:49-50`, `:116-123`; `lib/lowering/tal/ssa.chiral:21-22`; `lib/lowering/tal/check.chiral:66-77`; `lib/lowering/compile-back.chiral:252-272`; `.planning/TAL-CONFORMANCE-QUEUE.md` slice R2; `records/findings.md` FD-15 for the published theorem this row prices. Sixteen pins added 2026-09-08, all `raw`, all in `.planning/sources/`: `GHCCORE` (GHC `compiler/GHC/Core.hs`), `GHCCORELINT` (`compiler/GHC/Core/Lint.hs`), `GHCCORETOSTG` (`compiler/GHC/CoreToStg.hs`), `GHCSTGSYN` (`compiler/GHC/Stg/Syntax.hs`), `GHCSTGLINT` (`compiler/GHC/Stg/Lint.hs`), `GHCCMMTYPE` (`compiler/GHC/Cmm/Type.hs`), `GHCDEBUGFLAGS` (`docs/users_guide/debugging.rst`), all from `raw.githubusercontent.com/ghc/ghc/master`; `COMPCERTDRV` (`driver/Compiler.v`) and `COMPCERTCSHM` (`cfrontend/Cshmgen.v`) from `raw.githubusercontent.com/AbsInt/CompCert/master`; `LLVMOPAQUE` (`llvm/docs/OpaquePointers.md`, `raw.githubusercontent.com/llvm/llvm-project/main`); `MLTONMONO`, `MLTONSSADOC`, `MLTONRSSA` and `MLTONMACHINE` (`doc/guide/src/{Monomorphise,SSA,RSSA,Machine}.adoc`, `raw.githubusercontent.com/MLton/mlton/master`); `OCAMLLAMBDA` (`lambda/lambda.mli`, `raw.githubusercontent.com/ocaml/ocaml/trunk`); `WASMGCMVP` (`proposals/gc/MVP.md`, `raw.githubusercontent.com/WebAssembly/gc/main`); `WASMVALCONV` (`document/core/valid/conventions.rst`, `raw.githubusercontent.com/WebAssembly/spec/main`); `HOTSPOTC2` (`src/hotspot/share/opto/c2compiler.cpp`, `raw.githubusercontent.com/openjdk/jdk/master`); `JLS4` (Java Language Specification SE 21 chapter 4, `docs.oracle.com`). `COMPCERTRTL` (`backend/RTLtyping.v`), `JVMS4` (JVM Specification SE 21 chapter 4) and `FLAMBDA` were already pinned by FD-14 and are quoted here. ### FD-17 five published mechanisms discharge a coarse target type, and every one of them charges at a site written into the artifact

- state:    OPEN
- claim:    The question, for `.planning/TAL-CONFORMANCE-QUEUE.md` slice R3: when a target type system is coarser than the source, what published mechanisms discharge the resulting soundness obligation, and what does each cost the producer, the checker and the runtime? **Five were surveyed at their own sources: the checked downcast, duplication and specialization, type passing with intensional type analysis, term-level type representations, and a coercion calculus. Each one charges, and the charge always lands on a site the producer writes into the artifact.** The downcast charges the runtime a test and a trap. Specialization charges code size and gives up separate compilation. Type passing charges every polymorphic call a constructed type argument and charges the target type system a second copy of its computational machinery. Term-level representations move that charge into ordinary terms and make it optional per call site. A coercion calculus charges nothing at runtime, and charges the producer a proof term the checker verifies. **No surveyed mechanism discharges the obligation inside the checker's equality relation**, which is where `tal-ty=?` at `lib/lowering/tal/check.chiral:68-77` discharges it. The consequence for this tree is countable. Four of the five need an instruction naming the type it moves to, and `lib/lowering/tal/ssa.chiral:31-34` holds four instruction forms that carry no `ty` at all, three of them the heap forms where the JVM and WebAssembly put their cast.
- measured: **1. The checked downcast, and the division of labour it rests on.** The JVM verifier's static rule for `checkcast` is a retype of the stack top and nothing else. It is one clause, JVMS4:14637 "instructionIsTypeSafe(checkcast(CP), Environment, _Offset, StackFrame," whose body is JVMS4:14641 "    validTypeTransition(Environment, [class('java/lang/Object', BL)], CP," and whose prose is JVMS4:14631 "A <span class="emphasis"><em>checkcast</em></span> instruction with operand <code class="literal">CP</code> is type safe iff <code class="literal">CP</code>" refers to a class or array. The verifier proves that the operand is a reference and that the successor state is consistent. It proves nothing about whether the cast holds. That is deferred whole to one opcode, JVMS6:2838 "<span class="emphasis"><em>checkcast</em></span> = 192 (0xc0)", whose runtime behaviour is JVMS6:2876 "the resolved class, array, or interface type, the operand stack is" JVMS6:2877 "unchanged; otherwise, the <span class="emphasis"><em>checkcast</em></span> instruction throws a" `ClassCastException`. WebAssembly GC splits the same obligation across two instructions, WASMGCMVP:661 "* `ref.test <reftype>` tests whether a reference has a given type" and WASMGCMVP:666 "* `ref.cast <reftype>` tries to convert a reference to a given type", and pays it the same way, WASMGCMVP:669 "  - traps if reference is not of requested type". **The runtime cost is stated in machine operations.** WASMGCMVP:472 "This can compile to machine code that (1) reads the RTT from `$x`, (2) checks that the length of its supertype table is >= 3, and (3) pointer-compares table[2] against `$rttC`." Three operations per cast, plus a runtime type descriptor reachable from every castable value. The producer's price is the one FD-16 already measured at WASMGCMVP:8, a substantial number of runtime casts. **2. Duplication and specialization.** Harper and Morrisett state both halves. It works: INTENSIONAL:129 "the case analysis can be eliminated by duplicating and spe-" cializing the definition at the appropriate type, and the target keeps precise types with no coarse case left in it. It costs code, INTENSIONAL:159 "to excessive code-blowup.", and it stops at a compilation-unit boundary, INTENSIONAL:162 "rately compiling a denition from its uses because it is im-" possible to determine the types at which the definition will be used. Their conclusion is that it is an optimization and not a discharge, INTENSIONAL:170 "run-time type analysis to still be necessary for practical lan-" guage implementation. MLton is the shipping instance, measured in FD-16 at MLTONMONO:8. **3. Type passing with intensional type analysis.** INTENSIONAL:105 "1.2TypePassing" is the mechanism: a type is an argument, and a primitive analyses it, INTENSIONAL:114 "sub=:typecaseof" with one arm per representation class. The target type system grows two constructs, a term-level INTENSIONAL:212 "a recursivetypecaseconstruct calledtyperecin the following" and its constructor-level twin at INTENSIONAL:191 "usingTypecaseas follows:", so the discharge is to make the coarse type analysable at its point of use. The costs are measured in TIL, the compiler built on it. Against SML/NJ, TILPLDI:16 "faster, do one-fth the total heap allocation, and use one-" half the memory, at TILPLDI:20 "compile time TIL takes about eight times longer to compile" and at TILPLDI:67 "machine code which is slower and bigger than conventional" approaches wherever a type is unknown at compile time. **The type-passing tax on the language itself is separately stated.** TYPEPASS:71 "type informationalwaysbe constructed and passed" to polymorphic functions, which can be TYPEPASS:73 "siderable overhead if types are rarely examined at" run time; the semantics duplicates its devices, TYPEPASS:86 "suffices, but when types are passed it is necessarymost type systems abstraction may be achieved by hid-" ing to add a second heap for type data; and TYPEPASS:104 "type passing severely complicates typed" closure conversion, which is R4's subject. **4. Term-level type representations.** Crary, Weirich and Morrisett keep the analysis and drop the type passing. TYPEPASS:123 "dispatch is to be supported, then clearly on some level" types must be passed, and their answer is that the TYPEPASS:125 "approach is to construct and passtermsthat represent" types, tied to the type they represent by a singleton family, TYPEPASS:128 "in the static semantics by assigningethe special type" `R(t)`. The producer pays only where analysis happens, and the payment is visible: TYPEPASS:151 "is available at run time, so abstraction can be achieved" simply by not supplying a representation. The checker pays a new type family and a typecase rule that refines a type in place. The runtime pays ordinary terms with no second heap. **5. The coercion calculus.** System FC is the production mechanism that charges inside a typed IR. A coercion is a proof term, SYSTEMFC:752 "is evidence that a value of typescan be used in" any context expecting the other type, applied through an explicit cast, SYSTEMFC:748 "coercion to cast an expressione:sto typesusing thecast" expression. GHC's implementation says the same, GHCTYCOREP:849 "-- | A 'Coercion' is concrete evidence of the equality/convertibility" of two types, and Core carries the cast as a term form, GHCCORE:271 "  | Cast  (Expr b) CoercionR        -- The Coercion has Representational role". **The runtime cost is zero and the source says so.** SYSTEMFC:49 "nesses to justify explicit type-cast operations. Like types, coercions" SYSTEMFC:50 "are erased before running the program, so they are guaranteed to" SYSTEMFC:51 "have no run-time cost.", and GHC implements exactly that, GHCCORETOSTG:218 "treat coercions themselves as zero-width arguments". **GHC also ships the unchecked case, and names it.** GHCTYCOREP:1540 "A UnivCo is a coercion whose proof does not directly express its role" and kind, and GHCTYCOREP:1655 "It is vital that `UnivCo` (a coercion that lacks a proper proof)" tracks the coercions it depends on. An unproved equality is available in FC, it is a constructor a producer must name at a site, and its dependencies are tracked. **6. Who owes the check, stated as a theorem.** BLAME:1169 "Corollary 11.(Well-typed programs can't be blamed) Lettbe a" well-typed term with one cast. Its consequence for a coarse target, BLAME:1194 "precisely-typed term to a less-precisely-typed context must be" BLAME:1195 "blamed on the less-precisely-typed context. And sinceT <:S" implies the converse for the other direction. The obligation sits on the coarser side of the boundary in both directions, and it sits on a cast that exists. **7. What each mechanism would cost this tree, by coordinate.** `ttype` at `lib/lowering/upper/lower.chiral:35` is `(-> UT (Maybe TalTy))` and sends every erased binder to `(tt-word)` at `:49`. `TalTy` at `lib/lowering/tal/ssa.chiral:21-22` is five constructors. `tal-ty=?` at `lib/lowering/tal/check.chiral:68-77` gives `tt-word` a `true` arm against every type on either side, and `targs=?` at `:78-95` does the same for an empty argument list. *The downcast* needs a new `Instr` form carrying a source register and a target `TalTy`, a `ck-instr` arm that retypes the register the way JVMS4:14637 does, a runtime type descriptor on every value a `tt-word` can hold, and a trap. `tt-word`'s arm in `tal-ty=?` becomes a refusal, and every site the refusal exposes becomes a producer obligation. *Specialization* needs no target change at all. It needs the lowerer to duplicate a definition per instantiation, which deletes `tt-word` from `ttype`'s range, and `decision-erased-word-level.md` is then moot. INTENSIONAL:162 is the boundary it cannot cross, and `docs/decisions/decision-scope.md` self-hosting is one program. *Type passing* needs `LCore` to stop being type-erased at `lib/lowering/upper/lower.chiral:116-123`, since a type that is not carried cannot be passed, plus a `typecase` at the TAL term level and a `Typerec` at the `TalTy` level. It is the largest of the five by every measure the sources give. *Term-level representations* need one new `TalTy` constructor, a representation family indexed by a `TalTy`, and a `tal-ty=?` arm relating representations by index. `tt-word` survives, and it stops justifying itself, because analysing a word requires its representation to be in scope. *A coercion* needs a proof-term category in `Instr` and an axiom set. `tt-word` against `tt-str` has no derivation from reflexivity, symmetry and transitivity, so it needs an axiom, and an axiom admitting every one-word type is the collapse `docs/decisions/decision-erased-word-level.md:44` names. GHC's answer to that same need is `UnivCo`, at a site, with dependencies tracked. **8. The instruction-level consequence, measured today.** `lib/lowering/tal/ssa.chiral:24-25` states "one typed SSA instruction. Every value-defining form carries its result type annotation (`ty`), so the checker re-verifies independently of the kernel." Four of the eight forms at `:27-34` carry a `ty` field: `i-const`, `i-prim`, `i-call`, `i-con`. Four carry none: `i-bnew`, `i-bget`, `i-bput`, `i-blen`. FD-15 named three, and `i-blen` is the fourth; its result is a length and it is not a heap read of a value, which is why the three matter and the fourth is a count correction. Their rules at `lib/lowering/tal/check.chiral:215-218` supply the missing types from the checker's own constants: `i-bnew` yields `(tt-bytes)`, `i-bget` yields `(tt-i64)`, and `i-bput` demands `(tt-i64)` for the stored value. **That is the exact position where both cast systems put their check.** A `tt-word` flows into `i-bput`'s value slot through the `tt-word` arm of `tal-ty=?`, and it comes back out of `i-bget` as `(tt-i64)` by fiat, with nothing in the artifact recording what was stored. The JVM puts `checkcast` after the field read, WebAssembly puts `ref.cast` after the struct read, and the tree has no annotation on either instruction for such a rule to read. Any of the five mechanisms that names a target type requires these three forms to gain a `ty` field first. **The non-transitivity PRB-74 measured has an instruction-level face here.** A value stored through `i-bput` and read through `i-bget` arrives as `(tt-i64)`, and `(tt-i64)` against `(tt-str)` is `false` at `check.chiral:71`. The heap round trip narrows a word to an integer, so the collapse is silent on the way in and a refusal on the way out. **Not settled, and searched for.** No pinned source describes a system that discharges a coarse-to-precise flow inside the checker's type-equality relation with no site in the artifact. That is the same negative result FD-16 reached from the totality side, reached again from the discharge side, and the search here covered the JVM, WebAssembly GC, System FC, the intensional-type-analysis line and the blame calculus. No pinned source gives a measured cycle count or benchmark for a JVM `checkcast`; WASMGCMVP:472 gives the operation count and no timing. The WebAssembly core specification's validation rules for `ref.cast` were fetched from `raw.githubusercontent.com/WebAssembly/spec/main/document/core/valid/instructions.rst` at HTTP 200 and were not pinned, because the file states the rule as SpecTec macros, `$${rule: Instr_ok/ref.cast}`, and carries no readable typing judgment; the Wasm side therefore rests on the GC proposal, already pinned by FD-16. Leroy's coercion papers were not fetched, so the coercion-implementation account rests on Harper and Morrisett's description of it at INTENSIONAL:78 "1.1CoercionImplementations" and INTENSIONAL:101 "copying coercions do not work for mutable data structuresthere is no way to eliminate the polymorphism when sepa-", which is a secondary reading of a primary source. The JFP 2002 version of the erasure paper was fetched and the ICFP 1998 version was pinned. Gradual typing was held to the blame question as instructed, so no gradual-typing implementation cost was surveyed. Nothing here rules on which mechanism this tree should adopt; that is an author call and this row is the survey it rests on. **Reachability, and the transcription.** Four guessed URLs for the erasure paper returned HTTP 404 from this sandbox (`cs.cmu.edu/~crary/papers/1998/erasure/erasure.pdf`, `cs.cornell.edu/talc/papers/erasure.pdf`, `cs.cmu.edu/~rwh/papers/erasure/erasure.pdf`, `cis.upenn.edu/~sweirich/papers/erasure.pdf`) and `ecommons.cornell.edu` returned HTTP 429; a search located `cs.cornell.edu/talc/papers/typepass.pdf`, which returned HTTP 200. Five of the seven pins this run added are `transcribed`, extracted by a stdlib-only PDF reader because this sandbox has no `pdftotext` and no PDF library. The transcription drops `fi` and `ffi` ligatures, so "definition" reads "denition" and "efficient" reads "ecient"; it loses inter-word spacing inside runs; and on two-column pages a line occasionally carries text from both columns. Every span quoted above was checked to occur exactly once in its pinned file. `JVMS6` and `GHCTYCOREP` are `raw`, the bytes as served.
- evidence: `lib/lowering/upper/lower.chiral:35`, `:49`, `:116-123`; `lib/lowering/tal/ssa.chiral:21-22`, `:24-25`, `:27-34`; `lib/lowering/tal/check.chiral:68-77`, `:78-95`, `:215-218`; `docs/decisions/decision-erased-word-level.md:44`; `.planning/TAL-CONFORMANCE-QUEUE.md` slice R3; `records/findings.md` FD-15 and FD-16; `records/lenses/problems.md` PRB-74. Seven pins added 2026-09-08 in `.planning/sources/`: `INTENSIONAL` (Harper and Morrisett, "Compiling Polymorphism Using Intensional Type Analysis", POPL 1995, `cs.cmu.edu/~rwh/papers/intensional/popl95.pdf`, transcribed); `TYPEPASS` (Crary, Weirich and Morrisett, "Intensional Polymorphism in Type-Erasure Semantics", ICFP 1998, `cs.cornell.edu/talc/papers/typepass.pdf`, transcribed); `TILPLDI` (Tarditi et al, "TIL: A Type-Directed Optimizing Compiler for ML", PLDI 1996, `cs.cmu.edu/~rwh/papers/til/pldi96.pdf`, transcribed); `SYSTEMFC` (Sulzmann, Chakravarty, Peyton Jones and Donnelly, "System F with Type Equality Coercions", TLDI 2007 with the post-publication appendix, `microsoft.com/en-us/research/wp-content/uploads/2007/01/tldi22-sulzmann-with-appendix.pdf`, transcribed); `BLAME` (Wadler and Findler, "Well-typed programs can't be blamed", `homepages.inf.ed.ac.uk/wadler/papers/blame/blame.pdf`, transcribed); `JVMS6` (JVM Specification SE 21 chapter 6, `docs.oracle.com`, raw); `GHCTYCOREP` (GHC `compiler/GHC/Core/TyCo/Rep.hs`, `raw.githubusercontent.com/ghc/ghc/master`, raw). `JVMS4`, `WASMGCMVP`, `GHCCORE` and `GHCCORETOSTG` were pinned by FD-14 and FD-16 and are quoted here. The dates are the UTC stamps `xlat pin` writes.
