### FD-18 defunctionalization's obligation is the whole-program enumeration and one clause per abstraction, and the tree took the published route whose split it then widened

- state:    OPEN
- claim:    The question, for `.planning/TAL-CONFORMANCE-QUEUE.md` slice R4: what is the type-preservation obligation of defunctionalization as a closure-conversion strategy, how does it differ from the existential-packing obligation, and what does each require of the target type system? **The obligation is an enumeration and a clause set over it. Every lambda-abstraction in the whole program gets a tag, the tags become the constructors of a closed sum whose fields are that abstraction's free variables at their own source types, and one dispatcher carries one clause per tag whose right-hand side is that abstraction's own body. Type preservation holds when every clause typechecks at the dispatcher's declared domain and codomain, and the published literature offers exactly two ways to make that hold.** Route one splits the sum by source arrow type and gives each split a monomorphic dispatcher. It needs nothing of the target beyond ordinary algebraic data types, and it works only where the arrow types are ground. Route two keeps one sum `Arrow a1 a2` indexed by domain and codomain with a single polymorphic dispatcher. It works under System F polymorphism and it needs guarded algebraic data types: constructors carrying a specified result index, and case branches typechecked under the type equation that index yields. **The difference from existential packing is where the heterogeneity goes.** Typed closure conversion hides the environment type behind an existential so two closures at one arrow type with differently shaped environments still receive one target type. Defunctionalization hides nothing and makes each environment shape a constructor of a finite sum, which Danvy and Nielsen state is the same existential written out concretely because the program is whole. So the existential route charges the target a quantifier and keeps the transformation modular, and the defunctionalization route charges the target only a closed sum and charges the compiler whole-program knowledge. **The tree is on route one and its target already holds every type former route one's theorem needs.** What it widened is the split criterion. The published criterion is equality of the source arrow type, one distinct data type per distinct arrow type; `arrow-key-eq` at `lib/lowering/upper/closconv.chiral:382` keeps that criterion on the codomain through `cod-key-eq` and drops it on the domain through `shape-eq` at `:343`, which erases every non-arrow domain to one word. That is why the dispatcher's domains had to be restated as the erased word and why the capture constructor's fields did not. **The tree needs no guarded algebraic data types, because it did not take the route that wants them.**
- measured: **The enumeration, at the origin.** Reynolds builds the sum by listing lambda expressions and their free variables: REYNOLDS:649 "suggests that the new set FUNVAL should be a union of disjoint sets of records, one set" REYNOLDS:650 "for each lambda expression whose value belonged to the old FUNVAL, and that the fields" REYNOLDS:651 "of each record should contain values of the global variables of the corresponding lambda" expression, and one dispatcher over the union, REYNOLDS:667 "and the overall structure ofapplywill be:" with one branch per record predicate. The presentation is set-theoretic and untyped, and the shape is already `$clo`, `$k<i>_<j>` and `$apply`. **The modern statement, and the typing named directly.** DEFUNCWORK:120 "order data types: a first-class function is introduced with a constructor" DEFUNCWORK:121 "holding the values of the free variables of a function abstraction, and it" DEFUNCWORK:122 "is eliminated with a case expression dispatching over the corresponding" constructors. The whole-program precondition is part of the definition: DEFUNCWORK:226 "abstractions. All these function abstractions can be enumerated in a whole" DEFUNCWORK:227 "program. Defunctionalization is thusa whole-program transformation where" DEFUNCWORK:228 "function types are replaced by an enumeration of the function abstractions in" this program. **The relation to the existential is stated, and it is identity rather than rivalry.** DEFUNCWORK:231 "tion type hides typing assumptions from the context, and, as pointed out by" DEFUNCWORK:232 "making these assumptions explicit requires an existential type. For a whole pro-" DEFUNCWORK:233 "gram, this existential type can be represented with a finite sum together with the" DEFUNCWORK:234 "corresponding injections and case dispatch, and this representation is precisely" what defunctionalization achieves. The same source records that the dispatcher may be split: DEFUNCWORK:237 "apply functions, e.g., grouped by types, as in Bell, Bellegarde, and Hook's work". Constructor fields are concrete at the free variable's own type: DEFUNCWORK:150 "The first function abstraction contains one free variable (x, of type integer), and" DEFUNCWORK:151 "therefore the first data-type constructor requires an integer." **The existential obligation, for contrast.** Typed closure conversion fails without the existential for a reason that has no analogue here: TYPEDCLOS:131 "the type of the environment explicitly.That violates the" privacy of the environment representation, so TYPEDCLOS:134 "not in general result in a well-typed target program. For" example two arms of one conditional at one source arrow type get two target types, and the repair is TYPEDCLOS:156 "representation of the environmentmay be hidden using ex-" istential types. Pottier and Gauthier restate it the same way: POLYDEFUNCX:52 "type of a closure is a pair of a first-order function type (for the code" pointer and a record type, POLYDEFUNCX:54 "together within anexistentialquantifier, so that closures whose value" POLYDEFUNCX:55 "environments have different structure may still receive identical types." **Route one, its theorem and its stated limit.** POLYDEFUNCX:150 "introduces a family of monomorphic functions, of typet->t->" one per ground arrow type, and POLYDEFUNCX:153 "is that the definition ofapplycan now list only the tags whose" associated abstractions have that type, so every clause is type compatible. Indeed, every distinct arrow typein the source" POLYDEFUNCX:173 "program maps to a distinct algebraic data type in the target program." and the limit, POLYDEFUNCX:174 "As a result, there is no natural way of translating non-ground arrow" POLYDEFUNCX:175 "types. These remarks explain why this approach fails in the presence" of polymorphism. Huang and Yallop record route one as the standard practice and name its users: DEPDEFUNC:1150 "monomorphicapplyfunctions to make simply-typed defunctionalization type-preserving is a" well-known technique, citing Bell et al. 1997 among others. **Why route one cannot be stretched, and what route two costs.** Under one polymorphic dispatcher with no extra hypotheses, POLYDEFUNCX:128 "abstraction in the source program to have typeforallaa.a->a, which" POLYDEFUNCX:146 "cannot hold in general! This explains why it does not seem possible to" POLYDEFUNCX:147 "define a type-preserving notion of defunctionalization forSystemF." Route two buys it back by indexing the sum: POLYDEFUNCX:178 "We keep a singleapplyfunction, whose type isforallaa.a->a->" and POLYDEFUNCX:182 "must beArrowt t, for some distinguished, binary algebraic data" type constructor. Two ingredients are required and both are outside ordinary algebraic data types: POLYDEFUNCX:216 "with the algebraic data typeArrow, to be assigned the specific types" `Arrow int int` and `Arrow bool bool`, because POLYDEFUNCX:218 "an ordinary algebraic data type, then the nullary data constructorssucc" POLYDEFUNCX:219 "andnotwould necessarily have typeforallaa.Arrowaa. Second, when" performing case analysis, POLYDEFUNCX:221 "branch associated withsucc(resp.not) to be typechecked under the" POLYDEFUNCX:222 "extra assumptionArrowaa=Arrow int int(resp.Arrowaa=" `Arrow bool bool`. That mechanism is POLYDEFUNCX:229 "guarded algebraic data types. The termguardedstems from Xi, Chen," and the abstract states the whole result, POLYDEFUNC:18 "such as ML or System F, defunctionalization is not type-" preserving, and POLYDEFUNC:20 "F withguarded algebraic data typesallows recovering type" preservation. The theorem it yields is one line, POLYDEFUNCX:982 "Theorem 4.3true,emptyset|-p:t." over two lemmas, one for the term translation and one for the dispatcher. DEPDEFUNC:95 "generalized algebraic data type(GADT), in which the return type of each constructor can have a" specific index says the same of the sum. **The clause obligation, spelled.** POLYDEFUNCX:112 "clause is the body of the associatedlam-abstraction, renamed so that its" formal parameter is `arg`. Every clause's right-hand side is the abstraction's own body, and nothing else is a clause of the translation. **Separate compilation, and it is the sharpest published statement.** The transformation is whole-program by construction: POLYDEFUNC:28 "that the entire source program is available, a distinct tag is" associated with every abstraction, and POLYDEFUNCX:45 "is a whole program transformation, because definingapplyrequires" POLYDEFUNCX:46 "knowing about all of the functions that exist in the program." The contrast with the existential route is modularity: POLYDEFUNCX:2420 "appears to be a whole program transformation. This is in contrast" POLYDEFUNCX:2421 "with closure conversion, which is a modular transformation, because its" definition is compositional, which DEPDEFUNC:1162 "alization involves a globally-dened map indexed by code identiers (such as anapplyfunction" DEPDEFUNC:1163 "or our label environment), these closure conversions instead locally transform functions into" code-and-environment pairs repeats. Pottier and Gauthier then refuse the usual reading: the whole-programness is a property of the target language and not of the transformation, and POLYDEFUNCX:2436 "become modular, it is necessary, and sufficient, to adopt a richer target" POLYDEFUNCX:2437 "language, where function definitions can be split over several program" modules, which Prolog and multi-methods already offer. **What the tree holds, measured.** `TalTy` is five constructors, `lib/lowering/tal/ssa.chiral:21-22`, and `tt-data` carries a name and a type-argument list. So `Arrow t1 t2` has a spelling and `forall a1 a2. Arrow a1 a2 -> a1 -> a2` has none, because there is no type variable, no binder and no equation anywhere in `TalTy`. Route two is therefore unreachable in this target and route one is fully reachable: `tt-data`, `i-con` and `tt-case` are the closed sum, its injections and its dispatch, which DEFUNCWORK:233-234 names as the whole of what the transformation needs. The existential route is unreachable by a wider margin: it needs a quantifier `TalTy` does not have and an opening form none of `Instr`'s eight constructors provides (`lib/lowering/tal/ssa.chiral:27-34`). **The tree is on route one in its monomorphic form.** The sum is nullary, `apply-ptys` builds `(nt-data (clo-name i) nil)` at `lib/lowering/upper/closconv-driver.chiral:131-133`, and `apply-ty` at `lib/lowering/upper/closconv.chiral:1183-1185` puts `(c-tcon dname nil)` in the leading position. The dispatcher is a monomorphic global per family, `$apply<i>` at `:1200`, its body one `case` over the closure cell with one arm per site, `:1211-1213`. Capture constructors are `$k<i>_<j>` at `:1201` with fields at the capture's own source type, `site-fields->term` at `closconv-driver.chiral:156-166`. Every one of those five facts is route one's shape. **Where the split departs from the published criterion.** POLYDEFUNCX:172-173 requires one data type per distinct source arrow type. `arrow-key-eq` (`closconv.chiral:382`) is the conjunction of `shapes-eq` on the peeled domains, `cod-key-eq` on the codomain and `ieq-list` on the effect flags. `cod-key-eq` (`:365-376`) keeps a ground codomain exact through `core-eq`, which is the published criterion. `shape-eq` (`:343-350`) answers `true` for any two non-arrow types, which is not. Read off the two definitions, `(-> I64 I64)` and `(-> Str I64)` share one family and one dispatcher, where the published criterion gives them two data types and two dispatchers. The tree's own comment at `:337-340` states the wider claim that `(-> A B)`, `(-> I64 I64)` and `(-> Str Bool)` "all share one family/key"; `cod-key-eq` splits `Bool` from `I64`, so that comment describes `shape-eq` and overstates `arrow-key-eq`. **The consequence, and it is the E185 ruling read backwards.** Route one's clause obligation is that every abstraction in the family produce the family's codomain from the family's domain. A family that merges two domains has no one domain to state, which is exactly why `apply-ptys` states `nt-word` at every domain (`closconv-driver.chiral:118-133`) and why the fields stayed concrete. FD-17 measured that no published mechanism discharges a coarse target type inside the checker's equality relation, and the erased word at the dispatcher domain is that same discharge site. `closconv.chiral:360-364` records the codomain half of the criterion being kept deliberately for the reason route one gives, so the tree already applies the published criterion on one half of the arrow. **One fixture, compiled and run.** `/tmp/fam.chiral`, five defs, two higher-order call sites at `(-> I64 I64)` and `(-> Str I64)`, compiled to a 33,144-byte ELF and exited 3, the correct sum. A merged-domain family emits correct code, which is what PRB-45 already records: every value is one word at runtime and what is wrong is the type the IR carries. **Which of the three rows the published obligation speaks to.** PRB-49 hardest: DEFUNCWORK:226-228 makes the enumeration of every abstraction the transformation's precondition, and a bare `(lam ...)` in constructor position that is never registered as a site is an abstraction with a tag nobody minted and a clause nobody wrote. Route one's theorem has no antecedent over a program the pass did not enumerate. PRB-45 directly, and it agrees with the ruling already made: Reynolds at REYNOLDS:651, Danvy and Nielsen at DEFUNCWORK:150-151, and Pottier and Gauthier's constructor scheme all put the free variables in the constructor at their own types, so `concrete` is the published answer and `site-fields->term` already writes it. PRB-46 only through POLYDEFUNCX:112: the translation's clause for a tag carries that abstraction's own body, and an arm emitting the literal `0` carries something else, so that row lands on FD-15's totality rider instead of on the typing obligation. **Cheaper or dearer, for this tree.** Cheaper, and countable. Route one costs zero new `TalTy` constructors, zero new `Instr` forms and zero change to `tal-ty=?`; the target already has all three of the closed sum, the injection and the dispatch, and the residue is a split criterion in `arrow-key-eq`. The existential route costs one new `TalTy` constructor for the quantifier, a notion of type variable the checker does not have, one opening form in `Instr`, and an equality relation extended to work under a binder. Route two, which is what would be needed if the tree wanted one indexed sum and one dispatcher, costs everything the existential route costs plus branch-local type equations. The whole-program charge route one carries is already paid: `closconv-sig` runs over one whole `Sig`. **What the sources did not settle.** Bell, Bellegarde and Hook, "Type-driven defunctionalization", ICFP 1997, could not be reached: ACM DL returns HTTP 403 to a plain `curl` and the CiteSeerX document endpoint timed out at 45 seconds. Its content is attested at second hand by DEFUNCWORK:237 and DEPDEFUNC:1150, both of which name it as an instance of the family-of-dispatchers split, and the row leans on those two rather than on the paper. No surveyed source treats a split criterion coarser than source arrow-type equality and finer than one indexed sum, so the tree's representation-erased domain has no published occupant, which is consistent with FD-17's finding on the discharge site. No source was found stating what a defunctionalizing compiler owes when it declines to enumerate an abstraction it found, which is the question PRB-49 sits in.

### FD-19 the boundary declaration is a frame per block or a type per definition, and this tree took the second route and is three annotations short of it

- state:    OPEN
- claim:    The question, for `.planning/TAL-CONFORMANCE-QUEUE.md` slice R5: what must a block boundary carry for a target-level checker to be complete, and what obligation does that put on the pass that emits the code? **Two published disciplines put the boundary state into the artifact, and they bill the producer differently by two orders of magnitude.** Route one declares a frame per block: the JVM's `StackMapTable` gives the verification type of every local and every operand stack entry at the start of each basic block, TAL's register-file type is the same object, and WebAssembly's block type is the same object over a nesting tree. Route two types every value where it is defined and declares no frame: LLVM's `phi` carries one type field for the join, and Cranelift's block parameters are typed with every branch obliged to supply arguments that match. Route one moves the dataflow fixpoint from the checker into the producer and its annotation runs to about half the size of the code it annotates. Route two charges the producer only the writing down of what the emitter already computed. **The tree is on route two at every boundary it has, and its one full frame is already written.** `TFn`'s `params` is a positional register-file type over registers `0..n-1` and `seed-params` reads it back. A `tt-case` branch's `dsts` are declared through the scrutinee's `DCtor` field types in `CEnv`, which is the declaration Cranelift and WebAssembly ask for at a boundary. The JVM's frame is a wider object, and the tree has no jump target that would want one. `outline` spells the whole enclosing scope into a `TalSig` for the outlined `name$k`, so FD-15's stack-free alternative to CPS already carries a JVM-shaped frame at its boundary, written as a function signature. **The residue is three annotations and nothing else.** `i-bnew`, `i-bget` and `i-blen` bind a `dst` and carry no `ty`, which is PRB-75, and route two's closure is that every value's type is in the artifact. **The producer that would owe them is `compile-fn`, and it owes nothing today**, because no pass in the typed SSA path originates a byte-cell instruction. **The register allocator has no position, because there is no register allocator**: `nregs` is a frame-slot count and the x64 prologue turns it into one stack slot per SSA register.
- measured: **1. Route one, and the frame is the whole state at the boundary.** JVMS4:12247 "The intent is that a stack map frame must appear at" the beginning of each basic block, specifying the verification type of every operand stack entry and of JVMS4:12250 "each local variable at the start of each basic block. The type checker" reads them and generates a proof from them. Even the first frame is a declaration read off the artifact rather than a walk result, JVMS4:5336 "computed from the method descriptor by the" type checker. Three clauses make the declaration load-bearing. A branch is checked against a declared frame and the checker never computes a target's state: JVMS4:13873 "targetIsTypeSafe(Environment, StackFrame, Target) :-" over JVMS4:13874 "    offsetStackFrame(Environment, Target, Frame),". Where a frame is present the walk state is discarded in its favour: JVMS4:13804 "the type state must be assignable to the one in the" stack map frame, by JVMS4:13811 "    frameIsAssignable(frame(Locals, OperandStack, Flags), MapFrame)," and the tail then runs under `MapFrame`. **That clause is the completeness mechanism**, because a refusal inside one block cannot poison the next block's entry when the entry is read. And the checker refuses to walk code it has no declared state for: JVMS4:13850 "It is illegal to have code after an" unconditional branch without a stack map frame, implemented as JVMS4:13856 "write_ln('No stack frame after unconditional branch')," followed by `fail`. **2. The producer's bill for route one, measured twice.** Leroy states the split and its origin: LEROYBCV:2941 "Inspired by Necula and Lee's proof-carrying code [36], Rose and Rose [47] propose to split bytecode" LEROYBCV:2942 "verification into two phases: the code producer computes the stack and register types at branch" LEROYBCV:2943 "targets and transmit these so-called certificates along with the bytecode; the embedded system," then checks them LEROYBCV:2945 "rather than inferring these types itself. In other terms, the embedded system no longer solves" the dataflow equations iteratively. The gain is the fixpoint, LEROYBCV:2950 "one, since we avoid the cost of the fixpoint iteration. This speeds up verification to some extent". The price is size, and it is the only published figure found for a boundary annotation: LEROYBCV:2956 "A practical limitation of this approach is that certificates are relatively large: about 50% of the" LEROYBCV:2957 "size of the code they annotate [31]. Even if certificates are stored in persistent memory, they can" exceed the available space. **The implementation side says the producer runs the checker's old algorithm.** JDKSTACKMAP:52 " * StackMapGenerator is responsible for stack map frames generation." detects JDKSTACKMAP:59 " *      <li>Mandatory stack map frame include all jump and switch instructions targets," and all handlers, then iterates: JDKSTACKMAP:72 " *      <li>Generator loop process is repeated until all mandatory frames are cleared or until an error state is reached." That is a fixpoint, measured at JDKSTACKMAP:76 " *          <i>(Note: experimental measurements showed that more than 99% of the cases required only single pass to clear all frames," with under 1% needing a second. **Route one also creates a producer obligation that exists only because the checker must reach every instruction.** JDKSTACKMAP:79 " * <li>Dead code patching to pass class loading verification:<ul>" and JDKSTACKMAP:81 " *      <li>Each dead code block is filled with <code>NOP</code> instructions, terminated with" `ATHROW`. The producer rewrites code that cannot run so that the checker has a state to read for it. **3. Route two, and its closure.** LLVM declares the join's type on the join instruction: LLVMLANGREF:13556 "The type of the incoming values is specified with the first type field." positioned so that it is readable at the block head, LLVMLANGREF:13563 "There must be no non-phi instructions between the start of a basic block" and the PHI nodes. Cranelift moves the declaration onto the block: CRANEIR:108 "Cranelift does not have phi instructions but uses [BB parameter]s" CRANEIR:109 "instead. A BB can be defined with a list of typed parameters. Whenever control" CRANEIR:110 "is transferred to the BB, argument values for the parameters must be provided." and the function entry is the same object, CRANEIR:111 "When entering a function, the incoming function parameters are passed as" arguments to the entry BB's parameters. The closure is one sentence: every SSA value is CRANEIR:115 "BB parameters or instruction results.", both of which carry a type, so no environment is ever derived. The verifier reads the declaration back at both ends and derives nothing: CRANEVERIFY:1257 "    fn typecheck_entry_block_params(&self, errors: &mut VerifierErrors) -> VerifierStepResult {" checks the entry block's parameters against the function signature, and CRANEVERIFY:1529 "        for (arg, param) in args.zip(block_params.iter()) {" checks each branch argument against the declared parameter type. Both report non-fatally, which is FD-14's collect-all, and it is affordable for the reason FD-14 gave. The two routes are stated as equivalent in content: CRANECMP:132 "representations are equivalent, but the BB arguments are better suited to handle" blocks reached by several branches with different arguments. **4. WebAssembly is route one over a nesting tree, which is the published shape closest to this tree's `Block`.** The annotation sits on the structured instruction: WASMINSTRSYN:90 "A structured instruction can consume *input* and produce *output* on the operand stack according to its annotated :ref:`block type <syntax-blocktype>`." The single-pass algorithm reads it instead of computing it, WASMVALALG:353 "       case (block t1*->t2*)" then WASMVALALG:355 "         push_ctrl(block, [t1*], [t2*])". The surrounding state is inherited rather than restated, by WASMVALALG:164 "the height of the operand stack at the start of the block (used to check that operands do not underflow the current block)". Every branch is checked against a declared label type, WASMVALALG:375 "       case (br n)" popping `label_types`, where WASMVALALG:268 "     return (if (frame.opcode = loop) frame.start_types else frame.end_types)". The producer's obligation grew when the annotation grew: WASMMULTIVAL:117 "* block types (in `block`, `loop`, `if` instructions) are generalised from *resulttype* to *functype*", so WASMMULTIVAL:129 "* The type of `block`, `loop`, and `if` is the *functype* \[t1\*\] -> \[t2\*\] given as the block type", costing WASMMULTIVAL:148 "The binary requires a change to allow function types as block types. That requires extending the current ad-hoc encoding to allow references to function types." **5. What completeness needs in this tree, measured on the checker.** The tree's blocks nest and never join. `TalTerm` is two forms, `t-ret` and `tt-case` (`lib/lowering/tal/ssa.chiral:38`), there is no jump instruction, and a `Branch` carries a whole nested `Block` (`:39`). A block's entry environment is therefore the enclosing `REnv` at the case, extended by the branch's fields: `lib/lowering/tal/check.chiral:279`, `(ck-block ce (bind-dsts env dsts ftys) body ret)`. The enclosing half is derived by `ck-instrs` (`:225-230`), so one refusal in the enclosing instruction run makes every nested block unreachable to the checker. **The completeness gap is in the walk and not in `Block`.** **6. What each surveyed system would require `Block` to carry.** Under the JVM's discipline `Block` would carry a full register-file type at entry, every live register with its `TalTy`, restated at every block. Under Cranelift's and WebAssembly's it would carry nothing: Cranelift declares what crosses the edge and inherits the rest by dominance, WebAssembly declares `t1*->t2*` and inherits the rest by stack height. **A `tt-case` branch's binding is already the declaration those two want.** `ck-branches` takes `ftys` from `tck-find-ctor` over the scrutinee's constructor (`check.chiral:272-280`), and `DCtor`, `DData` and `CEnv` (`ssa.chiral:48-50`) are artifact data the checker reads rather than invents. The field types crossing into a branch are declared, positionally by `dsts`, in the same way Cranelift's block parameters are. What a branch does not declare is the enclosing environment, and no route-two system declares that either. **7. The three fields, counted.** `Instr` is eight constructors at `ssa.chiral:26-34`; the `(data Instr ()` header is line 26 and the eighth form `i-blen` is line 34, so PRB-75's `:26-33` and FD-17's `:27-34` each miss one end. `i-bput` defines no value. `i-bnew` (`:31`), `i-bget` (`:32`) and `i-blen` (`:34`) each bind a `dst` and carry no `ty`, and `check.chiral:215-218` supplies `(tt-bytes)`, `(tt-i64)` and `(tt-i64)` from the checker's own constants. Those three are the whole of the producer obligation route two would create here. **8. The producer, named.** `compile-fn` at `lib/lowering/upper/lower.chiral:412-420` emits every typed SSA `TFn`. It already holds everything an annotation would need: `expr` returns `(er-ok reg ty instrs st)` so every register it defines arrives with a `TalTy`, and `env` is `(List (Pair I64 TalTy))` all the way down. **`outline` already writes the tree's one full frame.** At `:311-320` it spells the enclosing scope as a boundary type, `(l-snoc TalTy (map-snd env) sty)`, into a `TalSig` for `name$k`, and `build-outline` at `:322-332` puts the same list in the outlined `TFn`'s `params`. The checker reads it back through `ck-fn` and `seed-params` (`check.chiral:287-292`). Producer and checker agree on the numbering by construction: `seed-env` runs from `(st 0 nil ...)` (`lower.chiral:412`, `ssa-fresh` at `:161`) and `seed-params` starts at `0`. **What `compile-fn` would have to know that it does not: nothing at the boundary, and nothing for the three byte forms either, because it never emits one.** Grepped over `lib/` and `prog/`: the four byte forms of `Instr` are constructed at exactly one place, `lib/lowering/upper/optimize.chiral:206-209`, which rebuilds an instruction that already exists under a register remap, so nothing in the tree originates one. The byte cells that exist are hand-written in the erased `TalIr` below the check, `lib/lowering/tal/bytes.chiral:54-67` and `lib/lowering/tal/sys-linkage.chiral:44-61`, over `ti-bnew`, `ti-bget`, `ti-bput` and `ti-blen` (`lib/lowering/tal/ir.chiral:26-29`), and `ck-prog` never sees them. PRB-75's laundering path has no producer to charge today, and that is a fact about reachability rather than about the annotation. **9. The register allocator, and there is none.** `nregs` is `(st-nreg st3)`, the fresh counter's high-water mark (`lower.chiral:420`). `ck-fn` binds it and reads it nowhere (`check.chiral:290-292`). Downstream it is a frame size: `emit-fn` emits `((mach-pro m) nregs)` as the prologue (`lib/lowering/mach/emit-core.chiral:533`), and the x64 `Mach` turns it into one `sub rsp, imm32` over `frame nregs` (`lib/lowering/x64/mach.chiral:73`, `frame` at `:53`). One stack slot per SSA register, no colouring and no spilling, so no pass has to preserve an annotation across an allocation. **Where an allocator does exist, the published position is that it consumes the register types and does not produce them.** CompCert's is untrusted and outside Coq, COMPCERTALLOC:13 "(** Register allocation by external oracle and a posteriori validation. *)" declared as COMPCERTALLOC:1355 "Parameter regalloc: RTL.function -> res LTL.function.", and the pass order hands the typing environment to the validator: COMPCERTALLOC:1360 "  match type_function f with" then COMPCERTALLOC:1365 "      | OK tf => do x <- check_function f tf env; OK tf". The obligation is per pseudo-register, COMPCERTALLOC:690 "  the type [env r] of [r] is compatible with the type of [l]. *)" at COMPCERTALLOC:698 "Definition loc_type_compat (env: regenv) (l: loc) (e: eqs) : bool :=". Cranelift says the same about block parameters, CRANECMP:135 "the register allocator treats them very" CRANECMP:136 "similarly. Arguments are assigned to registers or stack locations." **10. The one surveyed case that charges the allocator charges it because the checker was weakened to fit a smart card.** LEROYBCV:2980 "memory requirements, however, it has only one global register type that is shared between all control" points in the method, and for the same reason LEROYBCV:2983 "be empty at each branch instruction and at each branch target instruction." is required of the stack. The producer pays by rewriting: LEROYBCV:3019 "normalizations around branches and register reallocation by graph coloring, and are described in" [31]. That bill is measured against route one's, LEROYBCV:3023 "code, the increase in code size is less than 2%, and the increase in registers is negligible [31]." and LEROYBCV:3028 "Moreover, the size of the extra information is much smaller (2% vs. 50%)." **Not settled, and searched for.** No pinned source gives a size or a time cost for block-parameter annotations in an SSA IR; Cranelift and LLVM publish none, and the only two producer-cost figures located are Leroy's 50% and 2%, both for flat bytecode over a locals array. Proof-Carrying Code could not be read. Necula, "Proof-Carrying Code", POPL 1997, and Necula and Lee, "The Design and Implementation of a Certifying Compiler", PLDI 1998, were both located at `homes.cs.washington.edu/~mernst/teaching/6.893/readings/` and both fetched at HTTP 200, and both are page scans: measured at 56 and 334 `/CCITTFaxDecode` image streams with zero font objects, zero `/ToUnicode` maps and zero `/Differences` vectors, so this sandbox has no text to pin. Necula's own paper index at `people.eecs.berkeley.edu/~necula/papers.html` returns HTTP 404 and redirects to a departmental IT page, and FD-14 already recorded a 404 for the Berkeley PDF. Nothing in this row rests on PCC; LEROYBCV:2941 attributes the producer-computes-the-types split to Necula and Lee at second hand. Rose and Rose, "Lightweight Bytecode Verification", is behind Springer and was not fetched; its content is attested here only by LEROYBCV:2940-2964. The WebAssembly core specification's typing rules for `block`, `loop` and `if` are SpecTec macros: `document/core/valid/instructions.rst` fetched at HTTP 200 and its rule bodies read `$${rule: Instr_ok/block}` with no readable judgment, which is the same measurement FD-17 took on `ref.cast`, so the block-typing account rests on the syntax chapter and the algorithm appendix, both pinned. JSR 202 and the CLDC specification were not fetched; the split-verifier lineage is taken from LEROYBCV and from the JVM specification's own deferral, JVMS4:12148 "                              Verification on Java Virtual Machine implementations supporting the Java ME" CLDC and Java Card profiles being governed by their respective specifications. `regalloc2`, Cranelift's allocator crate, was not surveyed. Nothing here rules on whether the tree should adopt route one, route two's missing three fields, or neither; that is the author's call and PRB-75 already carries the instruction-level half of it. **Reachability and transcription.** `raw.githubusercontent.com/llvm/llvm-project/main/llvm/docs/LangRef.rst` returns HTTP 404: the file is `LangRef.md` and is pinned under that name, measured against the GitHub contents API listing for `llvm/docs`. `raw.githubusercontent.com/WebAssembly/multi-value/main/proposals/multi-value/Overview.md` returns HTTP 404; the branch is `master` and the pin uses it. Seven of the eight pins this run added are `raw`. `LEROYBCV` is `transcribed` by a stdlib-only extractor written for this run: it decodes Flate streams and `/Differences` vectors and reconstructs inter-word spacing from `TJ` kerning below -120 units, so ligatures and word breaks survive where FD-14's pins lost them. ### FD-20 accepting a target program witnesses target well-typedness alone, and every published route to a preservation claim is a function of two programs

- state:    OPEN
- claim:    The question, for `.planning/TAL-CONFORMANCE-QUEUE.md` slice R6: what does accepting a target program witness, and how do certifying compilers relate the source and the target? **An acceptance witnesses progress and preservation for the target under the target's own type system, and it names no source term. Certifying compilation does not close that gap, because the certificate attests the same target-only safety policy and Necula prices it himself as too coarse to catch an optimizer error. Every published construction that reaches a preservation claim is a function of TWO programs, and the split between them is arity: `Validate(S, C)` and `V : Source x Target -> boolean` take the source and the target, while `ck-prog` takes the target.** Two families reach the claim and they differ in when the relation is discharged. Translation validation discharges it once per compilation, at the price of a validator that is itself untrusted until it is proved. A verified compiler discharges it once over all programs. Both state the relation over semantics for each side and neither mentions a type translation. **So the answer to the total-`ttype` question is a fork, and it orders the queue.** A type-preservation claim of FD-15's published shape puts `[[t]]` in its own conclusion, so the total type translation FD-15 found missing is a precondition of writing the sentence down. A semantic-preservation claim of CompCert's shape names no type translation and requires instead an operational semantics for each side plus a two-argument relation over them. The tree holds one side of that already: `lib/lowering/tal/eval.chiral` is the target's operational semantics at 187 lines with zero importers, which is the object every surveyed validator is proved against.
- measured: **1. What a target-level acceptance witnesses, at TAL's own source.** TAL states the independence as the feature: TALTR:54 "the safety of the resulting assembly code can be checked independently of the source code or" the compiler. What acceptance yields is a property of the target alone, TALTR:1905 "Corollary 6.3 (Type Safety)If|-P, then there is no stuckPsuch thatP|->-->P." No source term occurs in that statement. TALT lists what still has to be trusted for it to mean anything, TALT:75 "One must trust that the TALx86 type checking soft-" ware correctly implements its type system. **2. The certifying compiler attests the same target-only property, and its author says so in the same paragraph.** NECTHESIS:1051 "handle complex safety properties. In contrast, certifying compilation as presented in this" dissertation handles only type safety. The receiver's position is NECTHESIS:2159 "have to trust the certifying compiler because it has other means to verify that the emitted" code has the desired properties, and the limit is stated four lines later: attaching the compiler to the PCC infrastructure does NECTHESIS:2164 "not verify the complete correctness of the compiler, but only that it emits code with certain" properties, such as type safety. **Necula then prices his own certificate against a source-to-target relation and calls it too coarse.** TVPLDI:265 "to verify full semantic equivalence but verifies only that the" output has certain expected properties, and a criterion of that kind, TVPLDI:423 "source is memory safe then so is the target, such as checked" TVPLDI:424 "by the Touchstone certifying compiler) does not allow the" TVPLDI:425 "detection of subtle errors in the optimizer." **3. Every published route to a preservation claim takes two programs, and the arity is the whole difference.** Necula's per-run form: TVPLDI:47 "we cannot prove that a compiler is always correct maybe" we can at least check the correctness of each compilation, and the object carrying it is a relation, TVPLDI:495 "simulation relation is a witness that two program fragments" are equivalent. Pnueli's group states the family identically, VOC:66 "which, after every run" of the compiler a validating tool formally confirms that the target code produced is a correct VOC:68 "translation of the source program.", and TVLOOPS:15 "self, translation validation validates the correctness of" each translation. CompCert types the object, COMPCERTCACM:435 ": a boolean-valued function" `Validate` COMPCERTCACM:439 ") that verifies the property" S ~ C a posteriori, and the two-argument shape is in the shipping code: COMPCERTALLOC:1345 "Definition check_function (rtl: RTL.function) (ltl: LTL.function) (env: regenv): res unit :=". Tristan and Leroy give the type, VALSCHED:99 "it sufces to model the validator as a function" V : Source x Target -> boolean whose truth implies the VALSCHED:114 "desired semantic equivalence result between the source code" and the compiled code. The proved-once alternative carries the same arity, COMPCERTDRV:446 "Theorem transf_c_program_correct:" concluding a backward simulation between the source program's semantics and the target program's, with COMPCERTDRV:419 "Theorem c_semantic_preservation:" as its per-program half. **4. What each addition costs, measured.** An unverified per-run validator: TVPLDI:23 "can be implemented with about the effort typically" TVPLDI:24 "required to implement one compiler pass.", and it buys confidence with no formal guarantee, because COMPCERTCACM:486 "a verified compiler: the validator could itself be incorrect." Closing that costs a proof, COMPCERTCACM:489 "is verified if it is accompanied with a formal proof of the" following property, and the price is on the record for two scheduling optimizations: VALSCHED:4308 "The Coq development accounts for approximatively 11000 lines" VALSCHED:4309 "of code. It took one person-year to design the validators, program" them and prove their correctness. A certificate in place of a relation costs artifact bytes instead: a Popcorn compiler CERTINDEP:1228 "of source code and compiles to 816 kilobytes of object code" carries annotations that naively CERTINDEP:1256 "is over 4.5 megabytes, several times the size of the object" code, and the paper's techniques bring it down so that total annotation size drops from 4.5 CERTINDEP:1337 "megabytes to 419 kilobytes and verification time is reduced" from 59 to 34.5 seconds. The certificate route also leaves a trusted base, FPCC:24 "only if there's no bug in the typing rules, or in the type-" checker or in the assembler; shrinking it is a separate program of work whose whole claim is that the FPCC:110 "trusted base can be smaller: its trusted base consists only" of the foundational verification system, the machine instruction semantics and the safety policy, making foundational proof-FPCC:43 "carrying code is PCC with trusted components an order" FPCC:44 "of magnitude smaller than previous PCC systems." **5. Whether a preservation claim can be stated without a total type translation. Yes for one family and no for the other, and the fork is which relation is preserved.** FD-15 measured the typed statement as five lemmas of the shape `|- e : t` implies `|- [[e]] : [[t]]`. `[[t]]` stands in the conclusion, so a total function on types is a precondition of writing the lemma at all, and nothing in this slice's sources weakens that. The semantic statement mentions no types. It assumes two semantics, COMPCERTCACM:126 "mantics for the source and target languages that associate" observable behaviors to S and C, and the validator is discharged against one of them: VALSCHED:135 "We then prove the correctness of these validators" VALSCHED:136 "against an operational semantics." Necula's validator is untyped throughout and TVPLDI:260 "then uses symbolic evaluation along with constraint solving" over the two intermediate forms. So the typed route needs the total `ttype` at `lib/lowering/upper/lower.chiral:35` that FD-15 measured as partial and non-injective, and the semantic route needs an evaluator for each side. **6. Where `ck-prog` sits against that, structurally.** `ck-prog` is `(-> CEnv Prog TckR)` at `lib/lowering/tal/check.chiral:308`. Both arguments are target-level: `CEnv` at `lib/lowering/tal/ssa.chiral:50` holds `TalSig`s, `DData`s and literals, and `ck-prog` rebuilds its `fns` field out of the `Prog` it was handed (`lib/lowering/tal/check.chiral:312`, `prog-fns`). No `UT` and no `Core` term is in scope. An acceptance is therefore TALTR's Corollary 6.3 over this target and nothing beyond it. **7. The tree has already measured one target program that `ck-prog` accepts and that does not implement its source.** `records/enforcement-arc.md` EN-20, checked 2026-09-04: `arm-body`'s `(none)` arm at `lib/lowering/upper/closconv.chiral:1057` emitted the literal `0` as a whole function body, and on a control fixture whose family codomain is `I64` the row records that `const 0 : i64` matches the declared return, `ck-prog` accepts, and nothing anywhere reddens. Three printed lines separated the source's answer from the target's, `direct box-f: 30`, `via $apply: 0`, `via $apply2: -10`. The defect is E188 and it is closed. The acceptance is the measurement this slice needed, taken inside this tree before the question was asked. **8. What the unreached reference interpreter is worth to the semantic route.** `lib/lowering/tal/eval.chiral` is 187 lines with zero importers, re-measured by PRB-67 on 2026-09-06 and re-run here (`grep -rl 'lowering/tal/eval' --include='*.chiral' lib/ prog/` returns nothing). Its header at `:10` already states a target-only cross-check, no `ck-ok` program evaluates to `r-err`, which is Corollary 6.3 read operationally. That is the object VALSCHED:136 names, and it is the target half of what a `Validate(S, C)` would be stated over; `lib/evidence/interp.chiral` at 109 lines is the source half. `docs/elements/catalog.md:484` (E169) pairs exactly those two files as half (a) and separately calls `preserve-check` "ALREADY a translation validator". **That second reading does not survive the definitions above.** Every validator in these sources is a function of the source and the target, and `ck-prog`'s type admits only the target. **9. What the sources did not settle, and what could not be reached.** Pnueli, Siegel and Singerman's TACAS 1998 original is behind Springer's paywall (`link.springer.com/content/pdf/10.1007/BFb0054170.pdf` returns a 3,038-byte HTML shell) and no open copy was found at `cs.nyu.edu`, `cs.tau.ac.il`, `wisdom.weizmann.ac.il` or `web.archive.org`; `VOC` and `TVLOOPS`, both by Pnueli's group, carry the family's own statement in its place. `people.eecs.berkeley.edu/~necula/` now 302s to a site that returns a 404 wrapper for every paper path, so the PLDI 2000 paper was taken from `web.archive.org`. Necula and Lee's PLDI 1998 was re-measured and FD-19's finding holds: the CMU handout copy is 334 `/CCITTFaxDecode` streams with zero `/FontFile`, so it is a page scan and Necula's 1998 thesis was pinned instead. Colby et al., "A Certifying Compiler for Java" (PLDI 2000), returns 404 at the author URL and stays unreached; nothing in this row rests on Special J. No source in this set states a preservation theorem over a partial type translation, and none states one over a checker that reads the target alone.

## The pipeline tooling

### FD-12 the scaffolder wrote the INDEX SPEC link one directory too shallow

- state:    FIXED
- claim:    `docs/examples/INDEX.md` spells the SPEC cell relative to its own directory. All 119 of its SPEC-linked rows read `[SPEC](../../docs/elements/specs/…)`, and `tools/pack/pack.py --spec` is the only writer of that cell.
- measured: the scaffolder emitted `../docs/elements/specs/`, one `../` short, which resolves from `docs/examples/` to `docs/docs/elements/specs/` and lands on nothing. The line was wrong from the start and no gate sees it: ledger-lint check F checks only link-shaped targets in `examples/`, so a markdown link with a wrong prefix passes. It was repaired by hand twice with the line left untouched, at `857a005` for E187 ("E187's SPEC link resolved to docs/docs/") and again during E197's SPEC run on 2026-09-05. Measured off-tree on 2026-09-05 by scaffolding E172 into two throwaway copies: HEAD wrote `../docs/elements/specs/E172-file-kinds-SPEC.md`, normalizing to `docs/docs/elements/specs/E172-file-kinds-SPEC.md`, absent; the fixed line wrote `../../docs/elements/specs/E172-file-kinds-SPEC.md`, normalizing to `docs/elements/specs/E172-file-kinds-SPEC.md`, present. Zero INDEX rows still carry the broken spelling, so both hand repairs held. `pack.py` emits no other `../` path; its one other INDEX link, the example cell at `:877`, is a sibling filename and all 131 of those resolve. The tool side moved. `pack.py` is Python, outside the compiler's closure, so no fixpoint was owed.

## The crypto model

### FD-13 the permutation machine has four slots and a Keccak round has five steps

- state:    OPEN
- claim:    `.planning/CRYPTO-MODEL.md:118-121` says one machine covers Keccak-f and Ascon-p: `rows` rows of 5 lanes of width `w`, an S-box across each row, a linear layer, a round constant, and that the lane permutation "is a function of `rows` and becomes the identity at `rows = 1`", so Ascon's missing step needs no special case. The question: does a Keccak-f round decompose into exactly an S-box across each row, a linear layer, a lane permutation derived from `rows`, and a round constant, such that `rows = 1` yields Ascon-p's round with the lane permutation as the identity and no step left unaccounted for? **No.** The S-box slot and the round-constant slot hold. The four-slot list has no home for the rotation step, and the lane permutation carries no `rows` term to specialize.
- measured: **Five steps, four slots.** FIPS202:15 "A round of a KECCAK-p permutation, denoted by Rnd, consists of a sequence of five transformations, which are called the step mappings", and FIPS202:25 "the round function Rnd is the transformation that results from applying the step mappings , , , , and , in that order" (the five Greek names have no glyph mapping in the extraction and come out empty; KECCAKSUM:179 "# θ step", KECCAKSUM:184 "# ρ and π steps", KECCAKSUM:187 "# χ step" and KECCAKSUM:190 "# ι step" carry them). §4 lists four slots. §5 of the same document counts five: `.planning/CRYPTO-MODEL.md:156` opens "Of the five steps in a Keccak-shaped round" and `:162` names "the rotation layer" as its own row. The model already disagrees with itself two sections apart. **ρ is the step with no slot.** FIPS202:22 "is to rotate the bits of each lane by a length, called the offset, which depends on the fixed x and y coordinates of the lane" is the whole of it. It is intra-lane, one rotation per lane, and it is neither π nor the linear layer. KECCAKSUM:185 "  B[y,2*x+3*y] = rot(A[x,y], r[x,y])," fuses it with π in one assignment, which is where a reading that drops ρ comes from. **π carries no `rows`.** Algorithm 3 is FIPS202:23 "[x, y, z]= A[(x + 3y) mod 5, x, z]", whose effect is FIPS202:23 "is to rearrange the positions of the lanes". The formula has no row-count parameter to instantiate. Its source index at y = 0 is A[x, x], which reads a lane at every y in 0 to 4, so a one-row state has no π to restrict. Read forward from KECCAKSUM:185, a source lane (x, 0) lands at (0, 2x mod 5), scattering row 0 across all five rows. Filling the slot with the identity at `rows = 1` is a legal free choice and it is available. Deriving it from `rows` is the half that fails. **θ and the Σ functions are two shapes under one name.** θ's effect is FIPS202:21 "is to XOR each bit in the state with the parities of two columns in the array", spelled at KECCAKSUM:180 "  C[x] = A[x,0] xor A[x,1] xor A[x,2] xor A[x,3] xor A[x,4]," and KECCAKSUM:181 "  D[x] = C[x-1] xor rot(C[x+1],1),". Ascon's linear layer stays inside one word: ASCONSPEC:77 "s different rotated copies of each word (horizontally, within each word)." and ASCONSPEC:102 "<i>S</i><sub>0</sub> := <i>S</i><sub>0</sub> &oplus; (<i>S</i><sub>0</sub> &#8921; 19) &oplus; (<i>S</i><sub>0</sub> &#8921; 28)". At one row θ's column parity is trivial and the step survives as A[x] ^= A[x-1] xor rot(A[x+1],1), which still crosses lanes, and Ascon has no cross-lane linear step. The step whose position Σ occupies is ρ. Pairing Σ with θ is what leaves ρ homeless, so the mispairing and the missing slot are one defect. **The Keccak-f[1600] and Ascon-p numbers all hold.** Table 1 is FIPS202:15 "b 25 50 100 200 400 800 1600 w 1 2 4 8 16 32 64 l 0 1 2 3 4 5 6"; KECCAKSUM:168 "The state is organized as an array of $5 \times 5$ lanes, each of length $w \in \{1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 32, 64\}$ and $b=25w$." gives 25 lanes; KECCAKSUM:171 "is given by $n = 12+2l$, where $2^l = w$. This gives 24 rounds for" gives 24. ASCONSPEC:71 "The round transformation consists of the following three steps which operate on a 320-bit state divided into 5 words" of 64 bits each, and ASCONSPEC:67 "12 rounds in <span class=" names Ascon-p[12]. One qualifier: ASCONSPEC:65 "This permutation iteratively applies an SPN-based round transformation for up to 16 rounds;" and 8 rounds are standardized as Ascon-p[8], so 12 is one of two standardized round counts. **Keccak-f[800] does not arrive free.** `:135` says it "arrives free as `rows = 5, w = 32`" while the machine's rounds row at `:127` carries 24. Table 1 gives l = 5 for b = 800, and FIPS202:26 "KECCAK-f [b] = KECCAK-p[b, 12 + 2l]." puts Keccak-f[800] at 22 rounds. Changing `rows` and `w` alone produces 24 rounds and a different permutation. Round count is a function of `w`, and the machine carries it as an independent type index. **What holds.** The S-box slot: KECCAKSUM:188 "  A[x,y] = B[x,y] xor ((not B[x+1,y]) and B[x+2,y])," varies x at fixed y, and χ's effect is FIPS202:24 "is to XOR each bit with a non-linear function of two other bits in its row"; ASCONSPEC:76 "applies a 5-bit S-box 64 times in parallel in a bit-sliced fashion (vertically, across words)." One bitsliced 5-bit S-box across five lanes, both sides, exactly as `:108-110` claims. The round-constant slot: KECCAKSUM:191 "  A[0,0] = A[0,0] xor RC" against ASCONSPEC:75 "s a round-specific 1-byte constant to word <i>S</i><sub>2</sub>.", and FIPS202:25 "Algorithm 5: rc(t)" is the LFSR derivation `:130` names. Same shape, different lane. **Terminology.** FIPS 202 calls the 5-lane sub-array at fixed y a plane and reserves "row" for a 5-bit sub-array at fixed y and z. §4's "rows of 5 lanes" are FIPS 202's planes. A naming collision with no effect on the finding. **Not settled.** NIST SP 800-232 was fetched (HTTP 200, 1,194,488 bytes) and could not be read here: this sandbox has no `pdftotext` and no PDF library, and the document draws its body text from CID-keyed fonts with hex strings, so the heuristic extractor that produced the FIPS 202 pin returns font tables. The Ascon claims therefore rest on the designers' specification page, which names SP 800-232 as the standard. The Ascon v1.2 submission PDF does extract, and it loses inter-word spacing and maps the Σ glyph to S, so `:129`'s name "the Σ functions" is unverified against a pinned source. The step it names is pinned at ASCONSPEC:77 and ASCONSPEC:102.
- evidence: `.planning/CRYPTO-MODEL.md:106-135`, `:154-165`; pins `.planning/sources/FIPS202.txt` (FIPS 202, PDF at nvlpubs.nist.gov, origin `transcribed`, pinned 2026-09-08), `.planning/sources/KECCAKSUM.txt` (keccak.team specifications summary, origin `raw`, pinned 2026-09-08), `.planning/sources/ASCONSPEC.txt` (ascon.isec.tugraz.at specification page, origin `raw`, pinned 2026-09-08). The three pin dates are the UTC stamps `xlat pin` writes, and the session ran on 2026-09-07 local. The FIPS 202 pin is a text extraction and carries the transcriber's reading: line numbers are one per PDF page, and every Greek letter is empty, which is why the step names are quoted from KECCAKSUM.
