## Attribution across a pass that creates definitions

### FD-21 no surveyed system holds one total per-definition attribution across a creating pass, and all six are two-level with the created definition carrying its own identity

- state:    OPEN
- claim:    The question, for E184's decision 1 and R6: when a compiler pass creates or destroys definitions between the point an attribution's domain is fixed and the point the artifact is emitted, what is the domain of a per-definition attribution, and how do published systems keep that attribution total across the pass? **Six systems were read at their own sources: DWARF 5, the TC39 source map standard, LLVM's debug-info rules, LLVM's machine outliner, GHC's cost centres, and AutoFDO. Not one of them carries a single total function from a pre-pass definition set into one closed sum. Every one is two-level. Level one is an entity the artifact contains, level two is an entity the source declared, and a created definition enters level one with its OWN identity plus a typed field naming its origin where an origin exists. The two levels carry different constructors, so a created definition is never a value of the same sum as the definition it came from, and the relation between them is a field on the created entity.** **They then split on which level the attribution ranges over, and the discriminator is measurable: whether the consumer has to name a source entity the artifact no longer contains.** A debugger does, so DWARF keeps both levels and keeps an abstract instance root that has zero concrete instances. A profile that feeds back into the same compiler does not, so AutoFDO fixes the domain at the source level and makes the compiler re-create the pass's creations until the two agree, discarding whatever fails to match. Source maps and the machine outliner take the artifact alone and mark the unattributable. GHC has shipped BOTH domains as two flags and states the price of each in its own manual. **One system implements E184's R1 shape exactly, and its domain is the post-pass set.** LLVM annotates every instruction that has no source location with one of four named values and makes an unannotated absence detectable by a build mode, which is a closed sum with the no-origin arms spelled and no silent arm. **So E184's option (i), the pre-pass set alone as the domain of a total per-definition sum, has no occupant in this survey, and the two occupied shapes are (ii) and (iii).**
- measured: **1. DWARF's domain is the artifact, the source definition survives as a second kind of entry, and the relation is a field.** The source-level definition becomes an abstract instance root, DWARF5:5894 "AnysubroutineentrythatcontainsaDW_AT_inlineattributewhosevalueis" other than DW_INL_not_inlined. Each copy in the artifact is a separate entry, DWARF5:5964 "Eachinlineexpansionofasubroutineisrepresentedbyadebugginginformation" DWARF5:5966 "entrywiththetagDW_TAG_inlined_subroutine." and the back-link is a field on the copy, DWARF5:6070 "entryhasaDW_AT_abstract_originattributethatmaybeusedtoobtainthe" missing information from the associated abstract instance entry. **The cardinality is stated and it is neither one nor onto.** DWARF5:6056 "Anygivenabstractinstancetreemaybe" DWARF5:6058 "associatedwithseveraldifferentconcreteinlinedinstancetrees,ormayevenbeassociated" DWARF5:6060 "withzeroconcreteinlinedinstancetrees." A definition with zero surviving copies keeps its entry, which is the case E184 §1 M-D measures for a pruned `x64`. **2. DWARF names the created-with-no-origin case and gives it its own arm.** DWARF5:6148 "3.Aconcreteinstancetreemaycontainentrieswhichdonotcorrespondto" entries in the abstract instance tree, to describe new entities specific to a particular expansion, and those entries DWARF5:6154 "entriesintheabstractinstancetree,donotcontainDW_AT_abstract_origin" attributes and must contain all their own attributes directly. That is E184 option (ii)'s `ft-created` arm, in a published standard, as the arm for a name with no pre-pass existence. **3. DWARF splits the creators by mechanism and gives each its own construct, so a single arm does not cover them.** An out-of-line copy of an inlinable subroutine reuses the origin field, DWARF5:6188 "concreteout-of-lineinstancemakesuseofDW_AT_abstract_originattributesin" exactly the same way, and differs in tag and in ownership, DWARF5:6214 "treesbeownedbythesameparententry." A compiler-created intermediary gets a different attribute entirely, DWARF5:6270 "Atrampolineisacompiler-generatedsubroutinethatservesasanintermediaryin" making a call to another subroutine, represented by a subprogram entry with a DWARF5:6280 "DW_AT_trampolineattribute.Thevalueofthatattributeindicatesthetarget" subroutine of the trampoline. LLVM emits exactly that for a created global whose name is the original plus a suffix, `sub1_.t0p` beside `sub1_`, and LLVMDBG:1431 "The targetFuncName field is the name of the function that the trampoline" calls. **This is the direct answer to whether `specialize-singletons` and `outline` take one arm or two: DWARF gives three mechanisms three constructs, and the discriminator is what the created entity stands in for.** **4. Source maps range over the generated file and make the unattributable position a representable shape.** The mapped-from side is defined as SRCMAP:257 "code which is generated by the compiler or transpiler." and a mapping segment is SRCMAP:794 "each segment is made up of 1, 4, or 5 variable length fields." The one-field shape is the created case: SRCMAP:807 "Segments with one field are intended to represent generated code that is unmapped because there is no corresponding original source code" SRCMAP:807 "such as code that is generated by a compiler." The standard states no totality requirement over generated positions, so a generated position may carry no segment at all, and the format cannot tell that case from a position nobody mapped. **5. LLVM is the one surveyed system that spells R1's shape, and it spells it over the post-pass set.** The guarantee is stated over the artifact, LLVMDBG:92 "the source-level state of the program**, regardless of which LLVM" optimizations have been run, and it is priced in accuracy for sampled profiles, LLVMDBG:72 "Maintaining" LLVMDBG:73 "deterministic and distinct source locations is necessary to maximize the" accuracy of mapping hardware sample counts to LLVM IR. The rule for a created instruction is a closed set of named reasons: LLVMDBGUP:166 "Whenever a new instruction is created and there is no suitable location for that" instruction, that instruction should be annotated accordingly, from a set LLVMDBGUP:168 "of special `DebugLoc` values that can be set on an instruction to annotate the" reason it has no valid location. The four are compiler-generated, LLVMDBGUP:172 "compiler-generated instruction, i.e. it is not associated with any user source" code; dropped, LLVMDBGUP:175 "intentionally had its source location removed, according to the rules for" dropping locations; unknown, LLVMDBGUP:179 "a known or currently knowable source location, e.g. that it is infeasible to" determine; and temporary, for instructions not expected to be emitted. **The silent arm is the one thing the design refuses.** A build LLVMDBGUP:190 "built with coverage-tracking" keeps track of these special locations LLVMDBGUP:192 "these special locations in order to detect unintentionally-missing locations;" and the manual's own reason for preferring an absence over a wrong annotation is LLVMDBGUP:194 "an absent location can be detected" and fixed. That is R1's no-`_`-arm requirement with a checker behind it, at instruction granularity, over the code the artifact contains. **6. LLVM's machine outliner is the published pass closest to this tree's `outline`, and its created definition gets an entry with no origin field and a dropped interior.** The created name is generated by the pass, LLVMOUTLINER:893 "  // Create the function name. This should be unique." and the scheme is a counter suffix the source calls unstable, LLVMOUTLINER:894 "  // FIXME: We should have a better naming scheme. This should be stable," regardless of changes to the outliner's cost model, built as LLVMOUTLINER:899 "  FunctionName += std::to_string(Name);". The body's attribution is discarded deliberately: LLVMOUTLINER:950 "    // Don't keep debug information for outlined instructions." with `auto DL = DebugLoc();` set on every duplicated instruction. The created function itself enters the domain as its own subprogram, LLVMOUTLINER:1002 "  // If there's a DISubprogram associated with this outlined function, then" emit debug info for it, built at LLVMOUTLINER:1017 "        0 /* Line 0 is reserved for compiler-generated code. */," and flagged LLVMOUTLINER:1020 "        DINode::DIFlags::FlagArtificial /* Compiler-generated code. */,". **No `DW_AT_abstract_origin` is set on it**, which is consistent with DWARF's rule, because LLVM's outliner merges N candidate sites into one function and no single origin exists. **This tree's `outline` (`lib/lowering/upper/lower.chiral:311-320`) extracts one case from one definition, so it has exactly one origin and DWARF's out-of-line-instance construct fits it where LLVM's outliner does not.** **7. GHC has shipped both domains, and its manual states the price of each.** The early domain is `-fprof-auto`: GHCPROF:204 "to the compiler, it automatically inserts a cost centre annotation" around every binding not marked INLINE, and the price is the passes, GHCPROF:207 "cost-centre annotations as the optimiser is careful to not move them around or" remove them, which can severely affect how the program is optimised. The late domain is `-fprof-late`: GHCPROF:499 "This is done late in the compilation pipeline after the optimizer has run and unfoldings have been created." and the price is stated in the next paragraph, GHCPROF:504 "While the results of this are generally informative, some of the compiler internal names" GHCPROF:505 "will leak into the profile." The created-name shape is this tree's exactly, a suffixed global with no source counterpart: cost centres added after simplification mean the names differ from the source, GHCPROF:418 "they appear in the source program (e.g. a call to " `f` may be inlined with GHCPROF:419 "    its wrapper, resulting in an occurrence of its worker, " `$wf`. The pass code exists as its own module, GHCLATECC:3 "-- | Adds cost-centers after the core pipline has run." **GHC also measures the case where a pass creates top-level definitions and the attribution gives up on distinguishing them.** Full laziness lifts constants out of function bodies, GHCPROF:316 "and higher), as constants in function bodies will be lifted to the top-level" GHCPROF:317 "and become CAFs. You will probably need to consult the Core" to determine what these CAFs correspond to, and the default attribution for all of them is one shared bucket, GHCPROF:311 "assigns every CAF in a module a stack consisting of the single cost" centre `M.CAF`. So the created definitions get a catch-all until a flag gives each its own, and even then their relation back to source is left to a human reading the intermediate dump. **8. The published lambda-lifting treatment carries the mapping inside the pass, and the lifted definition is a new name whose source binding is consumed.** Graf and Peyton Jones state the operational consequences as a numbered list: the transformation SELLAM:353 "Itcreatesanewtop-levelde" finition `f` with a mark, replaces the `let` by its body, and rewrites every occurrence at the call sites. The pass threads the relation explicitly, SELLAM:1071 "5.1VariablesInthevariablecase,wecheckifthevariablewasli" fted to top-level by looking it up in the supplied expander mapping. They attribute the construction to SELLAM:307 "Johnsson(1985)introducedit" for efficient code generation of lazy functional languages to G-machine code. **This is the one surveyed source whose shape matches R6's prescription of a mapping produced by the pass and carried onward, and the object it maps is a LOCAL binding that the transformation consumes**, so the original has no separate fate to keep. `lift-lifted` (`lib/lowering/upper/specialize-singleton.chiral:195`) lifts a field body out of a global that `prune-live` (`:227`) keeps whenever something still references it, which is the case lambda lifting does not have. **9. AutoFDO makes the domains agree instead of making the attribution total, and it discards the residue.** The attribution unit is a source location annotated with an inline stack, AUTOFDO:78 "otherentriesrepresentthesourcelocationsoftheinlined" call sites, and the database is a forest, AUTOFDO:156 "leisorganizedasaforestoftrees" whose AUTOFDO:161 "Innernodesinatreerepresent" AUTOFDO:162 "functioninlineinstances.Leaftreenodesrepresentsource" statements. All instances of one function AUTOFDO:420 "(inlinedorstandalone)arerepresentedbydifferentsubtrees." The reconciliation is a pass that re-performs the creations: the preparation AUTOFDO:423 "steptransformstheIRandmakesitresembletheinlinetree" structure stored in the profile, after which AUTOFDO:510 "eachoneofthenewlycreatedinlineinstanceshasexactly" one matching inner node in the profile tree. What fails to match is dropped: the profile AUTOFDO:516 "samplesfromcoldinstancesthatarenotinlined" AUTOFDO:517 "duringthepreparationsteparediscarded." So the totality AutoFDO gets is bought by re-running the creating pass under the profile's control, and the attribution itself stays partial. **10. What the survey says about R6's premise, which E184 §1 M-D already refuted from the source.** No surveyed system carries a rename mapping, because no surveyed pass renames. DWARF, LLVM and GHC all give a created definition a new identity in the artifact's own namespace and relate it to its origin through a field or a tag, and DWARF keeps the original entry even when the artifact contains nothing of it. **R6's "fates are a relation across a renaming" has no occupant in this survey; the occupied shape is a relation across a creation, with the created entity in the domain.** This row records the contradiction and amends nothing. **11. What the survey does not settle.** No source states a conservation obligation of R7's shape, that folding the attribution reproduces the emitted set and a missing or doubled entry fails the build. LLVM's coverage-tracking mode is the nearest and it detects a missing annotation without failing the compilation. Nothing surveyed prices the cost of computing the domain in two stages, which is E184 option (ii)'s stated cost. And nothing surveyed decides whether a definition that survives a pass and is also the origin of a created one takes one arm or two, because DWARF's abstract instance root sits in a different sum from the arms a concrete instance carries. **12. What could not be reached.** Johnsson's "Lambda Lifting: Transforming Programs to Recursive Equations" (FPCA 1985, LNCS 201, pages 190 to 203) is behind Springer's paywall: `link.springer.com/content/pdf/10.1007/3-540-15975-4_37` returns a 231,292-byte HTML shell with HTTP 200, and no open copy was found. Graf and Peyton Jones' ICFP 2019 paper is pinned in its place and carries the attribution to Johnsson. `llvm/docs/SourceLevelDebugging.rst` and `llvm/docs/HowToUpdateDebugInfo.rst` both return HTTP 404 at `main`, because the LLVM docs moved to Markdown; the `.md` paths were pinned. The DWARF 5 PDF was pinned as a transcription for the reason recorded in `evidence:` below.
- evidence: Ten pins added 2026-09-09 in `.planning/sources/`. **Seven are origin `raw`**: `SRCMAP` (ECMA-426, the TC39 Source map format specification, `spec.emu` at `github.com/tc39/ecma426`, which renders to `tc39.es/ecma426/`; the rendered HTML was fetched first at 568,647 bytes and rejected as a pin because its inline scripts put the normative text on lines shared with minified JavaScript), `LLVMDBG` and `LLVMDBGUP` (`llvm/docs/SourceLevelDebugging.md` and `llvm/docs/HowToUpdateDebugInfo.md`), `LLVMOUTLINER` (`llvm/lib/CodeGen/MachineOutliner.cpp`), `CLANGUSERS` (`clang/docs/UsersManual.md`, pinned for the sample-profile build cycle at CLANGUSERS:3097 "DWARF information can be improved with the `-fdebug-info-for-profiling`" and not otherwise quoted), `GHCPROF` (`docs/users_guide/profiling.rst`) and `GHCLATECC` (`compiler/GHC/Core/LateCC.hs`). **Three are origin `transcribed`**, because this sandbox has no PDF library and the text is a stdlib zlib-plus-content-stream extraction: `DWARF5` (DWARF Debugging Information Format Version 5, February 13 2017, `dwarfstd.org/doc/DWARF5.pdf`), `SELLAM` (Graf and Peyton Jones, "Selective Lambda Lifting", ICFP 2019, `arxiv.org/pdf/1910.11717`) and `AUTOFDO` (Chen, Li and Moseley, "AutoFDO: Automatic Feedback-Directed Optimization for Warehouse-Scale Applications", CGO 2016, `research.google.com/pubs/archive/45290.pdf`). All three lose inter-word spacing, which is why every span above is quoted unspaced; the `fi` and `fl` ligatures come through as single control bytes, so no span above crosses one; embedded font streams that happen to contain a `BT` operator were filtered out by a printable-ratio test before pinning, and the DWARF pin is 29,864 lines after that filter. Every span above was checked unique in its pin with `grep -acF`, which is what `xlat check` requires. Live tree spans, all re-read at HEAD during this run: `lib/lowering/upper/specialize-singleton.chiral:193-206` (`lift-fields`, `lift-add`, `lift-lifted`, `process-mk`) and `:227-232` (`prune-live`, `drop-pruned`, `specialize-singletons`, `sp-finish`); `lib/lowering/upper/lower.chiral:311-320` (`outline`, building `<name>$<ncase>` at `:313`, `:315` and `:318`); `lib/lowering/compile-back.chiral:272` (`(cons main extra)`), `:271` (`(mk-skrec name (sk-extern er))`) and `:214` (`prune-fix`); `lib/lowering/compile-front.chiral:211-223` (`peel-def`, four `(none)` arms). The 13 created names and 44 offered for pruning on `prog/compiler.prog` are `docs/examples/E184-def-fate-sum.md` §1 M-D's figures and are read here rather than re-derived. `docs/arcs/enforcement-arc.md` R1, R6 and R7 are the requirements this row is measured against. The dates are the UTC stamps `xlat pin` writes.
- checked:  2026-09-09
- element:  E184, decision 1 in `docs/examples/E184-def-fate-sum.md` §6. The domain stays the author's call and this row prices it: option (i) has no occupant in the survey, option (ii) is DWARF's shape, option (iii) is the shape of the one system that ships R1's total closed sum. R1's single-sum-over-one-domain form is what the survey finds unoccupied, and that is a change to R1 rather than an answer to it.

### FD-22 the tree's only shipping attribution consumer reads exclusively the population the post-pass domain deletes, and the published price of the retained level is artifact bytes this element never writes

- state:    OPEN
- claim:    The question, for E184's decision 1 as amended: when an attribution's consumer must name entities the emitted artifact does not contain, what does each published domain choice cost that consumer, and what does it cost the producer to keep the pre-pass entities addressable? **Twelve further sources were read at their own sources, and the answer has two halves.** **The consumer half is lopsided.** Every published consumer that must name a source entity the artifact lost pays for it with a second retained level, and each one states its own failure when that level is missing: `gdb` cannot place a breakpoint at an inlined call site because the site holds no code, and LLVM's coverage tool cannot tell an unreachable line from an executed one because it does not hold the frontend's knowledge. E184's consumer is on that side, and this run measures it rather than assuming it: `blame-chain` and `format-blame` are the only shipping readers of the attribution, they key on `def-name`, and all **347** records they read name definitions that produced no artifact entity. Under **(iii)** those 347 leave the domain, and with them three of R1's four arms lose every member they have, leaving `emitted` alone and turning one total function into one sum plus two side relations. **The producer half is balanced and it goes the other way.** The published price of keeping the pre-pass level addressable is artifact size, measured once by Fission at **87%** of object-file bytes and **84%** of binary bytes for the whole of debug information. E184's conformance row 4 pins every emitted ELF byte-identical, so the attribution ships no bytes and that price does not transfer; what **(ii)** costs here is a two-stage domain and a wider fold, against the fact that **(iii) is the shape of the code as it stands**, which threads `skips` beside `acc` in two channels today. **The sub-question splits two to one with one abstention, and the majority answer is ONE arm carrying a mechanism tag.** GCC gives every creating mechanism one construct, an origin pointer plus a `SUFFIX` string, and its function-splitting pass is `outline`'s near twin under that one construct. MLIR discriminates by the arity of the origin set and not by the mechanism. DWARF gives three mechanisms three constructs. LLVM's machine outliner does not reach the case. **No option is dominated and the ruling stays the author's.**
- measured: **1. THE DISCRIMINATOR APPLIED TO THIS TREE, VERIFIED AT HEAD `6e725c3` RATHER THAN ASSUMED.** R1's four arms and their populations, read from `docs/decisions/decision-def-partition.md:104-118` and not re-derived: `emitted` **22,395 of 22,742** across 63 roots; `skipped` **347**, in three producing channels (`body-does-not-lower` 182 at `compile-back.chiral:271`, `callee-cascade` 160 at `prune-pass`, `extern-with-no-wrapper` 5 at `filter-erasable`); `erased-by-design` **0**; `specialized-into` **unmeasured**. Three of the four name definitions with no emitted artifact entity. **The tree's only shipping consumer of the attribution reads those three and nothing else.** `SkRec` is `(mk-skrec (def-name Str) (why SkReason))` at `lib/lowering/skip-diag.chiral:16`; `blame-chain` (`:92`) keys the walk on `def-name` and `skwhy-name`; `format-blame` (`:119-121`) renders the chain and is what E187's R5 gates byte-for-byte. Every one of the 347 records exists because a definition produced no `TFn`. **347 of 347 sit on FD-21's "must name" side of the discriminator, and 0 of 347 name an entity the artifact contains.** That is the measurement FD-21 stopped short of.
  **2. WHAT THE CONSUMERS ON THAT SIDE NEED, STATED BY THEIR OWN DOCUMENTATION.** A debugger needs the producer to have kept the lost entity: GDBINLINE:82 "For <small>GDB</small> to support inlined functions, the compiler must" GDBINLINE:83 "record information about inlining in the debug information". A symbolizer reconstructs entities that carry no symbol: LLVMSYMBOLIZE:333 "If a source code location is in an inlined function, prints all the inlined" frames. A profiler does the same on the call graph and does it by default: PERFREPORT:606 "	If a callgraph address belongs to an inlined function, the inline stack" will be printed, PERFREPORT:608 "	default, disable with --no-inline." **And `gdb` states the failure that arrives when the named entity is absent from the artifact.** GDBINLINE:110 "Setting breakpoints at the call site of an inlined function may not" GDBINLINE:111 "work, because the call site does not contain any code." and GDBINLINE:117 "cannot locate the return value of inlined calls after" using `finish`, which the manual files as GDBINLINE:119 "debugging information; after <code>finish</code>" a limitation of what the compiler emitted. **A consumer that must name an absent entity degrades in named ways, and the manual names them.**
  **3. THE COVERAGE CONSUMER FIXES ITS DOMAIN BEFORE THE OPTIMIZER RUNS, AND SAYS WHY.** The producer is the frontend: LLVMCOVMAP:60 "For each function that requires code coverage, the frontend has to create" LLVMCOVMAP:61 "coverage mapping data that can map between the source code ranges and" the profile instrumentation counters. **The format carries a region kind whose whole job is a source range with no artifact entity.** LLVMCOVMAP:90 "* Skipped regions are used to represent source ranges that were skipped" LLVMCOVMAP:91 "  by Clang's preprocessor. They don't associate with" counters, and LLVMCOVMAP:93 "  executed. They are used by the code coverage tool to mark the skipped lines" inside a function as non-code lines. It is a numbered arm in the encoding, LLVMCOVMAP:561 "  * 2 - This mapping region is a skipped region." **This is E184's `skipped` arm in a shipped format, and its domain is the pre-pass source set by construction.** The document also states, in one sentence, what is lost when the domain is fixed later: without the frontend's zero counters the tool would think unreachable lines were executed, LLVMCOVMAP:220 "as it doesn't possess the frontend's knowledge." **The consumer cannot reconstruct what the producer knew and discarded.**
  **4. THE PUBLISHED PRICE OF THE RETAINED LEVEL IS ARTIFACT SIZE, IT IS MEASURED ONCE, AND IT IS MEASURED FOR THE WHOLE OF DEBUG INFORMATION.** DEBUGFISSION:137 "The "Fission" project was started in response to the problems caused by huge amounts of debug information in large applications". The figure: DEBUGFISSION:141 "the debug information accounts for 87% of the total size of the object files sent as inputs to the link step, and 84% of the total size of the output binary", for a large C++ application at `-O2 -g`. The breakdown puts the entries themselves at DEBUGFISSION:143 "Debug Information Entries - .debug_info (11%)" of object-file size, with relocations at 46%. **This measures the retained source level as a whole and isolates nothing.** No source read in this run measures debug-info size attributable to abstract instance roots specifically, and that is what the tree would need to price DWARF's retention directly.
  **5. DWARF STATES WHAT THE RETAINED ROOT IS AND STATES THE MECHANISM THAT HOLDS ITS COST DOWN.** The abstract instance root is an entity with no runtime existence, by the standard's own words: DWARF5:5944 "sincesuchentriesdonotrepresentactual(concrete)instancesandthusdonotactually" exist at run-time. The cost is amortized by sharing, DWARF5:5922 "Attributesandchildreninanabstractinstancearesharedbyallconcrete" instances, and the copies pay only their difference, DWARF5:6062 "Concreteinlinedinstanceentriesmayomitattributesthatarenot" specific to the concrete instance. The standard also grants an explicit omission for the case where the retained level would not be read: DWARF5:6158 "abstractinstancetreetoomitdebugginginformationentriesforanonymous" entities that are unlikely to be needed in most inlined expansions. **So the second level is priced as shared storage and the standard leaves the producer a way to drop the part nobody reads.**
  **6. E184's CONSUMER IS INSIDE THE COMPILE, SO THE PRICED COST DOES NOT TRANSFER.** `docs/examples/E184-def-fate-sum.md` §6 conformance row 4 requires every emitted ELF to stay byte-identical, so the attribution reaches no artifact and pays none of section 4's bytes. **What (ii) costs this tree instead is a fold and a two-stage domain**, and R7's own open question 2 already prices the fold at 22,742 records on every build. The created set adds to that: 13 lifted globals plus 40 outlined extras is **53 on `prog/compiler.prog`**, and across the 63 roots it is unmeasured, which is `docs/examples/E184-def-fate-sum.md` §1 M-D's F3.
  **7. THE PUBLISHED COMPILER-INTERNAL ATTRIBUTIONS THAT ARE NOT DEBUG INFO ARE LOGS, AND GCC SHIPS A CREATE-AND-DESTROY RECORD WITH A NAMED CONSUMER.** GCCDEVOPTS:520 "Create a dump file containing information about creation of call graph" GCCDEVOPTS:521 "node clones and removals of call graph nodes during inter-procedural" GCCDEVOPTS:522 "optimizations and transformations.  Its main intended use is that tools" GCCDEVOPTS:523 "that create live-patches can determine the set of functions that need to" be live-patched to completely replace a particular function. **That is R6's relation and R7's create-and-destroy account, published, with the compile itself as the consumer.** The create record is one line per clone, GCCCLONES:329 "Dump information about creation of a call graph node clone to the dump file" GCCCLONES:330 "created by the -fdump-ipa-clones option.  ORIGINAL is the function being" cloned, emitted as GCCCLONES:345 "	       "Callgraph clone;%s;%d;%s;%d;%d;%s;%d;%s;%d;%d;%s\n"," which is origin name, uid, file, line, column, then the clone's four, then the suffix. The destroy record is one line per removal, GCCCGRAPH:2269 "	     "Callgraph removal;%s;%d;%s;%d;%d\n", asm_name (), get_uid (),". ⚑ **AND IT IS PARTIAL BY CONSTRUCTION, WHICH IS THE POINT FOR R7.** The removal line is guarded: GCCCGRAPH:2267 "  if (symtab->ipa_clones_dump_file && symtab->cloned_nodes.contains (this))", and `cloned_nodes` is populated only where a clone was dumped, GCCCLONES:354 "        symtab->cloned_nodes.add (original);". **A function removed without ever having been cloned leaves no record at all.** The nearest published occupant of R7's conservation obligation is a log restricted to the entities one pass touched, and it neither folds to the emitted set nor fails the build. FD-21 item 11 recorded that nothing surveyed states R7's obligation; this run finds the nearest occupant and measures its partiality.
  **8. LLVM's OPTIMIZATION REMARKS ARE THE OTHER ONE, AND THEY ARE EVENT-KEYED AND OPT-IN.** LLVMREMARKS:6 "LLVM is able to emit diagnostics from passes describing whether an optimization" LLVMREMARKS:7 "has been performed or missed for a particular reason, which should give more" insight into what the compiler did. The serialized record carries LLVMREMARKS:167 "Pass: <pass>" and LLVMREMARKS:170 "Function: <function>" with a `DebugLoc`, and its type is a closed set, LLVMREMARKS:177 "The following entries are mandatory:" beginning LLVMREMARKS:179 "- `<TYPE>`: can be `Passed`, `Missed`, `Analysis`," `AnalysisFPCommute`, `AnalysisAliasing`, `Failure`. **The sum is closed and the domain is a transformation event.** Nothing makes it total over definitions, and the filter is a regex over pass names, LLVMREMARKS:113 "Only passes whose name match the given (POSIX) regular expression will be" LLVMREMARKS:114 "serialized to the final output." **A per-definition fate cannot be recovered from it, because a definition no pass reported is indistinguishable from one no pass touched.**
  **9. MLIR IS A SECOND OCCUPANT OF R1's NO-`_`-ARM CLAUSE AT ENTITY GRANULARITY, AND ITS PASS-LEVEL RECORD IS AN EVENT STREAM.** MLIRLOC:324 "Source location information is an extremely integral part of the MLIR" MLIRLOC:325 "infrastructure. As such, location information is always present in the IR," MLIRLOC:326 "and must explicitly be set to unknown. Thus, an instance of the `unknown`" location represents an unspecified source location. **That is R1's requirement stated as an invariant of the IR rather than as a checker over it**, and it is the second occupant after LLVM's coverage-tracking build. Its pass machinery carries no per-entity account: MLIRPASS:1035 "MLIR provides a customizable framework to instrument pass execution and analysis" MLIRPASS:1036 "computation, via the `PassInstrumentation` class. This class provides hooks into" MLIRPASS:1037 "the PassManager that observe various events:", and the events are pass-granular, MLIRPASS:1048 "This callback is run right after a pass has been successfully executed." beside MLIRPASS:1051 "This callback is run right after a pass execution fails." **So MLIR splits the two jobs: the per-entity attribution is an always-present location on the entity, and the pass record is an observer stream that never claims to be total.**
  **10. THE SUB-QUESTION, ANSWERED FROM THE SOURCES. TWO PUBLISHED ANSWERS SAY ONE ARM, ONE SAYS TWO, ONE ABSTAINS.** **GCC says ONE.** Every creating mechanism goes through one construct with one origin pointer, GCCCLONES:494 "  new_node->clone_of = this;", and the mechanism travels as a parameter of that construct, GCCCLONES:331 "cloned, CLONE is the new clone.  SUFFIX is a string that helps identify the" GCCCLONES:332 "reason for cloning, often it is the suffix used by a particular IPA pass to" GCCCLONES:333 "create unique function names.  SUFFIX can be NULL and in that case the" dumping does not take place. **GCC's function-splitting pass is `outline`'s near twin and it uses that one construct.** GCCSPLIT:21 "/* The purpose of this pass is to split function bodies to improve" inlining, producing GCCSPLIT:34 "   func.part (...)" holding the big branch while `func` calls it, created at GCCSPLIT:1376 "  /* Now create the actual clone.  */" as GCCSPLIT:1380 "     split_point->split_bbs, split_point->entry_bb, "part");" with one boolean beside it, GCCSPLIT:1382 "  node->split_part = true;". **This tree's `outline` (`lib/lowering/upper/lower.chiral:311-320`, re-read at HEAD) does the same shape**: it builds `<name>$<ncase>`, emits `(i-call nreg <name>$<ncase> …)` back in the parent at `:318`, and returns the extra through `st-extra`. **MLIR says ONE, and discriminates on a different axis.** A single origin takes a name wrapped around a child location, MLIRLOC:210 "An instance of this location allows for attaching a name to a child location."; many origins take a tuple, MLIRLOC:171 "An instance of a `fused` location represents a grouping of several other" MLIRLOC:172 "source locations, with optional metadata that describes the context of the" fusion, written for exactly the case where MLIRLOC:174 "may be fused together, e.g. pattern rewriting, that normally result partial" MLIRLOC:175 "or even total loss of location information. With `fused` locations, this is" a non-issue. **MLIR's discriminator is the ARITY of the origin set and the mechanism is metadata.** **DWARF says TWO**, giving three mechanisms three constructs, which is FD-21 item 3 and is read here rather than re-derived. **LLVM's machine outliner abstains**, because it merges N sites and sets no origin field, which is FD-21 item 6. **Both of this tree's creators have exactly one origin.** `lift-lifted` (`lib/lowering/upper/specialize-singleton.chiral:195`, re-read at HEAD) builds `<gname>$<i>` from one field body of one global; `outline` builds `<name>$<ncase>` from one case of one definition. **So arity does not separate them and only the mechanism does**, which puts them on one arm under GCC's and MLIR's shapes and on two under DWARF's. ⚑ **One caveat the tree owes itself: GCC's mechanism tag is a `const char *` and MLIR's fusion metadata is an open attribute, and E184's R2 forbids a `Str` standing for a relation.** The majority answer is one arm carrying a mechanism tag, and the tag's spelling is this tree's own problem.
  **11. THE PRICE OF (ii) FOR THIS TREE.** The domain becomes 22,742 plus the created set, which is 53 on the one root where it is measured. The consumer keeps every one of its 347 subjects inside the sum, so `blame-chain` needs no second channel and R7 stays a flat set compare, which is what §6 already predicted. The published price of the retention, section 4's 87% and 84%, is artifact bytes and this element writes none. **What is left of (ii)'s cost is the two-stage domain computation**, which no source read in this run or in FD-21 prices, and one arm's worth of sum. **The change to today's code is real**: `lower-defs` threads `skips` beside `acc` at `lib/lowering/compile-back.chiral:270-272`, so (ii) merges two channels that the live code keeps apart, which is E184 §6's own M-B finding.
  **12. THE PRICE OF (iii) FOR THIS TREE.** The domain becomes the emitted set, so **347 of 22,742 definitions leave it**, plus however many of the 44 offered for pruning are actually pruned, which is unmeasured. `skipped`, `erased-by-design` and `specialized-into` then have zero members apiece, because every member each of them has is a definition with no artifact entity. **R1 becomes one arm plus new arms for created names, and the three arms R1 argued for become side relations beside the sum**, which is what §6's option (iii) already names for `SpRel` and which this run measures as covering the whole of the tree's shipping consumer. `blame-chain` and `format-blame` then read a relation that the fate function does not range over, and E187's R5 gate keeps working only because that relation still exists. **Against that, (iii) is the shape of the code as it stands**, two channels threaded separately, and it is the shape of the one surveyed system shipping R1's closed sum. **And LLVM's shipped arms are the DUAL of E184's**: LLVM names an artifact entity with no source, and E184's three arms name a source entity with no artifact. Adopting LLVM's domain therefore does not bring LLVM's arms with it.
  **13. WHETHER THE PRICING IS LOPSIDED. IT IS LOPSIDED ON THE CONSUMER AXIS AND BALANCED ON THE PRODUCER AXIS, AND NEITHER OPTION IS DOMINATED.** On the consumer axis the measurement is one-sided: 347 of 347 shipping records name entities (iii) removes from the domain, and every published consumer in this class keeps a second level for exactly that reason. On the producer axis it reverses: (iii) is today's code and (ii) is a merge of two channels plus a domain computed in two stages, and the one published price of (ii)'s retention is artifact bytes that E184's byte-identical ELF requirement makes inapplicable. **A finding that one option is dominated would be reportable and this is not one.**
  **14. WHAT WAS CONSULTED AND CAME BACK EMPTY.** The DWARF committee's issue list, `dwarfstd.org/issues.html`, HTTP 200 and 87,752 bytes, carries **216** issue entries and not one title matches abstract instance, inline, concrete, out-of-line, trampoline, clone, specialization, or any size-reduction term. **The committee publishes no cost statement about retaining abstract instance roots**, and the tree would need one to price DWARF's retention against Fission's whole-of-debug-info 87%. `llvm/docs/CoverageMappingFormat.rst` and `llvm/docs/CommandGuide/llvm-symbolizer.rst` both return HTTP 404 at `main` for the same reason FD-21 recorded, the move to Markdown, and the `.md` paths were pinned. MLIR's `mlir/docs/Dialects/Builtin.md` is a 2,102-byte include shell whose location text is generated from the TableGen file, so `BuiltinLocationAttributes.td` was pinned in its place. No source read in this run states a conservation obligation of R7's shape, which leaves FD-21 item 11 standing with GCC's partial create-and-destroy log as the nearest occupant found so far.

### FD-23 five principle-holders were read and four of the five put the created entity in the domain under its own identity, the fifth deleted it from the artifact instead, and the one unanimous result is that no holder folds a created entity's account into its origin's

- state:    OPEN
- claim:    The question, for E184's decision 1 and framed by commitment rather than by job: among systems that hold one of chirality's principles as a hard design commitment, what does that commitment force about the domain of a total claim when a transformation creates or destroys the entities the claim ranges over? **Five holders were read at their own sources: seL4's binary verification for P1, CakeML for P2, Fuchsia's component framework for P3, Rust's monomorphization and symbol scheme for P4, and Alive2 with Necula's validator for P5. The pieces AGREE on exactly one thing and it is the strongest result in the run: not one of the five attributes a created entity's property to the definition it came from. Four give the created entity a place of its own; the fifth removes it from the artifact side of the claim so that the domain never has to move. That is E184 option (i) refuted from five further directions, and it does not separate (ii) from (iii).** **The pieces DISAGREE on whether the domain widens, and the discriminator is measurable: whether the attributed property is determined by the origin's property.** Rust's safety claim keeps the pre-pass domain because a monomorphization is safe if its generic item is, so the created copies are never re-checked. CakeML's name claim widens to the post-pass set because a generated application stub has no source function at all. Fuchsia widens the DECLARED domain to an abstract container that may hold zero instances and refuses to widen it to instances, so an inbound route distributes to every member and an outbound route from a created member is stated to be inexpressible. seL4 narrows the artifact instead of widening the domain, and pays for it with a step the checker is told to trust. **The tree already measured which side of that discriminator it is on**: a created def's fate is not a function of its origin's, so the domain must move, which is what R1's ⚑ and E184 §1 M-D already say. **The sharpest single hit is CakeML, and it is a second occupant for (iii)'s shape that FD-21 did not have**: `make_name_alist` is a total function over the POST-pass location set with a spelled `unknown_clos_fun` arm, its discriminant is a numeric band comparison rather than a string, and the map it feeds is threaded through the whole backend, unioned with each later pass's own created-stub names, and consumed against the emitted sections with a spelled `«NOTFOUND»` default. That is R6's "produced by the pass and carried the rest of the way" and R7's artifact-side fold, shipped, in a verified compiler. **P6 does not exist and this run reports that rather than inventing a holder.**
- measured: **1. P1's HOLDER IS seL4's BINARY VERIFICATION, AND ITS COMMITMENT FORCED IT TO DELETE THE CREATED ENTITY RATHER THAN NAME IT.** The claim is total over a fixed function set, SEL4TV:115 "Weprovideafullproofofcorrectnessofthecompilationby" standard gcc 4.5.1 of all previously formally verified functions in seL4 at optimisation level 1. **The entity sets on the two sides do not match, and the paper measures the mismatch rather than assuming it away.** SEL4TV:1765 "Thereare540functionsinseL4,butfarlesssymbolsinthe" binary after inlining. The named cause is the compiler creating and reshaping functions: inlining is done on the binary side whenever SEL4TV:1154 "calledfunctiondoesnotmatchanyCfunction,whichmaybebe-" SEL4TV:1155 "causethecompilerinventedthefunctionormodi" ed its signature. **The answer is a normalisation, not a domain.** A problem space is built, SEL4TV:1148 "space,asharedgraphnamespaceintowhichthebinaryandC" bodies of the function of interest are copied, and SEL4TV:1150 "isfreetobemodi" ed, in particular by inlining function calls, and SEL4TV:1153 "Inliningisdoneonthebinarysideoftheproblemwheneverthe" called function does not match. At `-O2` the same move handles a broken calling convention: SEL4TV:1771 "-O2haveoptimisedfunctionsignatures,andthuscannotbepaired" via the calling convention with their C counterparts, addressed at SEL4TV:1773 "nementstagebyinliningthesefunctions" everywhere, and stated plainly in the discussion, SEL4TV:1844 "ken'functionboundariesbyinlining(inthelogic)theseproblem-" SEL4TV:1845 "aticfunctions,i.e.suchfunctionboundariesdisappear." The cause is named, SEL4TV:1833 "Thesebreakthecallingconvention,i.e.gcc-O2hasperformedaggressive" SEL4TV:1834 "interproceduraloptimisations." ⚑ **AND THE NORMALISATION IS AN UNCHECKED STEP, WHICH IS P1's OWN FAILURE MODE INSIDE P1's OWN HOLDER.** The problem space after inlining is included in the proof script, SEL4TV:1169 "proofscript,andthecheckertruststhatthisproblemspaceisderiv-" able from the functions of interest, and checking that inlining was per- SEL4TV:1171 "formedcorrectlydidnotseemworthwhile." The mechanism is in the shipping tool: GRAPHREFINE:712 "def inline_at_point (p, n, do_analysis = True):" rewrites the `Call` node into the caller's graph and records the step, GRAPHREFINE:721 "	p.inline_scripts[tag].append ((detail, idx, f_nm))", replayed by GRAPHREFINE:428 "	def replay_inline_script (self, tag, script):". So a record of the deletion exists and is replayable; what is not checked is that the deletion was sound. **What the commitment forced: keeping the domain fixed at the pre-pass set is purchasable, and its price is a trusted step at exactly the point where the entity sets disagree.**

  **2. P2's HOLDER IS CakeML, AND ITS COMMITMENT FORCED A TOTAL NAMING FUNCTION OVER THE POST-PASS SET WITH THE NO-ORIGIN ARMS SPELLED.** Closure conversion is a pure creator: CAKECLOSBVL:2 "  This compiler phase performs closure conversion.  This phase puts" CAKECLOSBVL:3 "  all of the code into a table of first-order, closed, multi-argument" functions. The created entities occupy a reserved numeric band whose width is computed from one parameter, CAKECLOSBVL:262 "    (* generic apps *)         max_app" plus the partial-application wrappers plus two, and CAKECLOSBVL:26 "  The num_stubs function gives the total number of these functions." **The pre-pass entities are not renamed; they are relocated by a constant injection**, CAKECLOSBVL:606 "      MAP2 (λ(loc,args,_) exp. (loc + num_stubs max_app, args, exp))". **The attribution is a total function over the post-pass locations, and every no-origin case has a named arm.** CAKECLOSBVL:863 "  make_name_alist nums prog nstubs dec_start (dec_length:num) =" maps over the post-pass location list and discriminates CAKECLOSBVL:865 "      fromAList(MAP(λn.(n, if n < nstubs then" with three constants for the created band, CAKECLOSBVL:866 "                             if n = nstubs-1 then implode \"bvl_init\" else" CAKECLOSBVL:867 "                             if n = nstubs-2 then implode \"bvl_force\" else" CAKECLOSBVL:868 "                                                  implode \"bvl_stub\"", then recovers the origin arithmetically, CAKECLOSBVL:869 "                           else let clos_name = n - nstubs in", gives the top-level declaration band its own arm, CAKECLOSBVL:871 "                             then implode \"dec\" else", looks the rest up, CAKECLOSBVL:872 "                               case lookup clos_name src_names of", and spells the failure, CAKECLOSBVL:873 "                               | NONE => implode \"unknown_clos_fun\"". **The discriminant is a band comparison on a number and the string is the rendered output**, which is the opposite of a `Str` standing for a relation. The map leaves the pass beside the program, CAKECLOSBVL:890 "    let func_names = make_name_alist (MAP FST prog') prog n". **⚑ AND IT IS THREADED THROUGH THE WHOLE BACKEND, WHICH IS R6's PRESCRIPTION SHIPPED.** CAKEBACKEND:52 "    let (c',p,names) = clos_to_bvl$compile c.clos_conf p in" starts it, the next pass takes it in and hands it back out, CAKEBACKEND:55 "    let (s,p,l,bl,n1,n2,n3,names) = bvl_to_bvi$compile c.clos_conf.start c.bvl_conf names p in", four later passes union their own created-entity names onto it, CAKEBACKEND:65 "    let names = sptree$union (sptree$fromAList $ (data_to_word$stub_names () ++" CAKEBACKEND:66 "      word_to_stack$stub_names () ++ stack_alloc$stub_names () ++" CAKEBACKEND:67 "      stack_remove$stub_names ())) names in", and it is consumed against the artifact, CAKEBACKEND:77 "    let res = attach_bitmaps names c bm" whose body is CAKEBACKEND:38 "                  ; symbols := MAP (\\(n,p,l). (lookup_any n names «NOTFOUND»,p,l)) c'.sec_pos_len". **The final domain is the emitted sections and the total function has a spelled default.** FD-21 item 10 found no surveyed system carrying a mapping produced by the creating pass and carried onward; this is one, and it is not a rename mapping, it is a name map keyed on post-pass locations. **The cost half of P2 goes the other way and is worth naming.** The clock is the fuel, CAKEICFP16:294 "tionsuseaclock,whichactsasfuelforthecomputation,toensure" termination, and the created entities spend it: CAKEICFP16:484 "Insomeproofs,extrafuelneedstobeaddedtotheclockin" state1 CAKEICFP16:487 "becausethecompiledcodeuses(aconstantnumberof)" CAKEICFP16:488 "extraticks.Fuelisusedforevaluationofthecompiledcode;the" compiler itself is proved to always terminate, and that extra fuel is CAKEICFP16:490 "usuallyexistentiallyquanti" ed. **So CakeML names every created entity and declines to account its cost to anything**, which is the same refusal to fold seen in item 1 and items 3 to 5. The created set stays bounded because it is generated rather than discovered: the library code is CAKEICFP16:1673 "codefortheselibraryfunctionsisgeneratedfrom" veri CAKEICFP16:1674 "edgeneratorfunctions;givenamaximumnumberofargu-" CAKEICFP16:1675 "mentsthatcanbepassedinonego,thesegeneratorfunctionswill" CAKEICFP16:1676 "generatealloftherequiredlibraryfunctions." **What the commitment forced: the post-pass domain, arms for the created band, an arm for the unknown, and one map carried by every pass that creates.**

  **3. P3's HOLDER IS FUCHSIA, AND ITS COMMITMENT FORCED AN ABSTRACT ROOT THAT MAY HAVE ZERO INSTANCES.** The case is stated as the reason collections exist: dynamic children support uses FUCHSIAREALM:5881 "where the existence or cardinality of component instances cannot be determined" FUCHSIAREALM:5882 "in advance. For example, a testing realm might declare a collection in which" test component instances can be created. **The total routing claim does not widen to instances, and the refusal is stated as a property of the design rather than as a missing feature.** FUCHSIAREALM:5890 "Capabilities cannot be statically routed from dynamic instances. This is an" FUCHSIAREALM:5891 "inherent restriction: there&#39;s no way to statically declare a route from a" FUCHSIAREALM:5892 "capability exposed by a dynamic instance. However, certain capabilities can be" FUCHSIAREALM:5893 "routed from the collection as a whole." **The direction of the claim decides.** Inbound, the declared container distributes: when an offer targets a collection, FUCHSIAREALM:5916 "the offered capability is made available to every instance in the collection." Outbound, only an aggregate exists, FUCHSIAREALM:5918 "an aggregation over the corresponding capabilities exposed by the instances in" the collection. **This is (ii)'s "abstract root retained with zero instances" arrived at from a capability commitment instead of a debugger's**, and it adds an asymmetry DWARF does not state: the created entity is addressable as a member of a declared band and never by its own identity, and the claim that ranges over it is an aggregate. **What the commitment forced: a declared entity standing for a set that may be empty, and an explicit statement of which direction of claim cannot be made about a created member.**

  **4. P4's HOLDER IS RUST, AND ITS COMMITMENT DID NOT FORCE THE DOMAIN TO MOVE, WHICH IS THE ONE COUNTERWEIGHT IN THE RUN.** The safe-by-default shape is the commitment, RUSTUNSAFETY:5 "Unsafe operations are those that can potentially violate the memory-safety guarantees of Rust's static semantics." over a closed enumeration, RUSTUNSAFETY:8 "The following language level features cannot be used in the safe subset of Rust:", with the unused opt-in itself diagnosed, RUSTUNSAFECK:5 "The compiler will also warn if an unsafe block is used without any corresponding unsafe operations." **The transformation creates entities**, RUSTMONO:16 "Rust takes a different approach: it _monomorphizes_ all generic types." RUSTMONO:17 "This means that compiler stamps out a different copy of the code of a generic" function for each concrete type needed, and the post-pass domain is computed by a total collection, RUSTMONO:29 "First, we need to figure out what concrete types we need for all the generic things in our program." producing RUSTMONO:44 "The monomorphization collector will give you a list of `[main, banana," `peach::<u64>]`, and RUSTMONO:46 "These are the functions that will have machine code generated for them." **The safety claim stays behind that.** The check walks a pre-codegen tree, RUSTUNSAFECK:10 "It performs a walk over the [THIR] of a function and all of its closures and inline constants.", and the three IRs it could have used are all pre-codegen, RUSTUNSAFECK:16 "The unsafety check needs type information, so could potentially be done on the" RUSTUNSAFECK:17 "HIR, making use of typeck results, THIR or MIR." while RUSTMONO:53 "The monomorphization collector is run just before MIR lowering and codegen." **The created copies are never re-checked, and that is sound because a copy's safety follows from its generic item's.** ⚑ **Rust nonetheless names its created entities, and its stated reason is E184's defect verbatim.** The v0 scheme's motivation is RUSTMANGLE:29 "- Information about generic parameters and other things is lost in the" mangling process. One cannot extract the type arguments of a RUSTMANGLE:31 "  monomorphized function from its symbol name." The fix is a decodable identity, RUSTMANGLE:244 "Each monomorphization of a generic function has its own symbol name." RUSTMANGLE:245 "The monomorphizations are disambiguated by the list of concrete generic" arguments. **And it had to grow again because the same created entity can be created more than once**: RUSTMANGLE:294 "The compiler may produce \"crate-local\" copies of a monomorphization." so RUSTMANGLE:299 "names for monomorphizations must include the id of the crate they are" instantiated for. **What the commitment forced: nothing about the domain, and a third mechanism for the origin relation beside DWARF's field and CakeML's arithmetic injection, namely a structured identity that decodes back to its origin.**

  **5. P5's THREE HOLDERS ALL REFUSE THE CASE, AND THEY REFUSE IT THREE DIFFERENT WAYS.** ⚑ **Necula makes the function boundary itself the observable, so a pass that moves it cannot be stated in his criterion at all.** TVPLDI:455 "servable behaviors where the set of observable events are" TVPLDI:456 "function calls and returns. This equivalence criterion is in-" TVPLDI:457 "tended to cover all intraprocedural program transformations," such as those performed by the GNU C compiler, and the incompleteness is owned, TVPLDI:1085 "on intraprocedural optimizations." **Alive2 refuses by warning.** ALIVE2:29 "Alive2 does not support inter-procedural transformations. Alive2 may produce" ALIVE2:30 "spurious counterexamples if run with such passes." Its own driver is constructed to keep the entity set fixed, ALIVE2:245 "without any interprocedural passes, and then attempt to validate the" translation, which the paper states as the scope of the tool, ALIVE2PLDI:1095 "validateeveryintra-proceduralIR-leveltransformationthatthecompilerperforms". **seL4 refuses by normalising, which is item 1.** **The nearest thing to an occupant is Pnueli's group claiming structure-modifying reach**, VOC:262 "optimizations. In contrast, our work can also be applied to structure-modifying" optimizations, and this run did not find a creating pass among the ones they treat, so that line is recorded and not leaned on. **So: when the two sides of an agreement disagree about which entities exist, the published answer is to change the question.** Two constructions shrink the transformation until the entity set is invariant, one shrinks the artifact until it matches the domain, and none widens the domain. FD-21 item 11's gap does not close on this axis either; it moves.

  **6. P6 DOES NOT EXIST AND THIS RUN DOES NOT INVENT ONE.** `PRINCIPLES.md` carries five principles. The candidate sixth is named and refused in the same sentence: *"Govern the mediator, resolved structurally not as a principle"*, and *"The answer is not a sixth principle that says verify the checker, and it is not a quorum"* (`PRINCIPLES.md:250-252`). Its content is the LCF discipline, a small trusted core re-checking untrusted producers' certificates, and its entity-set question is whether a certificate names the entities it certifies. Items 2 and 5 already answer that from CakeML and Alive2, so this slot yields no separate holder and nothing was searched for one.

  **7. WHAT TRANSFERS TO DECISION 1, AND IT DOES NOT PICK.** **The discriminator the five pieces disagree over is whether the created entity's property follows from its origin's, and this tree has already measured its own answer.** `outline` (`lib/lowering/upper/lower.chiral:311-320`, re-read at HEAD `cf2e674`) builds `<name>$<ncase>` and `compile-back.chiral:272` adopts the extras into the accumulator on the `le-ok` arm alone, while the same call site's `le-skip` arm at `:270-271` files the parent as a refusal, so a created extra and its parent do not share a fate. R1's ⚑ records the same for `alloc-growing`, which is skipped at `compile-fn` and is the source of a `specialized-into` relation, so it takes two arms under the pre-pass domain and one under the post-pass domain. `lift-lifted` (`lib/lowering/upper/specialize-singleton.chiral:195`, re-read at HEAD) builds `<gname>$<i>` and `prune-live` (`:227`) keeps the origin whenever anything still refers to it. **Rust's precondition therefore fails here and (i) stays unoccupied, now from five further directions.** **The discriminator does not separate (ii) from (iii)**, because both put every created name in the domain and they differ only on whether a deleted pre-pass name stays. No piece read in this run holds a commitment that bears on deletion, and that is the honest limit of what commitment-first buys over FD-21 and FD-22's job-first survey. **What is new for the ruling is item 2**: FD-21 found LLVM the only occupant of R1's total closed sum and its granularity is one instruction; CakeML is a second occupant at definition granularity, over the emitted artifact, in a verified compiler, with R6's threaded map and R7's artifact-side fold in the same construction.

  **8. WHAT TRANSFERS NOTHING, SAID PLAINLY RATHER THAN STRETCHED.** **P1's holder transfers a warning and no shape.** seL4's move is to delete the created entity from the artifact side, and E184's conformance row 4 pins every emitted ELF byte-identical (`docs/examples/E184-def-fate-sum.md` §6), so this tree cannot change what the other side of its claim contains. seL4's answer needs a freedom E184 does not have, and what it leaves behind is the price tag: a fixed pre-pass domain costs one unchecked step at exactly the disagreement. **P4's holder transfers nothing to the domain choice** for the reason in item 7. What it does transfer is a mechanism question the tree owes itself and this row does not rule: `<gname>$<i>` and `<name>$<ncase>` are already Rust's structured-identity route done without a grammar, and RUSTMANGLE:29-31 is the defect that route develops when the grammar is missing. E184's R2 forbids a `Str` standing for a relation, and a mangled symbol is a string with a grammar and a demangler, so whether the route is what R2 forbids or what R2 wants depends on whether the grammar is in the type. **Author.** **P5 transfers a negative and it is a real result.** **P6 transfers nothing because it does not exist.**

  **9. WHETHER THE PIECES AGREE. ONE UNANIMOUS RESULT, ONE CLEAN SPLIT, AND THE SPLIT HAS A NAMED CAUSE.** Unanimous: no holder folds a created entity's account into its origin's. CakeML gives the stub band its own arms rather than charging them to a source function and puts its extra ticks in an existential; Fuchsia refuses to route a dynamic instance's capability through its collection's declaration and offers an aggregate instead; Rust gives each monomorphization its own symbol and then adds the instantiating crate rather than collapsing duplicates; seL4 and Alive2 remove the created entity from the claim rather than attribute it. Split: on whether the domain widens, three widen it (CakeML fully, Fuchsia to a declared container, DWARF already in FD-21), one keeps it (Rust), one narrows the artifact (seL4). Cause of the split: whether the attributed property is determined by the origin's. **That is the finding the job-first surveys could not produce, because six systems doing the same job all had the same answer to that question and five systems holding different commitments do not.**

RAML and Granule were listed as P2 candidates and CakeML answered both halves of P2, so no resource-bound system was opened and the claim in item 2 about cost is CakeML's alone. Effect handlers compiled away were listed for P3 and not reached. **What no source in this run settles**: nothing read here bears on whether a pre-pass definition the pass deleted should keep a fate, which is the only axis on which (ii) and (iii) differ, so the ruling rests on FD-21's and FD-22's pricing for that axis and on this row for everything else.
