## The width of a refusal report

### FD-24 no surveyed report type mirrors its source language's constructor set, and every mirror that is mechanically checked is either inside one module or generated from one table both tiers read

- state:    OPEN
- claim:    The question, for E184's decision 2: when a checker or a compiler pass must report which syntactic form it refused, does the published practice mirror the source language's constructor set in the report type, or classify into a smaller set of causes, and what does each choice cost when the source language gains a constructor? **Eleven systems and two papers were read at their own sources, twenty pins. The practice CONVERGES on one result and SPLITS on a second, and the two results answer different halves of the fork.** **The convergent result: not one surveyed report type mirrors its source language's constructor set.** GHC's `HsSyn` carries 321 data constructors and its `TcRnMessage` carries 251, in no correspondence; Go's `go/ast` has 53 node types and its type checker 145 error codes; rustc's const evaluator refuses the whole of MIR through a 7-arm sum naming no MIR form; Cranelift's entire code generator reports through 6 arms of which one is a `Str`; CompCert's verified pipeline reports through 3, none of which names a form. **The one published rule for choosing the width is Go's, and it is keyed on the checker's own refusal sites rather than on the language's grammar**: two errors are one code if a theoretical refactoring of the checker could emit them from exactly one place. **The divergent result is about a mirror the compiler cannot check against its original, which is this tree's actual position, and the four published answers divide on one fact: whether one table can be read from both tiers.** Where it can, the correspondence is GENERATED and the day the source grows is a compile error at the mapping function rather than at either declaration (LLVM's `Instruction.def` textually included into both directions of the C-API opcode mapping; Clang's `tblgen` and its `%enum_select`). Where it cannot, every surveyed system gives up the compile error: Rust's `#[non_exhaustive]` mandates a wildcard outside the defining crate and keeps exhaustiveness inside it, Java's `javax.lang.model` mandates `visitUnknown` and a runtime `UnknownElementException`, Go's `ast.Walk` panics in its default arm, and GHC's own hand-maintained `HsSyn`-against-`TH.Syntax` mirror is described by the authors of both as very difficult to keep in sync. **So exhaustiveness as the enforcement mechanism survives a module boundary and does not survive a tier boundary, in any system read here.** **Applied to E184: the sixteen-arm shape has no occupant in the survey as a REPORT type, and the enforcement it relies on is available in this tree only at the PRODUCER, in `compile-front.chiral`, where `Term` is visible and where the kernel's own exhaustiveness check applies. The two sites that would carry it are the two that today refuse through `_`.** The ruling stays the author's and this row rules nothing.
- measured: **1. NO SURVEYED REPORT TYPE MIRRORS ITS SOURCE LANGUAGE'S CONSTRUCTOR SET. THE COUNTS, SIDE BY SIDE, FROM THE PINS.** **GHC.** The abstract syntax is TREESGROW:126 "nofewerthan97distinctdatatypeswithatotalof321data" constructors. The typechecker's message type is `TcRnMessage`, GHCTCRNTY:325 to :4521, and 251 lines in that span match the GADT-constructor form `^  [A-Z][A-Za-z0-9_']* ::`. The two sets are unrelated, and the stated axis is per diagnostic: GHCERRWIKI:210 "The idea is to have one data constructor per error, so that a IDE using the GHC API would not have to parse strings to understand the errors." **The named consumer is an IDE reading the GHC API**, and the migration that produced the type is GHCTCRNTY:261 "As part of #18516, we are slowly migrating the diagnostic messages emitted" GHCTCRNTY:262 "and reported in the TcM from SDoc to TcRnMessage." **Go.** `ast.Walk`'s type switch carries 53 `case *X:` arms over the syntax tree; `internal/types/errors` carries 145 bare enumerator lines. **rustc's const evaluator.** RUSTCTFEERR:688 "pub enum UnsupportedOpInfo {" has 7 arms, and the forms it refuses are named by cause (unsized local, extern type field, partial pointer read, thread-local static, extern static) with no MIR statement, rvalue or terminator constructor among them. **Cranelift.** CRANEERR carries 6 arms for the whole code generator and the refused-form case is CRANEERR:32 "    /// Something is not supported by the code generator. This might be an indication that a" feature is used without explicitly enabling it, spelled CRANEERR:35 "    Unsupported(String)," which is a `Str` encoding a which-of-N, the shape [[definitions/pattern-boundary-sums]] forbids. **CompCert.** COMPCERTERR:32 "Inductive errcode: Type :=" has three arms, `MSG` of `string`, `CTX` and `POS`, and the verified compiler's theorem does not reach them. **Hazel's marked lambda calculus.** Marks are per failure case of a typing rule: MARKEDLAM:288 "mark forms corresponds to a di" MARKEDLAM:288 "rent kind of error message that might be shown by an editoror emitted by a compiler." and MARKEDLAM:288 "when the core is extended," new marks may be needed. **Six independent report types, and the axis is the refusal site in every one of them.**

  **2. THE ONE PUBLISHED RULE FOR CHOOSING THE WIDTH, STATED AS A RULE, AND IT IS DECIDABLE AGAINST THE CODE AS WRITTEN.** GOERRCODES:17 "// Error codes should be fine-grained enough that the exact nature of the error" GOERRCODES:19 "// implementation detail of the type checking algorithm. As a rule-of-thumb," errors should be considered equivalent if there is a theoretical refactoring GOERRCODES:21 "// of the type checker in which they are emitted in exactly one place." **The rule ranges over emission sites and never over the grammar.** Its stability clause is GOERRCODES:15 "// Error code values should not be changed: add new codes at the end." Applied to E184 decision 2 the rule reads directly: an arm exists for each refusal the code can emit from a place no refactoring could merge, which is a count of sites and not a count of `Term` heads. **This run does not perform that count for this tree, because performing it is the ruling.**

  **3. GHC'S NEAREST THING TO A MIRRORED SUM IS ITS CODE TABLE, IT LIVES IN A DIFFERENT MODULE FROM EVERY CONSTRUCTOR IT NAMES, AND THE CHECK IS NOT EXHAUSTIVENESS.** `GHC.Types.Error.Codes` carries 725 equations matching `^  GhcDiagnosticCode "`, one per constructor name across `PsMessage`, `TcRnMessage`, `DsMessage` and `DriverMessage`. The instruction is GHCERRCODES:70 "  After adding a constructor to a diagnostic datatype, such as PsMessage," add a type family equation. **The correspondence is enforced by reflecting the constructor's NAME into a type-level symbol through `GHC.Generics`**, GHCERRCODES:1318 "      => GDiagnosticCode namespace (M1 i ('MetaCons con x y) f) where", and a missing equation is a custom type error, GHCERRCODES:1456 "        (     'Text \"Missing \" ':<>: 'ShowType namespace ':<>: Text \" diagnostic code for constructor \"". Uniqueness is a second type-level device, GHCERRCODES:57 "  - uniqueness of diagnostic codes is ensured by the use of an injective type family,". **The price is stated and it is permanent**: GHCERRCODES:59 "  - a diagnostic code never gets deleted from the GhcDiagnosticCode type family" even when it is no longer used, and GHCERRCODES:106 "  Never remove a return value from the 'GhcDiagnosticCode' type family!". 32 of the 725 equations are already wrapped in `Outdated`. **So the one surveyed mirror that IS checked across a module boundary buys the check with a metaprogramming apparatus and pays for it with a table that only grows.**

  **4. THE MIRROR THAT CANNOT SEE ITS SOURCE. FOUR PUBLISHED ANSWERS, AND THEY DIVIDE ON WHETHER ONE TABLE IS READABLE FROM BOTH TIERS.**
  **(a) GENERATE THE MAPPING FROM THE SOURCE'S OWN TABLE. LLVM, and this is the closest published match to this tree's position.** `Instruction.def` is LLVMINSTDEF:10 "used as a central place for enumerating the different instructions and" should eventually be the place to put comments about them, and it is a macro table with LLVMINSTDEF:15 "// NOTE: NO INCLUDE GUARD DESIRED!". It carries 69 `HANDLE_*_INST` rows. The mirror is `LLVMOpcode` in the stable C API, 69 enumerators, and its contract forbids the maintenance move a sum would allow: LLVMCAPI:59 "/// External users depend on the following values being stable. It is not safe" to reorder them. **Nothing hand-reconciles the two lists. Both directions of the mapping textually include the source table**: LLVMCOREC:1796 "#define HANDLE_INST(num, opc, clas) case num: return LLVM##opc;" and LLVMCOREC:1805 "#define HANDLE_INST(num, opc, clas) case LLVM##opc: return num;". A new row in the table therefore names `LLVM##opc`, which does not exist until someone adds it, so the day the instruction set grows is a compile error at the MAPPING FUNCTION and at neither declaration. The runtime residue is LLVMCOREC:1795 "      default: llvm_unreachable(\"Unhandled Opcode.\");". **The cost of the stable half is visible in the mirror's own text**: LLVMCAPI:64 "  /* removed 2 due to API changes */" leaves a hole, and thirteen later opcodes were appended out of source order, LLVMCAPI:65 "  LLVMUncondBr       = 70,". **Clang is the same answer at diagnostic granularity**: CLANGINTERNALS:72 "using it. From this file, {program}`tblgen` generates the unique ID of the" diagnostic, its severity and its format string, and where a small which-of-N was previously a bare integer index into a message, the named enumeration is now generated too, CLANGINTERNALS:296 ": This format specifier is used exactly like a `select` specifier, except it" additionally generates a namespace, enumeration, and enumerator list based on the format string given.
  **(b) GIVE UP THE COMPILE ERROR AT THE BOUNDARY AND MANDATE A CATCH-ALL. Rust, Java and Go, three ecosystems, one answer.** Rust's rule is explicit about where exhaustiveness survives: RUSTNONEXH:286 "Within the crate that defines the enum, this attribute is essentially ignored," so the current crate can continue to exhaustively match, and RUSTNONEXH:314 "Outside the crate that defines the enum, users should be required to add a" wildcard arm to ensure forward compatibility. The purpose is stated as the whole point of the feature, RUSTNONEXH:12 "Adding this hint to enums will force downstream crates to add a wildcard arm to" RUSTNONEXH:13 "`match` statements, ensuring that adding new variants is not a breaking change." Java's compiler-API visitor says the same and adds a version stamp: JAVAVISITOR:62 "Visitor classes directly implementing this interface may be source" JAVAVISITOR:63 "incompatible with future versions of the platform. To avoid this" source incompatibility, extend the abstract visitor class; the catch-all is `visitUnknown`, JAVAVISITOR:219 "This can occur if the language evolves and new kinds" JAVAVISITOR:220 "of elements are added to the Element hierarchy.", and the failure is a runtime throw, JAVAVISITOR:227 "UnknownElementException - a visitor implementation may optionally throw this exception". Go has no exhaustiveness check at all and its syntax walker ends its type switch with GOASTWALK:352 "		panic(fmt.Sprintf(\"ast.Walk: unexpected node type %T\", n))". **Three systems, three catch-alls, and in two of the three the residue is a runtime failure rather than a compile-time one.**
  **(c) HAND-MAINTAIN IT AND STATE THAT IT DRIFTS. GHC against Template Haskell, by the authors of both.** TREESGROW:68 "thatcrossesseveralcompilerphases;andasecondentiredata" type `TH.Syntax`, and TREESGROW:76 "datatypesarelarge(dozensoftypes,hundredsofconstructors)andarevery" TREESGROW:77 "culttokeepinsync." **That is the duplication objection stated by people who paid it, about a mirror of an AST living in a package that cannot be checked against it.** The two options they name before proposing a third are the fork exactly: TREESGROW:61 "anewdatatyperepresentingtheoutputdecoratedtree,atthecostofmuch" duplication, or TREESGROW:62 "duplication.Orshecanwriteasingledatatypewithallthenecessary" fields and constructors, TREESGROW:63 "andconstructors,atthecostofhavingmanyunused" fields and constructors at different stages of compilation. **Their answer is to stop mirroring**, which requires the mirror and the source to be one parameterised type, and the idiom is shipped in GHC: GHCHSEXT:4 "{-# LANGUAGE UndecidableInstances    #-} -- Wrinkle in Note [Trees That Grow]". Its own stated cost includes the mechanism the sixteen-arm option is bought for: TREESGROW:2697 "Ouruseofpatternsynonyms(whichisoptional,of" TREESGROW:2698 "currentpattern-matchexhaustivenesschecker.And" TREESGROW:2699 "evenifwedidnotuseapatternsynonym,theextraconstructor(" will be flagged as unmatched even when unused. **Wadler's framing is the general statement**: EXPRPROBLEM:41 "to define a datatype by cases, where one can add new cases to the" datatype and new functions over it, without recompiling existing code, and while retaining static type safety.
  **(d) CARRY A STATED TOTALITY THEOREM INSTEAD OF A COMPILER CHECK. Hazel.** MARKEDLAM:353 "Theorem 2.1 (Marking Totality)." and the recipe for extending the calculus is MARKEDLAM:3758 "by considering each possible failure case, one may" systema MARKEDLAM:3758 "tically derive the necessary error marks and marking rules. The metatheorems, particularly" MARKEDLAM:3758 "totality, ensure that no rules or premises have been missed." **The instrument that forces the update when the language grows is a proof obligation carried in the metatheory, and the mark set is derived from the FAILURE CASES rather than from the term formers.** **GHC ships a fourth device for the same job that is worth naming, because it removes a wildcard without adding an arm**: a mirrored constructor that cannot occur in a given phase is typed with an empty type, and the coverage checker then proves the arm unreachable. GHCHSEXT:110 "The field of type `XXHsDecl p` is strict for a good reason: it allows the" GHCHSEXT:111 "pattern-match coverage checker to conclude that any matches against XHsDecl" are unreachable, because GHCHSEXT:121 "is an empty data type, there is no possible way to reach the right-hand side" of the case.

  **5. WHAT IT COSTS WHEN THE SOURCE GAINS A CONSTRUCTOR, MEASURED WHERE THE COST IS STATED RATHER THAN INFERRED.** **Clang publishes the checklist and publishes the failure mode for skipping a step.** Adding one expression node begins at CLANGINTERNALS:3312 "   the node in `include/clang/Basic/StmtNodes.td` and creating a new class for your" expression, and continues through printing, profiling and serialization support in three further hand-edited files; the manual states what a missed step produces, CLANGINTERNALS:3330 "     your expression. Omitting this step will lead to hard-to-diagnose" failures regarding matching of template declarations. **That is the silent gap, in the largest surveyed compiler, at exactly the seam the sixteen-arm option exists to close, and nothing in that toolchain catches it.** **GHC's own migration to one constructor per diagnostic is unfinished and the incompleteness is representable in the type**: `diagnosticCode` returns a `Maybe`, GHCERRTYPE:275 "  -- some errors in GHC still use the old system of just writing the" GHCERRTYPE:276 "  -- error message in-place (instead of using a dedicated error type" GHCERRTYPE:277 "  -- and constructor), we do not have error codes for all errors." **GHC also keeps a real catch-all arm in the sum and polices it with a comment rather than with the type**: GHCTCRNTY:329 "  TcRnUnknownMessage :: UnknownDiagnosticFor TcRnMessage -> TcRnMessage", carrying GHCTCRNTY:318 "  -- Please don't use this function inside the GHC codebase;" and GHCTCRNTY:322 "you should add a new constructor to 'TcRnMessage' instead." **Rust's structured-diagnostic refactor produced no closed sum at all.** RUSTDIAGSTRUCT:33 "`Diagnostic` can only be derived on structs and enums." and a diagnostic is one derived struct per error, keyed to a code, RUSTDIAG:76 "Most errors have an associated error code." **A new syntactic form in Rust breaks no diagnostic match, because there is no match to break.** **And GCC answers the refused-form question inside the AST rather than in a report**: GCCTREEDEF:36 "/* Any erroneous construct is parsed into a node of this type." GCCTREEDEF:37 "   This type of node is accepted without complaint in all contexts" GCCTREEDEF:38 "   by later parsing activities, to avoid multiple error messages" for one error, which is Hazel's mark with one arm rather than many.

  **6. THIS TREE'S COORDINATES, VERIFIED AT HEAD `4c12958` RATHER THAN TAKEN FROM THE PROMPT.** **The `diag.chiral` precedent is real and it is NOT a mirror of `Term`.** `lib/typing/diag.chiral:324` reads "NO `_` arm, on purpose: a new Reason constructor must break every renderer" loudly, and the sum it guards is `Reason` with 10 arms; the two codes beside it are `Subject` with 12 arms and `Judg` with 36. Not one of the 58 is per-`Term`-constructor. The file's own stated rule is `lib/typing/diag.chiral:120`, one constructor per evidence shape. **The sum, its 30 `dg-` renderers and every `case` that must break are in ONE file**, so the exhaustiveness that enforces it is a within-module mechanism, which is the only place any surveyed system gets it for free. **The refusal set.** `Term` has 16 constructors at `lib/surface/syntax.chiral:18-34`. `term->ntalty` names 5 (`t-primty`, `t-tcon`, `t-refine`, `t-var`, `t-pi`) and refuses the other 11 through `_` at `lib/lowering/compile-front.chiral:72`; `term->ncore` names 11 and refuses the five type formers through `_` at `:134`; `peel-def` is `:211-223` with four `(none)` arms. **`lib/lowering/skip-diag.chiral:8` reads `(import "prelude/prelude")` and there is no second import in the file**, so a sum declared there cannot mention `Term`. All five verified by reading. **This tree HAS the enforcement mechanism.** `all-covered?` gates a `case` with no default and refuses with `(r-judged (subj-none) (jg-nonexhaustive))` at `lib/typing/kernel.chiral:1319`, rendered at `diag.chiral:448`. **So exhaustiveness exists in this tree. What the fork turns on is where the correspondence would be checked.**

  **7. THE PUBLISHED POSITION THIS TREE IS IN IS LLVM's, ONE INSTRUMENT SHORT, AND THE SURVEY SAYS SO PRECISELY.** **No surveyed system checks a mirror at the mirror's DECLARATION.** LLVM does not: `LLVMOpcode` and `Instruction.def` are two declarations and neither checks the other. What LLVM checks is the MAPPING, and it checks it by generating the mapping's arms from the source's table. **`SkHead`'s declaration would sit in `skip-diag.chiral` where `Term` is invisible, and its producers would sit in `compile-front.chiral` where `Term` is visible and where the kernel's own exhaustiveness check applies.** That is the same split LLVM has, and the same place the check can live. **The instrument this tree lacks is the shared table**: LLVM's 69 arms and its 69 cases are one list read twice, and sixteen `SkHead` arms with sixteen `Term` cases would be two lists written twice, with only the producer's `case` forcing the second to keep up with the first. **And the exhaustiveness that would force it is disabled today at exactly the two sites that would carry it**, the `_` arms at `compile-front.chiral:72` and `:134`. A producer that keeps a `_` gets the sixteen arms and none of the enforcement they are bought for.

  **8. PRB-81 IS EVIDENCE ON THE OTHER SIDE OF THE FORK, AND THIS ROW SAYS SO RATHER THAN INHERITING THE FRAMING IT WAS GIVEN.** PRB-81 measures a report type that is too COARSE: `sk-extern` carries all 187 records where 5 are extern refusals, a 97% misattribution on that tag, and the three real body classes have no tag at all (`records/lenses/problems.md` PRB-81, read at HEAD and not re-derived). **That is the failure of under-classification.** Nothing measured in this tree is a mirrored sum drifting from the sum it copied. **The say-it-once tension the fork names is real and it is unmeasured here; the misattribution the three-arm option risks is measured here at 182 records.** Both halves of the fork therefore have evidence and the two halves come from different evidence classes, which is the honest statement of the price.

  **9. WHETHER THE PRACTICE CONVERGES OR SPLITS, SAID PLAINLY.** **It converges on the report type**: zero of six surveyed refusal reports mirror a source constructor set, and the one published width rule ranges over emission sites. **It splits on the mirror**, and the discriminator is mechanical rather than stylistic: a mirror inside one module is checked by exhaustiveness; a mirror across a boundary where one table is readable from both tiers is checked by generation; a mirror across a boundary where no such table exists goes unchecked, and all three systems in that position mandate a catch-all and accept a runtime failure. **The direct answer to the fork's last clause: in this survey the mirroring shape is defensible only where the correspondence is mechanically enforced, and it is a finding against sixteen arms at `SkHead`'s declaration site. It is NOT a finding against sixteen arms at the producers, because the producers are in the kernel image, they see `Term`, and the kernel checks exhaustiveness.** Whether that relocation is acceptable is the author's, and the relocation is what E184 decision 3 already puts in front of the author under a different name.

  **10. WHAT THE SOURCES DO NOT SETTLE.** No source read here states a width rule keyed on the source language's grammar, so the sixteen-arm shape has no published rule endorsing it and no published rule forbidding it; it has an absence of occupants. Nothing surveyed prices a hand-maintained mirror across a tier that cannot import its source, because every system that faces one either generates it, mandates a catch-all, or reports that it drifts without measuring the drift. Nothing surveyed measures the cost of a report arm that has never fired, which is E184 decision 7's question and stays open. Nothing surveyed states what a report arm should carry when the refused form is an INTERNAL recursion failure rather than a head, which is the case `term->ntalty` produces at its `t-tcon` and `t-refine` arms and which neither the three-arm nor the sixteen-arm spelling in `docs/examples/E184-def-fate-sum.md` §5 addresses.
  **WHAT WAS CONSULTED AND CAME BACK EMPTY.** The GHC wiki page for the structured-errors work is 856 lines and carries no cost, drawback, burden or granularity discussion: a case-insensitive grep for `drawback`, `downside`, `burden`, `cost`, `granularity` and `proliferat` over `GHCERRWIKI` returns zero lines. The cost of that design is stated in the source instead, in `GHCERRCODES` and `GHCERRTYPE`, and is quoted in items 3 and 5. `clang/docs/InternalsManual.rst` returns HTTP 404 at `main` because the Clang docs moved to Markdown; the `.md` path was pinned, matching FD-21's experience with the LLVM docs. **WHAT WAS NOT OPENED.** No attribute-grammar treatment was read. The visitor-pattern leg was taken from a shipped API contract (`javax.lang.model.element.ElementVisitor`) rather than from the pattern literature, because the contract states the boundary rule and the literature would have to be read for it.

It leaves two things in the author's hands that the survey sharpens rather than settles: whether the correspondence check may move from the sum's declaration to its producers in `compile-front.chiral`, which is where every surveyed system puts it, and whether two hand-written lists are acceptable where every surveyed checked mirror generates one from the other.

## Where the exhaustiveness obligation sits

### FD-25 the second walk has one surveyed occupant, its two walks are published as disagreeing, and every other surveyed system has the producer leave evidence the classifier only reads

- state:    OPEN
- claim:    The question, for E184's decisions 2 and 3 taken together: when a checker's classifier and the pass it classifies live at different tiers, where does the exhaustiveness obligation belong, and what does each placement cost when the classified language grows a form? **Five systems were read at their own sources, twelve new pins and one re-read. The two placements E184 states are the ends of a three-position spectrum and the surveyed weight sits in the middle.** **The pure second walk, where a classifier re-derives from the same input what the producer already decided, has exactly ONE surveyed occupant: CPython's PEG parser. Its two walks disagree, the implementers say so in their own documentation with a worked example, and the shipped code carries a repair that throws the second walk's answer away and keeps the first walk's.** **At the other end, GHC did retype its producers, and the price is published as prose and as a date: the authors call a single change "highly impractical" for touching a lot of modules, the tracking issue is open since 2020-07-30, and the item that converts the typechecker's own error types is still unticked.** **Between them, two systems put a value at the producer and have the classifier read it: rustc's `ErrorGuaranteed` is a coarse token whose page states it carries no class at all, so nothing a classifier says can contradict it, and GHC's `CtOrigin` is 64 arms of provenance attached where the check happens so the classifier has nothing left to derive.** **The blast radius of placement one in this tree was measured here and nothing recorded it before: 27 call sites, all 27 in `lib/lowering/compile-front.chiral`, spread over 12 enclosing functions, with 3 sites originating a refusal, 19 propagating one, and 3 sinks discarding it.** The ruling stays the author's and this row rules nothing.
- measured: **1. THE BLAST RADIUS, MEASURED AT HEAD `bc8f4e2` BY GREP OVER `lib/ prog/ tools/ bin/`, AND IT IS ONE FILE.** Call sites in call position, per function: `term->ntalty` **5** (`compile-front.chiral:69` its own recursion, `:76` in `term->ntalty-list`, `:213` in `peel-def`, `:243` in `field-tys->n`, `:331` in `prim->n`); `term->ntalty-list` **4** (`:68`, `:78`, `:219`, `:329`); `term->ncore` **12** (`:114`, `:115`, `:123`, `:128`, `:129`, `:131` its own recursion, `:141` in `tnc-keep`, `:146` in `term->ncore-list`, `:151` in `term->narms`, `:156` in `term->ndflt`, `:216` and `:221` in `peel-def`); `prim->n` **1** (`:336` in `prims->n`); `field-tys->n` **2** (`:245`, `:251` in `ctors->n`); `tnc-keep` **3** (`:125`, `:140`, `:143`). **Total 27, and 27 of 27 are inside `lib/lowering/compile-front.chiral`, which is inside `prog/compiler.prog`'s closure.** Nothing outside that file calls any of the six: the three other tree-wide hits are comments (`lib/lowering/upper/closconv.chiral:1004`, `:1030`, `:1101`) and one is a fixture string in a shell gate (`tools/test/capture-fields.sh:241`). **Eleven of the 27 are a function calling itself. Sixteen cross a function boundary.** **Twelve distinct enclosing functions would have to read the new shape**: the six named plus `term->ncore-list`, `term->narms`, `term->ndflt`, `peel-def`, `ctors->n` and `prims->n`. **The refusal ORIGINATES at three sites and propagates at nineteen.** The three originations are `compile-front.chiral:67` (a `t-primty` whose name is in neither the three-way table nor either porttype predicate), `:72` (the eleven heads `term->ntalty` does not name) and `:134` (the five type formers `term->ncore` does not name); the other nineteen `(none)` occurrences inside the six are `(none (none))` pass-throughs. **The refusal is ABSORBED at exactly three sinks, and every one of them drops a whole entity and continues**: `peel-globals` at `:232` `(none      (peel-globals em sp r))` drops a definition, `datas->n` at `:260` `(none      (datas->n rest))` drops a data type, `prims->n` at `:337` `(none (prims->n r))` drops a prim. **So placement one is 3 sites that must name a refusal, 19 that must thread it, 3 that must consume it, and 12 signatures, all in one file.** ⚑ `docs/examples/E184-def-fate-sum.md` §5 already carves `prim->n` out under **Deliberately omitted**, keeping its `Maybe` because the extern region stays E107's, so on that reading the count is 26 sites and 11 functions.

  **2. THE PURE SECOND WALK HAS ONE SURVEYED OCCUPANT, AND ITS TWO WALKS DISAGREE IN SHIPPED CODE.** CPython's PEG parser is decision 3's second option built and shipped. The first walk refuses and names nothing; a second walk over the same tokens runs a separate rule set whose only job is to classify. CPYPARSER:494 "  - Only if the first pass fails to parse, a second pass including the invalid" rules will be executed. The second walk is deliberately incapable of accepting: CPYPARSER:706 "   rules that start with an \`invalid_\` prefix. By design this attempt" CPYPARSER:707 "   **cannot succeed** and is only executed to give to the invalid rules a" chance to detect specific situations. In the code, `_PyPegen_run_parser` runs `_PyPegen_parse` twice with `p->call_invalid_rules` flipped between them: CPYPEGEN:1002 "_PyPegen_run_parser(Parser *p)", CPYPEGEN:1019 "       // Make a second parser pass. In this pass we activate heavier and slower checks" to produce better error messages, and CPYPEGEN:945 "    p->call_invalid_rules = 1;" in the reset. **THE DISAGREEMENT IS PUBLISHED WITH A WORKED EXAMPLE.** The documentation's own case is a `print` classifier rule against the input `print(something) $ 3`: CPYPARSER:751 "of the parser will fail (because of the \`$\`) and in the second phase, the rule will match the" `print(something)` and the error will be reported there instead of the `$` character. **The first walk refused at one place and the classifier named another, and the classifier won.** **THE REPAIR IS IN THE SHIPPED CODE AND IT DISTRUSTS THE SECOND WALK.** `_PyPegen_run_parser` captures the first walk's position before resetting, CPYPEGEN:1022 "        Token *last_token = p->tokens[p->fill - 1];", and the reconciler uses it: CPYPEGENERR:398 "    // Use the last token we found on the first pass to avoid reporting" CPYPEGENERR:399 "    // incorrect locations for generic syntax errors just because we reached" CPYPEGENERR:400 "    // further away when trying to find specific syntax errors in the second" pass, then CPYPEGENERR:402 "    RAISE_SYNTAX_ERROR_KNOWN_LOCATION(last_token, \"invalid syntax\");". The stated rule is the same in the doc: CPYPARSER:496 "  - If the parser fails in the second phase with a generic syntax error, the" CPYPARSER:497 "    location of the generic failure of the first pass will be used (this avoids" reporting incorrect locations due to the invalid rules. **THE CLASSIFIER ALSO PERTURBS THE PRODUCER, AND THE ONLY DEFENCE IS A REVIEW INSTRUCTION.** CPYPARSER:722 "> - Make sure the parser doesn't behave differently for regular rules when you introduce invalid rules". **This is decision 3's stated fear, measured, in a production compiler, by the people who built it.**

  **3. WHAT FORCES THE CLASSIFIER TO GROW WHEN THE LANGUAGE DOES, IN THAT SYSTEM: NOTHING, AND THE RATIO IS 70 AGAINST 209.** `Grammar/python.gram` is one file carrying both the grammar and its classifier, so this is a mirror INSIDE one module, which FD-24 measured as the one place exhaustiveness comes free. It gets no such check, because a PEG grammar has no coverage obligation. Counted on the pin, rule heads at column zero matching `^[a-z_0-9]+(\[...\])?( (memo))? *:`, deduplicated: **279 rules, of which 70 begin `invalid_` and 209 do not.** The convention is stated in the grammar's own header, CPYGRAM:36 "#     - These rules are NOT used in the first pass of the parser.". **A new ordinary rule creates no obligation on any `invalid_` rule and produces no diagnostic of any kind**: nothing in CPYPARSER or CPYGRAM states a check, and the residue when the classifier finds nothing is the single generic string at CPYPEGENERR:402. **The one occupant of the second-walk shape therefore also demonstrates its silent-gap failure mode**, which is the same residue FD-24 measured in Java's `visitUnknown` and Go's `ast.Walk` panic and which those two at least reach at runtime.

  **4. THE OTHER END: GHC RETYPED ITS PRODUCERS, AND THE PRICE IS PUBLISHED AS PROSE AND AS A DATE.** The construction moved to the producing sites and did not stay in a classifier. Every erroring primitive in the typechecker's monad now takes a `TcRnMessage` value where it took an `SDoc`: GHCTCMONAD:1344 "addErr :: TcRnMessage -> TcRn ()", GHCTCMONAD:1981 "failWithTc :: TcRnMessage -> TcM a               -- Add an error message and fail", GHCTCMONAD:1989 "checkTc :: Bool -> TcRnMessage -> TcM ()         -- Check that the boolean is true". `TcRnMessage` appears on 53 lines of that one module, 57 occurrences. **So every producer that refuses constructs its own refusal, which is E184 decision 3's first option at whole-compiler scale.** **The authors' own statement of the cost is the reason the work was split:** GHCERRWIKI:6 "As this strand of work touches a lot of modules, doing everything as a single gargantuan MR seems highly impractical." Their plan has thirteen boxes, nine ticked and four open, and one of the four open ones is the typechecker itself: GHCERRWIKI:63 "- [ ] Convert the \`TcRn\` error types to adhere to the new error-messages" infrastructure. Their own headline figure is GHCERRWIKI:3 "**Overall progress (estimated)**:" 85%. **The tracking issue was opened 2020-07-30 and was still open when this pin was taken**: GHCERRISSUE:1 "GHC spits out errors as SDocs, which makes it hard for clients of the GHC API to respond sensibly to them.", GHCERRISSUE:1 "\"created_at\":\"2020-07-30T08:25:45.905Z\"", GHCERRISSUE:1 "\"state\":\"opened\"". ⚑ **The scales are not comparable and this row does not compare them.** GHC's migration is a whole compiler's diagnostics across four subsystems; this tree's measured figure in item 1 is 27 call sites in one file. What transfers is the shape of the cost, which is that retyping a producer is paid at every site that reads it, and the count of those sites is the price.

  **5. GHC ALSO RUNS A SEPARATE CLASSIFIER, AND WHAT KEEPS ITS TWO WALKS FROM DISAGREEING IS THAT THE SECOND ONE DOES NOT DECIDE.** The constraint solver does not classify. It leaves residue, and a separate 3,013-line module walks the residue: GHCTCERRORS:154 "-- | Report unsolved goals as errors or warnings. We may also turn some into" GHCTCERRORS:156 "reportUnsolved :: WantedConstraints -> TcM (Bag EvBind)". **The second walk cannot disagree about WHY a constraint failed, because the producer attached that at the moment of the check and the classifier only reads it.** The provenance type has its own module, whose header states its purpose: GHCCTORIGIN:3 "-- | Describes the provenance of types as they flow through the type-checker." GHCCTORIGIN:4 "-- The datatypes here are mainly used for error message generation." `CtOrigin` carries **59 constructors**, counted on the pin at `GHCCTORIGIN:359-524`, one leading arm plus 58 lines matching `^  \|[ ]*[A-Z]` before the next top-level declaration. The threading is stated as the mechanism: GHCCTORIGIN:1055 "This information gets passed along as we make progress on solving the constraint," GHCCTORIGIN:1056 "and if we end up with an unsolved constraint we can report an informative error" message to the user using the origin. **The residue where the classifier meets a shape it has no arm for is a crash and not a compile error**, four sites in the classifier's own fall-throughs: GHCTCERRORS:1177 "  = pprPanic \"mkUserTypeError\" (ppr item)", GHCTCERRORS:1201 "        _ -> pprPanic \"mkImplicitLiftingError\" (ppr item)", GHCTCERRORS:1281 "       _ -> pprPanic \"mkSkolReporter\" (ppr item1 $$ ppr item2)", and a fourth over the enclosing implication at `:1211`. Nine `pprPanic` sites in the module in total.

  **6. RUSTC SPLITS THE DIFFERENCE AND STATES WHY THE TWO HALVES CANNOT DISAGREE: THE COARSE HALF CARRIES NO CLASS.** The producer returns a total value carrying a token, and the token is unforgeable: RUSTSPANLIB:2846 "pub struct ErrorGuaranteed(());", RUSTSPANLIB:2839 "/// Useful type to use with \`Result<>\` indicate that an error has already" been reported to the user, and RUSTSPANLIB:2842 "/// The \`()\` field is necessary: it is non-\`pub\`, which means values of this" type cannot be constructed outside of this crate. What it buys is stated: RUSTERRGUAR:7 "\`ErrorGuaranteed\` is a zero-sized type that is unconstructable outside of the" `rustc_errors` crate, so RUSTERRGUAR:11 "\`ErrorGuaranteed\`, the compilation is _statically guaranteed to fail_." **The answer to why a producer and a classifier here cannot disagree is that the producer says nothing a classifier could contradict**: RUSTERRGUAR:17 "* It does _not_ convey information about the _kind_ of error." **The type system carries the token and no class**: RUSTTYSTY:524 "    pub fn new_error(tcx: TyCtxt<'tcx>, guar: ErrorGuaranteed) -> Ty<'tcx> {" makes a refused type into a total `Ty`, so no call site changes shape. **The gap this leaves is closed by a conservation check at the end of the compile, and it is E184's R7 in another compiler.** A producer that has no token yet registers a promise: RUSTTYSTY:528 "    /// Constructs a \`TyKind::Error\` type and registers a \`span_delayed_bug\` to ensure it gets used." with the message RUSTTYSTY:531 "        Ty::new_error_with_message(tcx, DUMMY_SP, \"TyKind::Error constructed but no error reported\")", built by RUSTDCX:1078 "    pub fn span_delayed_bug(". At the end, an unredeemed promise is an internal compiler error: RUSTDCX:1479 "        let note1 = \"no errors encountered even though delayed bugs were created\";" RUSTDCX:1480 "        let note2 = \"those delayed bugs will now be shown as internal compiler errors\";", suppressed only when a real error did occur, RUSTDCX:1457 "            // If an error happened already. We shouldn't expose delayed bugs." **A refusal with no report is caught by a fold at the end of the compile, which is exactly what R7's `conserve` does over `FateRec`.**

  **7. THE THREE POSITIONS, AND WHERE EACH ONE PUTS THE OBLIGATION WHEN THE LANGUAGE GROWS A FORM.** **(a) Retype the producer.** The obligation is a compile error at the producer's `case`, which is where this tree's instrument lives (`lib/typing/kernel.chiral:1319`, `all-covered?` raising `(r-judged (subj-none) (jg-nonexhaustive))`, and it applies to a `case` with no default). Price is the count in item 1. Occupant: GHC's `TcRnMessage`. **(b) Re-walk on the refusal path.** The obligation is nowhere, and the drift is measured in item 2. Occupant: CPython, one, and it repairs the drift at runtime. **(c) The producer leaves a value the classifier reads.** The obligation splits by what the value carries. Carrying the full provenance (GHC's `CtOrigin`) puts the obligation back at the producer with the classifier's fall-throughs as the residue. Carrying one bit (rustc's `ErrorGuaranteed`) removes the obligation from both and replaces it with a whole-compile conservation check. **No surveyed system leaves a re-walking classifier as the sole account of a refusal.**

  **8. THIS TREE'S COORDINATES, AND E157'S RULE AS THE TREE STATES IT.** Verified by reading at HEAD `bc8f4e2`, which is one commit past the `4c12958` FD-24 read. `lib/lowering/compile-front.chiral:72` and `:134` are the two `(_ (none))` sites and they are the only two in the file; `term->ntalty` is declared `(-> Term (Maybe NTalTy))` at `:58` and defined `:60-72`; `lib/typing/kernel.chiral:1319` is `(false (tc-err (r-judged (subj-none) (jg-nonexhaustive))))` inside the `(none dflt)` branch, so the check reaches a `case` with no default and neither `:72` nor `:134` is subject to it today; `lib/lowering/skip-diag.chiral:8` is `(import "prelude/prelude")` and is the file's only import; `lib/lowering/compile-front.chiral:18` is `(import "surface/parse")`, which is where `Term` enters. **E157's rule, quoted from the tree and not paraphrased**: `lib/typing/diag.chiral:12-18` reads "THE TAXONOMY RULE (from the worked example, and it is checkable at review): ONE `Reason` constructor per EVIDENCE SHAPE, never per message. What a reason is ABOUT varies far more than what it CARRIES, so the varying part is a separate `Subject`, and the judgments whose only evidence IS their subject share one arm (`r-judged`) over a closed `Judg` code. `Judg` is a sum and not a Str precisely because it is which-of-N: adding a judgment is then a compile error in this file's renderer, never a silently-accepted new string." ⚑ **The rule's own enforcement clause names a renderer in the same file.** It says the compile error lands "in this file's renderer", which is a within-module check, and it is silent about a producer in another module. That is the clause E184 decision 3 is being asked to extend, and E157 does not already cover it.

  **9. WHETHER THE EVIDENCE IS LOPSIDED, SAID PLAINLY.** **Against option (b) as E184 spells it, the evidence is lopsided.** One occupant was found, its two walks disagree, the disagreement is documented by the implementers with a worked example, the shipped code carries a repair that discards the classifier's answer, and the same system shows the silent-gap failure at 70 classifier rules against 209 grammar rules. Nothing was found on the other side of it: no surveyed system re-derives a refusal on a second walk and reports that the two agreed. **Between (a) and (c) the evidence is balanced and the two are priced in different currencies.** (a) costs call sites, and this tree's count is 27 in one file, against GHC's unbounded count still unfinished after six years at a scale three orders larger. (c) costs the classification itself: rustc's coarse token is explicitly free of a class, so the which-of-N that `SkHead` exists to carry would have no home at the producer under that shape, and the check that replaces it is a fold at the end of the compile. **The fork E184 decision 3 states is therefore not the fork the practice ran into**, and the middle position is unstated in `docs/examples/E184-def-fate-sum.md` §5 and §6. **Whether decisions 2 and 3 are one ruling.** They are not the same question, and decision 3 determines whether decision 2 still has content. Under (a) with the `_` arms removed, the exhaustiveness check at `:72` and `:134` forces one arm per unnamed head and `SkHead`'s width follows from the ruling on 3. Under (c)-with-one-bit, `SkHead` never reaches the producer at all and its width is a free choice with no instrument, which is decision 2 unchanged. **So ruling 3 first collapses or restores 2, and ruling 2 first settles nothing about 3.** This row states that ordering and takes neither.

  **10. WHAT THE SOURCES DO NOT SETTLE, AND WHAT WAS NOT REACHED.** **No published call-site count was found for retyping a partial producer into a result-sum producer in a named codebase.** GHC's is the nearest and its scale is published only as prose and as an unticked box; a count of `failWithTc` and its siblings' call sites across GHC needs a checkout, which this run did not take, so item 1's number stands alone with no external figure beside it. **No defect-tracker instance of CPython's two walks disagreeing was sought.** The disagreement is stated by the implementers in their own documentation and repaired in `Parser/pegen_errors.c`, so the primary source already carries it and a bug report would add nothing the pins do not hold. **`ErrorGuaranteed`'s own page states no cost.** A case-insensitive grep for `cost`, `drawback`, `downside`, `burden` and `expensive` over `RUSTERRGUAR` returns zero lines across its 33 lines, and the same grep for `drawback`, `downside` and `burden` over `CPYPARSER` returns zero lines across its 888. **Nothing surveyed measures how often a coarse-token producer lost a classification a consumer wanted**, which is the question that would price (c) against (a) directly, and it stays open. **Item 3 of this run's brief gained no new occupant.** No system was found relying on a downstream compiler's exhaustiveness check to force an upstream update across a module, image or language boundary, beyond the two FD-24 already holds, which are LLVM generating the mapping from `Instruction.def` and GHC's `GHC.Types.Error.Codes` reflecting constructor names into a type family. Both replace exhaustiveness with a different instrument. **WHAT WAS NOT OPENED.** GHC merge request 4551 and its twelve siblings named in `GHCERRWIKI` were not fetched, so the per-MR diff sizes that would give (a) a number are unread. The `ghc-proposals` pull request 306 named at `GHCERRISSUE:1` was not fetched, because the wiki it points to is already pinned and carries the design. No attribute-grammar or error-recovery literature was read; every leg here is a shipped implementation or its own maintainers' documentation.

Every span quoted above was checked unique in its pin with `grep -acF`, which is what `xlat check` requires; one candidate span in `RUSTDCX` came back 2 and was replaced by the signature line at `:1078`. **Live tree spans, all read at HEAD `bc8f4e2` during this run**: `lib/lowering/compile-front.chiral:18` (the `surface/parse` import), `:58-60` (`term->ntalty`'s declaration and definition), `:67`, `:72`, `:134` (the three origination sites), `:68`, `:76-78`, `:114-133`, `:140-156`, `:213-222`, `:243-253`, `:329-337` (the propagation and call sites counted in item 1), `:232`, `:260`, `:337` (the three sinks); `lib/lowering/skip-diag.chiral:8` (the only import); `lib/typing/kernel.chiral:1315-1322` (`all-covered?` and its `jg-nonexhaustive`, reached only on the `(none dflt)` branch); `lib/typing/diag.chiral:12-18` (E157's taxonomy rule, quoted in item 8). It leaves three things in the author's hands: which of the three positions in item 7 this tree takes, whether the ordering in item 9 is accepted so that decision 3 is ruled before decision 2, and whether the middle position is worth adding to §5's knob list before either is ruled.

## Making an out-of-range index unrepresentable

### FD-26 no surveyed system refuses a bad index with a length index alone, and the four whose seam is closest to this tree put the length on the value and let a refinement name the preceding binder

- state:    OPEN
- claim:    The question: what does a language give up, and what must it add, so that a string or byte-buffer type carries its length in its type and an out-of-range index is a static type error? **Ten systems were read at their own sources, nineteen pins. The practice SPLITS into two families and the split is the answer.** **Family A puts the length in the type** (Idris 2, Agda, DML, ATS) and pays for it with a dependent pair at every boundary. **Family B puts the length on the value as a pure function and bounds the index with a refinement that names a preceding value binder** (F*, Low*, LiquidHaskell, Dafny), and refuses exactly the same bad call with no index at all. **This tree's measured blocker, `load: unknown name s` at `docs/arcs/parts/diagnostics-L5.md:261-268`, is the Family B capability.** The tree already has Family A machinery and already has refinements, and it has neither of them pointed at `Str`. **A length-indexed `Str` is one of two routes and the sources price it as the more expensive one.**
- measured: **1. THE TREE'S OWN MACHINERY, COUNTED AT HEAD `ec494bf` BY GREP OVER `lib/`, AND NOTHING IN THE TREE RECORDS THIS.** **Refinement seats: 5.** Two are function-argument seats, `lib/ports/sock.port:69` (`(refine I64 (> 0))` on `sock-recv`) and `lib/memory/mem-linear.chiral:27`. Three are `data` constructor fields, `lib/runtime/proc.chiral:28`, `:42`, `:43`. Two further `(refine ` hits in `lib/` are neither: a parser error string at `lib/surface/parse.chiral:392` and the erasure case at `lib/lowering/compile-front.chiral:69`. **Type-level index seats: 33**, every one of the shape `(-> (0 n I64) ...)`, in five files: `lib/ports/pool.port` 3, `lib/memory/mem-linear.chiral` 3, `lib/memory/mem-region.chiral` 6, `lib/protocol/grid.chiral` 6, `lib/protocol/vt-parser.chiral` 15. A 34th occurrence is prose inside a comment at `lib/module/loader.chiral:452`. **Every erased binder in `lib/` is `I64`-typed; there are zero of any other type.** **Every one of the 33 indexes a linear resource**, `(Pool n)`, `(Region n)` or `(Grid n)`, and is paired with a `(1 x (T n))` seat. **`Str` and `Bytes` are indexed nowhere:** `grep -rnE '\(Str [a-z]|\(Bytes [a-z]' lib/` returns nothing, and `lib/surface/syntax.chiral:29` declares both as `t-primty`, a bare primitive. **The two kinds of machinery meet in exactly one seat in the whole tree**, `lib/memory/mem-linear.chiral:27`. **The three probes in `diagnostics-L5.md` §2 all reproduce today against `bin/chirality`:** `(-> (0 n I64) (=> Str I64 (refine I64 (<= n)) Str))` checks at exit 0; applying it as `(sub-safe 3 "abc" 0 99)` fails `load: cannot prove refinement` at exit 1; and `(=> (s Str) I64 (refine I64 (<= (str-len s))) Str)` fails `load: unknown name s` at exit 1.

  **2. HOW THE INDEX IS SPELLED, AND THE TWO SPELLINGS ARE NOT EQUAL IN COST.** **`Fin n` is an inductive and its values are unary.** Agda declares AGDAFIN:34 "data Fin : ℕ → Set where" with the successor case AGDAFIN:36 "  suc  : (i : Fin n) → Fin (suc n)", so the index 1000 is 1000 constructors before any compiler optimisation, and the lookup takes it as its own type, AGDAVEC:59 "lookup : Vec A n → Fin n → A". Idris 2 spells the same pair, IDRISVECT:16 "data Vect : (len : Nat) -> (elem : Type) -> Type where" and IDRISVECT:144 "index : Fin len -> Vect len elem -> elem". **The systems that target machine code refuse `Fin` and use a singleton over a machine integer plus a constraint, and DML states the reason.** The purity requirement on index objects is what forces it: DMLPOPL99:223 "connection to run-time values by using" singleton types, DMLPOPL99:228 "which contains just the integer" n, and DML says this is what makes the application possible at all. Its array signature is DMLPOPL99:3615 "sub <| {n:nat} {i:nat | i < n}" over DMLPOPL99:3616 "'a array(n) * int(i) -> 'a", where DMLPOPL99:3597 "is a singleton type which contains only" that one integer. **ATS ships the same spelling over a C `char*` today.** Its indexed string type is ATSBASICS:525 "string_int_type(n: int) = string_type" where ATSBASICS:523 "string_type = ptr // = char* in C", and its indexing function is ATSSTRING:176 "  (s: string(n), i: size_t(i)):<> charNZ" under the static quantifier `{n:int}{i:nat | i < n}` at `ATSSTRING:175`. **ATS's substring is PRB-24's and PRB-47's function with the bad call unrepresentable:** ATSSTRING:339 "  (str: string(n), st: size_t st, ln: size_t ln):<!wrt> strnptr(ln)" under `{n:int}{st,ln:nat | st+ln <= n}` at `:338`. ATS indexes bytes the same way, ATSBASICS:613 "typedef bytes (n:int) = @[byte][n]". **The singleton spelling is this tree's `(0 n I64)` plus `(refine I64 ...)` with one piece missing: a machine integer whose type-level value is itself.** The tree can bound a seat by an index; it cannot yet say that a given `I64` value *is* the index.

  **3. THE FOUR SYSTEMS CLOSEST TO THIS TREE'S SEAM CARRY NO INDEX AT ALL, AND THEY REFUSE THE SAME CALL.** **F* makes length a function of the value and lets the refinement call it.** FSTARSEQ:26 "val length: #a:Type -> seq a -> Tot nat", then FSTARSEQ:32 "val index:  #a:Type -> s:seq a -> i:nat{i < length s} -> Tot a", and the slice is FSTARSEQ:64 "val slice:  #a:Type -> s:seq a -> i:nat -> j:nat{i <= j && j <= length s} -> Tot (seq a)". That last line is the signature `diagnostics-L5.md` §4 Shape B wanted and could not construct. **LiquidHaskell does it with a measure and names the capability.** LHTUTOR:106 "measure vlen :: Vector a -> Int" then LHTUTOR:112 "assume (!) :: x:Vector a -> {v:Nat | v < vlen x} -> a", and the tutorial calls the enabling feature Dependent Refinements, LHTUTOR:140 "signature for `length` names the input with the binder" and LHTUTOR:142 "the signature for `(!)` names the input vector `x` so that the index" can be constrained against it. **Dafny does it with a field.** DAFNYTYPES:2755 "The length of an array is retrieved using the immutable `Length`" and indexing is admitted DAFNYTYPES:2763 "For any integer-based numeric `i` in the range `0 <= i < a.Length`,". **In all four, the type of the carrier is unchanged and the bound is stated at the seat.** The single capability they share and this tree lacks is a refinement atom that can name a value binder introduced earlier in the same arrow.

  **4. WHERE THE LENGTH COMES FROM AT A BOUNDARY: THE PUBLISHED NAME IS THE EXISTENTIAL, AND FAMILY B HAS NO BOUNDARY PROBLEM.** Idris states the use directly: IDRISDPAIR:986 "One use for dependent pairs is to return values of dependent types" IDRISDPAIR:987 "where the index is not necessarily known in advance", and gives the other name at IDRISDPAIR:1016 "Dependent pairs are sometimes referred to as" Sigma types. DML calls it a weak dependent sum and says what it is for: DMLPOPL99:3121 "pressed using an existential dependent type, also called weak" DMLPOPL99:3122 "dependent sum. Also, existential types can mediate between" dependent and ordinary ML types, which is how a library function of unknown length is given a type at all. ATS spells the existential in the type itself, ATSBASICS:535 "typedef String = [n:int] string_int_type(n)" and ATSBASICS:536 "typedef String0 = [n:int | n >= 0] string_int_type(n)". Idris's conversion from an unindexed carrier is IDRISVECT:342 "fromList : (xs : List elem) -> Vect (length xs) elem", which computes the index from the value. **For Family B the question does not arise**: a string read from a socket already has a length, because the length is a function of the value rather than a parameter of the type.

  **5. WHAT BECOMES UNWRITABLE, FROM THE SOURCES.** **Any function whose output length depends on a runtime predicate stops returning the carrier and starts returning a pair.** In Idris's own `Data.Vect` that is IDRISVECT:656 "filter : (elem -> Bool) -> Vect len elem -> (p ** Vect p elem)", and the same for `nubBy`, `nub`, `delete`, `partition`, and IDRISVECT:396 "mapMaybe : (f : a -> Maybe b) -> (xs : Vect len a) -> (m : Nat ** Vect m b)". DML states the cause: DMLPOPL99:3105 "express the length of the resulting list since it depends on" the predicate, so the length is inexpressible in the constraint domain. **The pair is not free at runtime.** Idris's own source carries the price as a comment: IDRISVECT:697 "deleteBy : {len : _} -> -- needed for the dependent pair", an implicit at unrestricted multiplicity, which is a `Nat` that survives erasure. **Concatenation needs arithmetic on indices, and the one mainstream language that added const generics refused to do it.** RFC 2000 states its own position: RUSTCONSTGEN:308 "minimal unification of abstract const expressions possible - it essentially" does not unify them. The feature that would allow it is still marked RUSTGCE:13 "Warning: This feature is incomplete; its design and syntax may change." on a tracking issue open since 2020. **A function over two carriers of different lengths needs two index binders**, which in Idris is the ordinary case and in this tree would be a second `(0 m I64)` on every such seat.

  **6. ERASURE: FAMILY A ERASES THE INDEX AND LOSES `length` WITH IT; FAMILY B ERASES THE LENGTH AND PAYS AT THE CALL SITE; RUST KEEPS IT AND PAYS A WORD.** **DML proves the erasure and states there is no reason to keep the index.** DMLPOPL99:1903 "We define the index erasure function" and DMLPOPL99:1816 "dices at run-time|there is simply no need to do so. Clearly," this would change only if runtime type-checking became necessary. **ATS's indexed string erases to a bare pointer**, ATSBASICS:525 "string_int_type(n: int) = string_type" over ATSBASICS:523 "string_type = ptr // = char* in C", so the index costs nothing in the C output. **Idris 2 uses the same quantities this tree uses and names the consequence.** IDRISQTT:13 "``0``, meaning that the variable is *erased* at run time" is the tree's `(0 n I64)` in another notation. The consequence is that a length function must un-erase the index: IDRISQTT:393 "vlen : {n : Nat} -> Vect n a -> Nat", and a caller whose own index is erased is refused with IDRISQTT:410 "m is not accessible in this context". **Low* is the sharpest case, because its output is C.** LOWSTARLIB:345 "The length is available in ghost (proof) code only: just like in C, one" LOWSTARLIB:346 "cannot compute the length of a buffer at run-time. Therefore, a typical" LOWSTARLIB:347 "pattern is to use refinements to tie together a buffer and its length, as we" saw earlier, and the published pattern is a runtime length passed beside the buffer: LOWSTARLIB:350 "let do_something (x: B.buffer UInt64.t) (l: U32.t { U32.v l = B.length x }): St unit =". That signature is the constructible form `diagnostics-L5.md` measured, with the tie that this tree cannot write. **Rust made the opposite trade and the cost is published in the reference:** a pointer to a slice is RUSTDST:11 "twice the size of pointers to sized types, since they also store *metadata*", and RUSTDST:12 "Pointers to slices store the number of elements; pointers to `str` store the length in bytes."

  **7. WHETHER ANYONE RETROFITTED IT, AND WHAT IT COST. THREE DID, AND THE PRICES ARE DIFFERENT BY AN ORDER OF MAGNITUDE.** **DML retrofitted onto ML and the language half was cheap.** DMLPOPL99:2787 "makes our extension truly conservative in the sense that" DMLPOPL99:2788 "pure ML programs will work exactly as before, not requir-" ing any annotations, and the annotation burden is reported as DMLPOPL99:285 "very little internal change, annotations are usually to the" point. **The published price is at the seam, and DML names it as a loss:** DMLPOPL99:3133 "represent ML types leads to a major loss of information at" DMLPOPL99:3134 "module boundaries. We would like soon to address this issue" by exporting dependent types in signatures. **ATS's price is a doubled API, visible in the shipping prelude.** The unindexed and indexed string types both survive and the name resolves to either, ATSBASICS:532 "stadef string = string1 // 2nd-select" beside a first-select line for the unindexed one. `strlen` is therefore two functions, ATSSTRING:404 "  (x: NSH(string)):<> size_t" for the unindexed carrier and ATSSTRING:407 "  {n:int} (x: NSH(string(n))):<> size_t(n)" for the indexed one returning a singleton, resolved by an overload table: ATSSTRING:411 "overload strlen with string0_length of 0" and ATSSTRING:412 "overload strlen with string1_length of 10". **LiquidHaskell's retrofit is the cheapest measured and it changed the library by zero lines.** `liquid-vector/src/Data/Vector_LHAssumptions.hs` is **47 lines** against the unmodified `Data.Vector` from Hackage: one measure, LHVECTOR:13 "measure vlen    :: forall a. (Data.Vector.Vector a) -> Int", **eight `assume` axioms** including LHVECTOR:17 "assume Data.Vector.!           :: forall a. x:(Data.Vector.Vector a) -> vec:{v:Nat | v < vlen x } -> a", one invariant, and **eleven `qualif` hints** for the inference engine. **The price is trust, and the tutorial says so:** an assume is used LHTUTOR:132 "because we don't have the code" for the function being specified, so the bound is enforced at every call and the library's own body is never checked.

  **8. THE COUNTER-CASE, AND THE ONE SYSTEM THAT GAVE UP.** **Rust chose the runtime check and states it as a property of the language:** RUSTSLICETY:30 "access to a slice is always bounds-checked in safe methods and operators", with the total form RUSTSLICE:183 "    fn get(self, slice: &[T]) -> Option<&T> {" guarded by `slice.len()` at `:184`. The published reason for stopping at const generics is cost: RUSTCONSTGEN:279 "This feature adds a significant amount of complexity to the type system," and it requires rules RUSTCONSTGEN:281 "around abstract const equality, which result in surprising edge cases." **Cogent, the closest published relative of this tree's lane, has no array type in its own language at all.** COGENTFFI:127 "Cogent provides an escape hatch in the form of a" foreign function interface, arrays come from there, COGENTFFI:147 "engineers provide data structures and their associated operations, including iterators," COGENTFFI:148 "in a special dialect of C, and import them into Cogent, including in formal reasoning." The type system does not see the bound: the signature is COGENTFFI:233 ")!, U32, a!)", an unrefined `U32` index with a caller-supplied default returned on failure, COGENTFFI:251 "where array indices and length are unsigned 32-bit integers", and the consequence is stated as the paper's problem: COGENTFFI:129 "This poses a problem when verifying Cogent programs, as imported C components do not enjoy the same level of static guarantees" that Cogent does.

  **9. WHETHER THE EVIDENCE SUPPORTS THE FRAMING, SAID PLAINLY.** **The framing's first half holds and is not controversial.** Every surveyed system except Rust and Cogent refuses the out-of-range call at check time, and in ATS it is refused over a C `char*` with the index erased, which is this tree's exact target. **The framing's second half, that the move is a length-indexed carrier, is one of two routes and the sources price it as the more expensive.** The length-indexed route costs a dependent pair at every boundary, a doubled API where an unindexed carrier survives, index arithmetic that Rust declined to build, and a kept `Nat` wherever the pair is consumed. The refinement-over-a-measure route costs one language capability. **This tree already pays most of the length-indexed route's fixed cost for its linear resources** (33 seats), so the marginal cost of extending it to `Str` is smaller here than the literature's average. **This tree has zero of the other route**, and it is the route four of the surveyed systems took, including both whose output is C. **⚑ The two are not exclusive.** ATS ships an indexed string AND a `strlen` returning a singleton; F* ships a `length` function AND refinements that call it. A refinement that can name a preceding value binder is a prerequisite for the Family B route and is also what makes a Family A index usable at a boundary, because that is how `(refine I64 (<= n))` gets tied to the string rather than threaded by hand. **On the evidence the binder-scope defect is the load-bearing one and the index is a separable second question.** The ruling is the author's.

  **10. WHAT THE SOURCES DO NOT SETTLE, AND WHAT WAS NOT REACHED.** **No source gives a measured annotation burden for retrofitting a length index onto an existing string type in a self-hosting compiler.** DML's "very little internal change" is a claim without a count, ATS's doubled prelude is visible but unpriced, and LiquidHaskell's 47 lines are for a route that adds no index. **Xi and Pfenning's PLDI 1998 paper, which carries the array-bound-check elimination benchmarks, was not reached:** `https://www.cs.cmu.edu/~fp/papers/pldi98.pdf` returns 404, as do three mirrors tried at `cs.bu.edu` and `ats-lang.org`, and the ACM DL copy is paywalled. The POPL 1999 paper pinned here cites it for those numbers and does not repeat them. **ATS's `prelude/SATS/string.sats` is a symlink whose target 404s on `raw.githubusercontent.com`**, so the signatures were read from the generator source `prelude/SATS/CODEGEN/string.atxt`, which is what the `.sats` is generated from. **No surveyed system was found that retrofitted a length index onto a string type that a live compiler already used for its own source text**, which is this tree's position, so item 7's prices are from adjacent cases rather than from the case at hand. **Nothing was found on what a length index costs a self-hosting bootstrap**, where the compiler's own `Str` values are the program text.
- evidence: **Nineteen pins added 2026-09-10 local in `.planning/sources/`, sixteen origin `raw` and three `transcribed`.
