This row prices both routes and rules neither. It leaves three things in the author's hands: whether a refinement atom may name a preceding `=>` value binder, whether `Str` and `Bytes` gain a type-level length index, and whether the two are ordered or taken together. PRB-17 bears on either route, because a bound that wraps at the I64 maximum is a bound the checker accepts for the one value that cannot satisfy it.

## Binding a build-state claim to evidence

### FD-27 every surveyed binding keys a claim to a declared artifact identity and fails by a lookup that cannot resolve, only one surveyed field fires when the RULE moves instead of the artifact, and this tree's resolver is the exact inverse of the rule that would have caught E76

- state:    OPEN
- claim:    The question: how do comparable systems bind a recorded build-state claim to evidence, so that the claim goes stale loudly rather than silently when the artifact moves underneath it? **Nine systems were read at their own sources, eighteen pins, every one raw.** **Every surveyed binding has one shape. A claim carries a key over an artifact identity. The loudness is a lookup that fails, and no comparison of two statements is involved.** The families differ in what the key is over. **NAME** (a Nix store path, in-toto's `REQUIRE`). **BYTES** (in-toto's subject digest, SLSA's subject match, Bazel's external-dependency hash manifest). **THE RULE THE VERDICT WAS TAKEN AGAINST** (SLSA's Verification Summary Attestation carries `policy.digest`). **THE CLOCK** (in-toto layout `expires`, TUF `EXPIRES`). **COVERAGE**, where an artifact that no rule consumed fails the run outright (in-toto `DISALLOW *`, cargo-vet's exemptions table, rebuilderd's `UNKWN`). **Applied to E76 the families split cleanly.** Name keys and coverage keys fire. Byte keys MISS a pure rename, and in-toto states that property in its own words. Input-addressed build keys, the Nix derivation and the Bazel action, MISS entirely: their reach is the declared input set, and both projects state that the enumeration is a human's responsibility whose completeness is best effort. **The one surveyed field that fires on E76's actual defect, where the artifact held still and the design moved over it, is the VSA's policy digest, and no state column in this tree records which rule a `built` verdict was taken against.** **The measured local cause is sharper than the survey: `MAP.md:20` declares three extensions importable and `MAP.md:27` has the resolver probe all three, so `target-linux.chiral` becoming `target-linux.manifest` is invisible to every consumer by construction.** Nix makes the name part of the identity so that a rename breaks every reference. This tree makes the extension the file's declared KIND and then resolves across kinds, so a change of kind breaks nothing and announces nothing. **The cheapest catch needs no new mechanism: `ledger-lint` check AI already fails a row whose cited files changed after its `checked:` date, and it cannot reach a `State` column because that column carries neither a `checked:` date nor an `evidence:` field.**
- measured: **1. THE E76 CASE, MEASURED IN THE TREE TODAY, AND THE CLAIM NAMES TWO PATHS THAT DO NOT EXIST.** The ledger row is `docs/elements/ledger.md:194`, State `built (ENFORCED)`. It names "the swappable 40-row `SysReg` in `target-linux.chiral`" and closes with "Gate: `test-syscall-manifest.sh` 12/12". **`find . -name 'target-linux*'` returns exactly one file, `lib/lowering/tal/target-linux.manifest`, and `find . -name 'test-syscall-manifest*'` returns nothing.** The gate that exists is `tools/test/syscall-manifest.sh`, wired as phase 5 at `tools/test/run-tests.sh:146`. **Seven further live spans across four files name the file by its dead name**: `lib/lowering/tal/sys-check.chiral:11` and `:13`, `lib/lowering/tal/sys.chiral:785` and `:1003`, `lib/module/loader.chiral:263`, plus `tools/test/syscall-manifest.sh:14` and `:73`. The one span that resolves is the extensionless module import at `lib/lowering/tal/sys-linkage.chiral:23`, `(import "lowering/tal/target-linux")`. **The file was added already carrying `.manifest` at `9a206fe` dated 2026-08-30, whose message is "migrate slice 1: move the source, rename the language, compile nothing".** It holds 40 `(sys-row ...)` entries, which is the row's "40-row" figure. **Seven carry the syscall number `16`, at `:30`, `:31`, `:32`, `:33`, `:34`, `:35` and `:60`, and three of the seven (`:30`, `:31`, `:32`) carry no comment at all**, so the ioctl request that distinguishes them is nowhere in the file. `.planning/MANIFEST-DESIGN-MAP.md:118` is requirement 3, "Constructors have named fields", and `:119` is requirement 4, "Anything distinguishing two rows is a field", whose stated justification is "7 rows carry `16`; only comments tell them apart, and `sexp.chiral` drops comments at the lexer". The file satisfies neither. **`records/conformance-map.md` has no E76 row**, and `docs/elements/ledger.md:17-18` names that file as the per-element build-state authority, with `:20` making it win when the two disagree. **That same header carries a third dead path**: `docs/elements/ledger.md:17` sends a reader to `SELF-IMPLEMENT-CATALOG.md` for the detail, and `find . -name 'SELF-IMPLEMENT-CATALOG*'` returns nothing. The file is `docs/elements/catalog.md`.

  **2. FAMILY NAME: THE ARTIFACT'S NAME IS PART OF ITS IDENTITY, SO A RENAME BREAKS EVERY REFERENCE.** Nix puts the name inside the key. A store path base name is NIXPATH:27 "Store path base names are pairs of" a digest and NIXPATH:30 "A symbolic [name]{#name} for people to read", and NIXPATH:22 "A store path base name will always reference exactly one store object." A reference that names the old path finds nothing, which is a failure with no comparison in it. in-toto spells the same key as a rule rather than a path: INTOTOSPEC:880 "**REQUIRE**: indicates that the specified artifact must appear as a material" or product of the step, and INTOTOSPEC:999 "artifact name is not present in the queue of remaining artifacts, rule" processing errors out. **Cost: every reference must carry the full key, and every rename is a tree-wide edit.** Nix pays it with a store nobody edits by hand. in-toto pays it with a layout that enumerates the artifacts by name.

  **3. FAMILY BYTES: THE DIGEST IS THE IDENTITY, AND IN-TOTO STATES THAT THIS MAKES A RENAME INVISIBLE.** in-toto's statement type says INTOTOSTMT:39 "Subjects are assumed to be _immutable_" and then says how matching works: INTOTOSTMT:54 "Subject artifacts are matched purely by digest, regardless of" name or URI. SLSA's verification procedure is the same step, SLSAVERIFY:98 "that statement's `subject` matches the digest of" the artifact in question, and it names what that buys: SLSAVERIFY:116 "covers tampering of the artifact or provenance" after the build. **Bazel carries the same key in a tracked file, which is the surveyed shape closest to a ledger row.** External dependencies go in a BAZELARTIFACT:254 "workspacewide manifest file that lists a _cryptographic hash_ for every external" dependency, and the consequence is stated plainly: BAZELARTIFACT:262 "If the artifact we download has a different hash than the one declared in the" manifest, BAZELARTIFACT:263 "manifest, the build will fail unless the hash in the manifest is updated." The update is a reviewable change, because it must be approved and checked into BAZELARTIFACT:265 "source control before the build will accept the new dependency." **Cost: the claim must be re-signed or re-hashed on every legitimate edit, and a digest says that something changed without saying what obligation broke.**

  **4. FAMILY RULE: THE VERDICT RECORDS WHICH POLICY IT WAS TAKEN AGAINST, AT WHICH VERSION.** SLSA's Verification Summary Attestation is a verifier's recorded claim that an artifact passed, so a consumer need not re-run the check. Its `policy` field is required, and the spec says what it must carry: SLSAVSA:171 "The entry MUST contain a `uri` identifying which policy was applied and" SLSAVSA:172 "SHOULD contain a `digest` to indicate the exact version of that policy." It also carries a clock on the verdict itself, SLSAVSA:152 "Timestamp indicating what time the verification occurred." **This is the only surveyed field that fires when the artifact holds still and the rule moves over it.** Every other mechanism in the survey keys on the thing judged. **Cost: the policy must be a citable object with a stable identity, and every amendment to it invalidates every verdict taken under the old digest, which is loud on purpose and expensive where the policy is churning.**

  **5. FAMILY CLOCK: A CLAIM WITH NO EXPIRY CANNOT BE DISTINGUISHED FROM A CLAIM NOBODY RECHECKED.** in-toto puts the date in the layout: INTOTOSPEC:707 "EXPIRES determines when layout metadata should be considered expired and no" INTOTOSPEC:708 "longer trusted by clients. Clients MUST NOT trust an expired file." and verification checks it first, INTOTOSPEC:1232 "The expiration time is verified to ensure that this layout is still fresh." TUF spells the same field, TUFSPEC:732 "A <a>date-time</a> string indicating when metadata should be considered" expired, and it is the only surveyed source that names the threat the field exists to stop: TUFSPEC:178 "**Indefinite freeze attacks.**  An attacker cannot respond to client" TUFSPEC:179 "requests with the same, outdated metadata without the client being aware" of the problem. TUF's update workflow makes the check a step with a named failure, TUFSPEC:1364 "trusted root metadata file MUST be higher than the fixed update start time." and TUFSPEC:1365 "If the trusted root metadata file has expired, abort the update cycle," and report the potential freeze attack. **Cost: renewal churn, and the signal says a date passed without saying which obligation broke.** **This is the only family that fires on a population of claims nothing has touched, which is the shape of the 56 elements in item 9.**

  **6. FAMILY COVERAGE: AN ARTIFACT NO RULE ACCOUNTED FOR FAILS THE RUN, AND AN UNMEASURED ARTIFACT GETS A STATE OF ITS OWN.** in-toto makes coverage opt-in and states the consequence of taking it. With the catch-all disallow rule at the end of a rule list, INTOTOSPEC:899 "in-toto verification fails if an artifact" INTOTOSPEC:900 "was not consumed by an earlier rule." without it, artifacts can enter a step undetected. Its rule vocabulary also carries a positive drift check, INTOTOSPEC:887 "materials of this step, and their hashes must not be the same." for `MODIFY`. **cargo-vet turns the same idea into a tracked backlog.** A claim is keyed to a version, CARGOVETAUDIT:8 "Specifies that this audit entry corresponds to an absolute version that was" audited in its entirety, an uncovered version is refused in CI, CARGOVETHOW:43 "verify that the new code has been audited by a trusted organization. If not," the patch is refused, and a counter-claim overrides a covering one, CARGOVETAUDIT:33 "If a `violation` entry exists for a given crate version, `cargo vet` will reject" the dependency even when it is exempted. **The backlog is a tracked table.** CARGOVETCONF:207 "This table enumerates the set of crates which are being used despite missing the" required audits, each entry pinned to a version, CARGOVETCONF:213 "Specifies the exact version which should be exempted." The bootstrap move is to enter the discipline from an approved state, CARGOVETHOW:28 "These are automatically added to the exemptions list" at init. **rebuilderd gives the third-party version.** It recomputes rather than records, REBUILDERD:25 "rebuilder backends like [archlinux-repro][1] to verify the provided binary" packages can be reproduced from the given source, REBUILDERD:30 "It tracks the state of successfully verified packages", and a package that has not been measured yet holds a distinct state rather than a stale verdict, REBUILDERD:167 "Check the package was successfully added to the database with status `UNKWN`". Its own docs treat an unknown as a queue question, REBUILDERD:96 "If the rebuilder seems to have outdated data or lists a package as unknown the" update may still be in the build queue. **Cost: the coverage rule must enumerate the domain, and the exemptions table is a permanent visible debt that someone has to keep from growing.**

  **7. FAMILY GENERATED: THE DOCUMENT HOLDS A POINTER OR A CHECKSUM, NEVER AN AUTHORED VERDICT.** Cog writes generated text into a hand-edited file and guards the region with a checksum, COGRUN:43 "Checksum the output to protect it against accidental" edits, and the failure is refusal rather than a silent overwrite: COGRUN:207 "If the generated code is edited by a misguided developer, the next time cog" COGRUN:208 "is run, the checksum won't match, and cog will stop to avoid overwriting the" edited output. The gate half is a separate flag, COGRUN:224 "You can use the ``--check`` option to run cog just to check that the files" would not change if run again, with COGRUN:228 "The ``--diff`` option will show a unified diff of the change that caused" the failure. **Scorecard takes the stronger position of storing no verdict at all.** The README holds a badge URL whose value is computed elsewhere, SCORECARD:207 "This badge also auto-updates for every change made to the repository." over a recomputation on a fixed cadence, SCORECARD:139 "We run a weekly Scorecard scan of the 1 million most critical open source" projects. **A document that holds a pointer cannot hold a stale claim, because it holds no claim.** **Cost: the measurement has to be cheap enough to re-run on the cadence, and a pointer is unreadable in a diff and in a fresh clone with no network.**

  **8. FAMILY ONE-ARTIFACT: THE CLAIM AND THE CHECK ARE THE SAME TEXT, AND THE CONFORMANCE-PACKAGE VARIANT SHIPS THE RAW LOGS AND THE CHECKER'S OWN GIT STATE.** doctest states the purpose as keeping prose honest, PYDOCTEST:15 "To check that a module's docstrings are up-to-date by verifying that all" PYDOCTEST:16 "interactive examples still work as documented." and its author names the loudness property from experience, PYDOCTEST:1946 "examples stops working after a" harmless change. **The Vulkan CTS submission is the same idea at industrial scale, and it is the only surveyed mechanism that pins the CHECKER's identity as hard as the artifact's.** The package is enumerated at VKCTS:496 "The conformance submission package must contain the following:" and the first two items are the raw evidence and the checker's provenance: VKCTS:498 "1. Full test logs (`TestResults.qpa`) from CTS runs against all driver builds and all fractions" and VKCTS:499 "2. Result of `git status` and `git log` from CTS source directory". The checker must be unmodified or the modification must be exhibited, VKCTS:517 "The CTS build must always be done from clean git repository that doesn't have any" uncommitted changes, and VKCTS:524 "Any changes made to CTS must be committed to the local repository, and provided" as patches. The claim itself names the checker version, VKCTS:552 "CONFORM_VERSION:         <git tag of CTS release>". **Cost: the package is large, the discipline is per-submission rather than continuous, and a product that changes after the submission is unexamined until the next one.**

  **9. THE E76 TEST, MECHANISM BY MECHANISM, AND FOUR OF THE NINE MISS.** **FIRES, and most cheaply: a name key.** The ledger row at `docs/elements/ledger.md:194` names `target-linux.chiral` and `test-syscall-manifest.sh`, and neither resolves. A check that every path a `built` row names must exist would have failed the day `9a206fe` landed. **FIRES: coverage.** E76 is absent from `records/conformance-map.md`, which its own ledger header calls the authority, and the manifest requirements at `.planning/MANIFEST-DESIGN-MAP.md:116-119` govern a file nothing tests them against. An in-toto `DISALLOW *` over the deliverable set, or a cargo-vet exemptions table over the `built` population, makes that absence a failure rather than a silence. **FIRES: the policy digest.** The row's verdict is dated 2026-08-22 in its own prose; `.planning/MANIFEST-DESIGN-MAP.md:25` carries "Amended 2026-09-02", which moved five things. A VSA-shaped row recording which rule the verdict was taken against, at which version, shows the verdict predating its own policy. **FIRES: expiry.** The row would have gone stale on the clock whatever moved. **MISSES: a byte digest alone.** in-toto matches INTOTOSTMT:54 "Subject artifacts are matched purely by digest, regardless of" name, so a pure rename is invisible to it, and this rename was a `git mv` plus a compile-nothing commit. **MISSES: the input-addressed build key.** Nix requires that the input set be enumerated by hand, NIXDRV:101 "Nix requires that all inputs be explicitly collected in the inputs field", and states whose job that is: the "responsibility of the creator of a derivation" to include everything referenced. Bazel's key is over declared inputs too, BAZELHERM:28 "**Source identity**: Hermetic build systems try to ensure the sameness of" inputs, BAZELHERM:30 "unique hash code. Hermetic build systems use this hash to identify changes to" the build's input, enforced by a sandbox where BAZELARTIFACT:220 "they are unable to read any files they don’t declare". SLSA says the same about its own enumeration, SLSAPROVB:222 "Unordered collection of artifacts needed at build time. Completeness is best" effort, and again at SLSAPROVB:298 "SLSA Build L3, completeness is considered" best effort. **A requirement nothing consumes is outside every input-addressed key, and the surveyed systems say so themselves.** **MISSES: regenerate-and-compare.** Generating the `State` column from the last green run of `tools/test/syscall-manifest.sh` keeps the column honest about the gate. The gate passes. Requirements 3 and 4 are not in it, so the generated column would still read `built (ENFORCED)`. **MISSES: the conformance package.** It pins the checker and the product at submission time and says nothing about the months after. **MISSES, and this is the local cause: this tree's resolver is the inverse of the Nix rule.** `MAP.md:5` states "The extension is the file's kind. The resolver checks it." and `MAP.md:20` makes `.chiral`, `.port` and `.manifest` all import targets, so `MAP.md:27` has the resolver probe three extensions. The module key `lowering/tal/target-linux` therefore resolves whichever of the three kinds the file carries, and the import at `lib/lowering/tal/sys-linkage.chiral:23` kept working across a change of declared kind. **The build could not have noticed, because the tree decided that a file's kind is not part of its identity.**

  **10. WHAT IS TRANSFERABLE HERE, WITH ITS PRICE, AND ONE PIECE IS ALREADY BUILT.** **The already-built piece is `ledger-lint` check AI.** Its docstring quotes the rule it enforces, "A row whose `checked:` date predates the last change to the files it cites is unverified", at `tools/ledger-lint/ledger-lint.py:2120-2122`, and it implements it by `git log -1 --format=%cs` per cited path, `:2129-2134`. **That is the clock family and the name family in one check, and it is scoped by field presence.** It reaches a row that carries `evidence:` and `checked:`. `docs/elements/ledger.md`'s `State` column carries the evidence as prose (E76's row names a gate, a commit `612a2d9`, and two dates) and carries no `checked:` field, so nothing parses it and AI cannot see it. **Price: two fields per built row, and the 109 rows in item 9's population would each need one date.** **The cheapest new check is the name key, and it needs nothing the tree lacks**: every path and gate name a `built` row cites must exist. It fires on E76 today and its price is one pass over the ledger. **The policy digest is the one that fires on the defect rather than on its symptom**, and its price is that a requirement set has to become a citable object with a version, which `.planning/MANIFEST-DESIGN-MAP.md` currently is not. **The coverage rule is the one that addresses the population rather than the case**, and cargo-vet's price is the honest one: an exemptions table that enumerates every claim standing without a measurement. **Requirement 4 is mechanically decidable and is written as prose in the session tier.** A `.manifest` in which two rows agree on every field the reader parses is exactly the doctest shape: the requirement and its check would be one artifact, and `sexp.chiral` dropping comments at the lexer is what makes the predicate total.

  **11. WHAT THE SOURCES DO NOT SETTLE, AND WHAT WAS NOT REACHED.** **No surveyed system binds a PROSE design requirement to an artifact automatically.** Every mechanism read here keys on an identity or re-runs a predicate; the step from "requirement 4 says rows must be distinguishable by fields" to a runnable check is human work in all nine, and none of them prices it. **No source gives a measured cost for retrofitting any of these onto an existing hand-written state column.** cargo-vet's five-minute init is for a tool that reads a lockfile. A table of prose verdicts is a different job and no surveyed source prices it. **The formal-methods half of the question was not reached.** The TLA+ and Alloy model-to-implementation drift literature was looked for and the primary sources are PDFs; this sandbox has no `pdftotext`, and the survey holds no pin for it, so nothing here speaks to the case where the claim is a specification rather than a build verdict. **Terraform's `plan -detailed-exitcode`, the closest surveyed-adjacent shape to a re-runnable drift predicate with a distinct exit code, was not pinned**: `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/hashicorp/terraform/main/website/docs/cli/commands/plan.mdx` returns 404 and the current path was not found. **No source in the survey measures how often a name key produces a false alarm**, which is the cost that would fall on this tree hardest, because a legitimate rename would fail every row that cites the old path until each is edited. **Khronos conformance expiry was looked for and not found in the pinned CTS README**: the file states the submission package contents and the `CONFORM_VERSION` pin, and says nothing about a submission ceasing to be valid, so the claim that conformance expires is unsupported by the source pinned here.

- element:  `none`. This row surveys and prices; it rules nothing and mints nothing. It leaves four things in the author's hands: whether a `built` row gains a `checked:` date and an `evidence:` field so that `ledger-lint` check AI reaches it; whether a state claim records the rule it was taken against and at which version, which is the only surveyed field that fires on E76's actual defect; whether the population of `built` rows standing without a measurement becomes an enumerated exemptions table or stays implicit; and whether `MAP.md:27`'s three-extension probe is the right trade now that a change of declared kind is measured to pass silently under a `built (ENFORCED)` claim.

### FD-28 one of fifteen surveyed mechanisms fires on a carrier that reasons from a changed rule, it fires by storing the rule's fingerprint inside the carrier, and Case 1's carrier cited the authority twice while three greps disagreed about finding it

- state:    OPEN
- claim:    The question: when one rule has to be stated in prose to be understood, executed in a check to be enforced, and quoted by a body of records as the premise of their own measurement, what keeps it single-sourced so that changing it is one edit? **Fifteen mechanisms were read at their own sources. Seventeen pins added, every one raw, two earlier pins reused.** **Every surveyed mechanism that fires on a changed rule requires the carrier to emit a key the tool can index: a code like `E0123`, a spec anchor like `esid`, a link UID, a resolvable reference. None of them reads prose looking for a paraphrase.** The families are six. **MARK THE NORMATIVE SENTENCE** (RFC 2119 keywords, with RFC 8174 saying in its own words that unmarked text is still normative). **AMEND BY NEW IDENTITY** (an RFC republished under a new number, an ADR whose status names the id that superseded it). **ONE ARTIFACT** (doctest, the CommonMark specification whose worked examples are the conformance suite, rustdoc doctests, Gherkin). **TRANSCLUDE** (Sphinx `literalinclude` sliced by text markers, `#[doc = include_str!]` over a README, cog's checksummed generated region). **REGISTRY AND COVERAGE** (rustc's error codes, where one tidy check demands an explanation, a doctest inside that explanation, a UI test, and proof the code is emitted at all). **FINGERPRINT THE CITATION** (Doorstop, where a child stores the parent's hash and one parent edit marks every child suspect). **Applied to Case 1's missed carrier, fourteen of the fifteen MISS and the one that fires is Doorstop**, which fires twice over: the stored fingerprint of `docs/goals/README.md` no longer matches, and a normative item carrying no parent link is a finding in its own right. **Two more fire only in a pair the tree does not have**: a registry coverage rule over the citation key, and a retired-id check paired with amendment by new identity. **Measured in this tree, the carrier was never uncitable.** `docs/decisions/decision-primitive-with-consumer.md:126-127` writes `[[goals/README]]` and then `` `:27` `` on the next line, so the three obvious searches disagree: `git grep -l 'belongs to exactly one arc'` returns **five** files and misses it, `git grep -l -F 'goals/README.md:27'` returns **four** and misses it, `git grep -l 'exactly one arc'` returns **seven** and finds it, and a reverse index on `[[goals/README]]` returns **seven** and finds it. **What let the carrier through is that the rule was corrected under its own key.** `docs/goals/README.md:27` now holds the amended sentence at the same path and the same line number, so every citation still resolves and no reachable check can see that the thing it resolves to changed meaning. The surveyed amendment pattern does the opposite move: RFC 2026 republishes with a new number and MADR's status field names the superseding id, which converts a stale citation into a live pointer at a document that is still true as of its own date. **For the 48-record case the survey yields one check and one refusal.** Doorstop's suspect link is the check, and it is the only surveyed thing that turns one rule edit into 48 flagged records. test262 is the refusal: its contributing guide discourages quoting the specification into a test at all, admits the quote will go unmaintained, and keeps the stable anchor while the executable body carries the meaning. **Nothing surveyed rewrites 48 quoted copies. `doorstop clear` clears the flag and leaves the text.** **The cheapest transferable piece needs no new format and no new parser**: `ledger-lint` check F already reads every `[[link]]` in the tree and walks it forward to prove it resolves, and the corpus holds **5,124 wikilink occurrences over 501 distinct targets**, so the reverse index answering "which documents cite this one" is one pass over what check F already parses.
- measured: **1. CASE 1 MEASURED IN THE TREE TODAY, AND THE THREE SEARCHES DISAGREE.** The live carrier is `docs/decisions/decision-primitive-with-consumer.md:125-128`, under `## What this does not rule on`, and its two citing spans sit on different lines: `:126` ends with `[[goals/README]]` and `:127` opens with `` `:27` makes an element belong to exactly one arc, so the seat has to be picked``. **Four searches, four answers, all run at `254d1d0` over tracked files.** `git grep -l 'belongs to exactly one arc'` returns 5 files (`.planning/BOUNDS-AUTHOR-CALLS.md`, `.planning/README-PLAN.md`, `docs/decisions/decision-four-lenses.md`, `records/author-calls.md`, `records/bounds-residue.md`) and the carrier is absent, because it inflects the verb. `git grep -l -F 'goals/README.md:27'` returns 4 files and the carrier is absent, because it spells the path as a wikilink and the line as a separate code span. `git grep -l 'exactly one arc'` returns 7 and the carrier is present. `git grep -l -F '[[goals/README]]'` returns 7 and the carrier is present. **The authority sentence at `docs/goals/README.md:27` now reads "An element belongs to at least one arc, or the unspoken lens says nobody has ruled on it", and `records/lenses/unspoken.md` carries that string 48 times.** The path and the line number did not move when the sentence was corrected.

  **2. FAMILY MARK: THE NORMATIVE SENTENCE IS LABELLED, AND THE LABEL DOES NOT ENUMERATE.** RFC 2119 restricts its own vocabulary, RFC2119:77 "Imperatives of the type defined in this memo must be used with care" RFC2119:78 "and sparingly.  In particular, they MUST only be used where it is" actually required for interoperation. RFC 8174 makes the label mechanical, RFC8174:94 "This document updates RFC 2119 by clarifying that only UPPERCASE" usage carries the defined meaning, and RFC8174:149 "The words have the meanings specified herein only when they are in" all capitals **The same document states the limit that decides this question**, RFC8174:144 "required.  Specifically, normative text does not require the use" RFC8174:145 "of these key words.  They are used for clarity and consistency" RFC8174:146 "when that is what's wanted, but a lot of normative text does not" RFC8174:147 "use them and is still normative." **MISSES Case 1.** The carrier's sentence carries no keyword and would carry none under the convention, and the IETF says unmarked prose is normative anyway. **Cost: one sentence of boilerplate per document, and no enumeration of what the rules are.**

  **3. FAMILY AMEND BY NEW IDENTITY: THE OLD TEXT IS NEVER EDITED, SO A CITATION TO IT STAYS TRUE.** RFC 2026 handles a correction by publishing again under a different number, RFC2026:1128 "cases, the IESG or RFC Editor may be asked to republish the RFC (with" RFC2026:1129 "a new number) with corrections, and this will not reset the minimum" clock, and a status change is itself a republication, RFC2026:1111 "Change of status shall result in republication of the specification". MADR carries the same move in a field, MADRTPL:3 `status: "{proposed | rejected | accepted | deprecated | … | superseded by ADR-0123}"`. **MISSES Case 1 alone and FIRES in a pair.** It cannot report anything by itself; it makes the report possible, because a citation to a superseded id is mechanically distinguishable where a citation to an edited line is not. **Cost: the corpus grows monotonically and the reader follows a chain.** **This is the exact move this tree did not make.** The correction rewrote `docs/goals/README.md:27` in place, which preserved every citation and destroyed the only signal.

  **4. FAMILY ONE ARTIFACT: THE PROSE AND THE CHECK ARE THE SAME TEXT.** doctest states the purpose, PYDOCTEST:15 "To check that a module's docstrings are up-to-date by verifying that all" PYDOCTEST:16 "interactive examples still work as documented." **CommonMark is the industrial version and is the closest surveyed object to a specification written for a person that is also a test suite.** CMSPEC:259 "It contains many examples with side-by-side Markdown and" CMSPEC:260 "HTML.  These are intended to double as conformance tests.  An" CMSPEC:261 "accompanying script `spec_tests.py` can be used to run the tests" against any Markdown program, and the README gives the count and the command, CMREADME:30 "[The spec] contains over 500 embedded examples which serve as conformance" CMREADME:33 "    python3 test/spec_tests.py --program $PROG". rustdoc runs the same loop over doc comments, RUSTDOCTEST:3 "`rustdoc` supports executing your documentation examples as tests." RUSTDOCTEST:4 "This makes sure that examples within your documentation are up to date and working." Gherkin puts the whole specification in the test file, GHERKINREF:155 "In addition to being a specification and documentation, an example is also a *test*. As a whole, your examples are an" GHERKINREF:156 "*executable specification* of the system." **MISSES Case 1, structurally.** The family removes the need for a second statement inside the authority. A different document reasoning about the rule is outside the artifact by construction. **Cost: it reaches only rules whose consequence is executable.** "An element belongs to at least one arc" has one, and `check_ae` is it; the paragraph reasoning about which seat a split pair takes does not.

  **5. FAMILY TRANSCLUDE: THE SECOND COPY IS A REFERENCE, AND THE SLICE KEY DECIDES WHETHER IT SURVIVES AN EDIT.** Sphinx includes a file's text into a document, SPHINXDIRS:928 "Longer displays of verbatim text may be included by storing the example text" SPHINXDIRS:929 "in an external file containing only plain text.  The file may be included" using the directive, and the drift-resistant slice is by content marker, SPHINXDIRS:1082 "Another way to control which part of the file is included is to use" SPHINXDIRS:1083 "the ``start-after`` and ``end-before`` options (or only one of them)." SPHINXDIRS:1085 "only lines that follow the first line containing that string are included." rustdoc transcludes a whole file and then tests it, RUSTDOCTEST:633 "#[doc = include_str!(\"../README.md\")]" with RUSTDOCTEST:638 "This will include your README as documentation on the hidden struct `ReadmeDoctests`, which will" then be tested. cog writes generated text into a hand-edited file and guards it, COGRUN:43 "Checksum the output to protect it against accidental" change, and refuses on a mismatch, COGRUN:207 "If the generated code is edited by a misguided developer, the next time cog" COGRUN:208 "is run, the checksum won't match, and cog will stop to avoid overwriting the" edited output, with the gate half separate, COGRUN:224 "You can use the ``--check`` option to run cog just to check that the files" would not change. **MISSES Case 1 and PREVENTS the copy.** A transcluded statement cannot drift because it is one statement. **Cost: the wording cannot be adapted to its context, and cog's guard makes the region unwritable by hand.** **The slice key is the transferable part.** Sphinx's `start-after` keys on text; this tree keys on line numbers, and holds **9,307 spans of the form `path.ext:NN` across 399 tracked markdown files**, every one of which renumbers when a line is inserted above it.

  **6. FAMILY REGISTRY AND COVERAGE: EVERY NORMATIVE ITEM GETS AN ID, AND FOUR SEPARATE CHECKS DEMAND THAT SOMETHING CITE IT.** rustc assigns the id and requires the prose, RUSTERRCODE:2 "We generally try to assign each error message a unique code like `E0123`." RUSTERRCODE:5 "All error codes have an associated explanation: new error codes must include them." The tidy check enumerates its own coverage, RUSTTIDYEC:1 "//! Tidy check to ensure error codes are properly documented and tested." RUSTTIDYEC:5 "//! 1. We create a list of error codes used by the compiler. Error codes are extracted from `compiler/rustc_error_codes/src/lib.rs`." RUSTTIDYEC:7 "//! 2. We check that the error code has a long-form explanation in `compiler/rustc_error_codes/src/error_codes/`." RUSTTIDYEC:8 "//!   - The explanation is expected to contain a `doctest` that fails with the correct error code." RUSTTIDYEC:11 "//! 3. We check that the error code has a UI test in `tests/ui/error-codes/`." RUSTTIDYEC:16 "//! 4. We check that the error code is actually emitted by the compiler." RUSTTIDYEC:17 "//!   - This is done by searching `compiler/` with a regex." Removal is guarded too, RUSTTIDYEC:42 "    // Check that no error code explanation was removed." Exemptions are two named constants carrying explicit lists, RUSTTIDYEC:32 "// Error codes that (for some reason) can't have a doctest in their explanation. Error codes are still expected to provide a code example, even if untested." and RUSTTIDYEC:35 "// Error codes that don't yet have a UI test. This list will eventually be removed." **FIRES on Case 1 only where the carrier emits the id**, which `E0123` does and a prose paraphrase does not. **The measured cost is visible inside this pin, and it is this row's question turned on the surveyed tool.** The docstring at RUSTTIDYEC:8 names `EXEMPT_FROM_DOCTEST` and RUSTTIDYEC:14 names `EXEMPTED_FROM_TEST`, while the file defines RUSTTIDYEC:33 "const IGNORE_DOCTEST_CHECK: &[&str] = &[\"E0464\", \"E0570\", \"E0601\", \"E0602\", \"E0717\"];" and RUSTTIDYEC:36 "const IGNORE_UI_TEST_CHECK: &[&str] =", and one user-facing message still prints the dead name, RUSTTIDYEC:261 "it shouldn't be listed in `EXEMPTED_FROM_TEST`!\"". **Three prose homes for two constants, two of them stale, inside the check written to stop exactly that.** The tool has no check on its own docstring.

  **7. FAMILY FINGERPRINT: THE CHILD STORES THE PARENT'S HASH, AND A PARENT EDIT MARKS EVERY CHILD.** Doorstop keeps each requirement as a file and validates the graph, DOORSTOP:19 "When a project leverages this tool, each linkable item (requirement, test case, etc.) is stored as a YAML file in a designated directory." The hash covers the rule's own text, DOORSTOPITEM:177 "Each item has a fingerprint. By default, the UID of the item, the values of the" DOORSTOPITEM:178 "[text](item.md#text) and [ref](item.md#ref) attributes, and the UIDs of the" links attribute contribute to it. A citation carries the hash, DOORSTOPITEM:203 "A link consists of two parts, the parent item UID and the" DOORSTOPITEM:204 "[fingerprint](item.md#reviewed) of the parent when it last reviewed. If the" link has not been reviewed the fingerprint is null, and the purpose is stated plainly, DOORSTOPITEM:222 "The link fingerprint is used by Doorstop to detect when a parent item is" DOORSTOPITEM:223 "changed, as a convenience to the writer since such change may also affect its" DOORSTOPITEM:224 "children." Validation reports it, DOORSTOPVAL:81 "Each link consists of the parent item UID and the" DOORSTOPVAL:83 "fingerprint of a parent item changes, the link is reported as suspect during" DOORSTOPVAL:84 "validation." **The second half is the coverage rule that reaches an undeclared carrier**, DOORSTOPVAL:43 "* A normative, non-derived item in a child document has no links." with the reverse direction available on a flag, DOORSTOPVAL:55 "To confirm that every item in a document links to its parents:" DOORSTOPVAL:58 "$ doorstop --strict-child-check", and one explicit exemption, DOORSTOPITEM:92 "Doorstop does not expect parent links on derived items." **FIRES on Case 1, twice.** The carrier's stored hash of the authority would no longer match, and had it declared no link at all the non-derived rule reports it. **FIRES on the 48-record case, and it is the only surveyed mechanism that does**: one edit to the authority flags all 48 at once. **Cost, and it is high.** Every carrier becomes a YAML item with a declared parent; a person clears each flag, DOORSTOPVAL:102 "You can clear suspect links with the `doorstop clear` command."; and the source itself calls the signal advisory at DOORSTOPITEM:222-224, "as a convenience to the writer". **Clearing a flag does not correct the quoted copy.**

  **8. FAMILY REFUSE THE COPY: KEEP A STABLE KEY AND ADMIT THE QUOTE IS DECORATION.** test262 is a body of records that each quote a rule as the premise of their own measurement, which is Case 2's shape at scale. Its key is required, TEST262CON:114 " - [**esid**](#esid) (required for new tests)" and it is an anchor, TEST262CON:138 "This key is required for all new feature tests. This key identifies the hash ID from the portion of the ECMAScript draft which is most recent to the date the test was added. It represents the anchors on the generated HTML version of the specs." **The quoted copy is discouraged in the project's own words**, TEST262CON:145 "This allows a long, free-form comment. The comment is almost always a direct" TEST262CON:146 "quote from ECMAScript. It is used to indicate the observable being tested" TEST262CON:147 "within the file. Its use is not encouraged in new tests except where the content" TEST262CON:148 "is deemed important for understanding them (and even then, be mindful that it is" TEST262CON:149 "unlikely to be updated for editorial changes of upstream specification text)." **The positional key was deprecated for precisely the reason this tree's `path:NN` citations have**, TEST262CON:254 "This key identifies the section number from the portion of the ES6 standard that is tested by this test _at the time the test was written_. The es6ids might not correspond to the correction section numbers in the ES6 (or later) specification because routine edits to the specification will change section numbering. For this reason, only the esid is required for new tests." and the migration is stated as in progress, TEST262CON:44 "**Note** The project is currently transitioning from a naming system based on specification section numbers." **MISSES Case 1's carrier and dissolves the 48-record case.** A test's premise is re-derived by running it, so a stale prose copy costs nothing. **Cost: the record's meaning has to live somewhere executable.** The 48 rows here measure that no arc names an element, which `check_ae` computes, so the premise is already executable in this tree and the quoted copy is already decoration.

  **9. FAMILY DANGLING REFERENCE, AND THIS TREE ALREADY HAS IT.** Sphinx fails on an unresolvable target, SPHINXCONF:945 "Enables nitpicky mode if :code-py:`True`." SPHINXCONF:946 "In nitpicky mode, Sphinx will warn about *all* references" SPHINXCONF:947 "where the target cannot be found." Doorstop raises the same condition to an error, DOORSTOPVAL:50 "* An item's link is an invalid or unknown UID." **MISSES Case 1.** The carrier's `[[goals/README]]` resolves correctly and always did. `ledger-lint` check F is this mechanism, at `tools/ledger-lint/ledger-lint.py:315-364`, and it walks links forward only.

  **10. FAMILY SCHEMA-FIRST, WHICH LOOKS LIKE AN ANSWER AND IS NOT ONE.** Putting prose next to the machine-readable rule does not bind them. JSON Schema separates the two behaviours outright, JSONSCHVAL:70 "Keywords which are not defined to exhibit a particular behavior MUST NOT affect" JSONSCHVAL:71 "that aspect of the evaluation outcome. In particular, the keywords defined in" JSONSCHVAL:72 "{{annotations}} produce no assertion result and therefore are not considered" during validation, and `description` is one of them, JSONSCHVAL:680 "These general-purpose annotation keywords provide commonly used information for" JSONSCHVAL:681 "documentation and user interface display purposes." **MISSES.** A `description` sits beside the rule and binds nothing. One that contradicts its own schema validates.

  **11. THE MISSED-CARRIER TEST, MECHANISM BY MECHANISM.** **FIRES as shipped: Doorstop, on the stored fingerprint (item 7).** **FIRES in a pair the tree lacks: amendment by new identity joined to a dangling-reference check (items 3 and 9), because a retired id makes a stale citation reportable where an edited line does not; and a registry coverage rule over the citation key (item 6), which fires because the carrier does emit `[[goals/README]]`.** **MISSES: RFC 2119 and RFC 8174 keyword marking, and the source says why.** **MISSES: doctest, CommonMark, rustdoc doctests, Gherkin**, four one-artifact mechanisms, all four for the same structural reason. **MISSES: Sphinx transclusion, `include_str!`, cog**, which prevent a copy without detecting a paraphrase. **MISSES: Sphinx nitpicky and Doorstop's unknown-UID error**, because the link resolves. **MISSES: test262's key**, which ships no check of its own. **MISSES: JSON Schema annotations.** **Fourteen of fifteen miss, and the pattern behind the count is uniform: a mechanism fires when the carrier emits a key the tool indexes, and prose reasoning emits none by default.**

  **12. WHAT IS TRANSFERABLE HERE, WITH ITS PRICE, AND TWO PIECES ARE ALREADY BUILT.** **The reverse index is the cheap one and it needs no new format.** Check F at `tools/ledger-lint/ledger-lint.py:315-364` already parses every `[[link]]` and resolves it forward; the corpus holds 5,124 wikilink occurrences over 501 distinct targets, so "which documents cite this one" is one pass over the same parse, and on Case 1 it returns seven files including the missed carrier. **Price: it reports citers and rules nothing about whether a citer went stale.** **The content check is the second built piece and its granularity is the open question.** Check R at `:477` already tests that "A line citation lands on the symbol it is cited FOR", which is Doorstop's fingerprint idea restricted to code symbols, and check AI is keyed on a whole file, so a record goes unverified when any cited file changes for any reason. **Doorstop's unit is the item.** Moving from file-keyed to item-keyed is the difference between 48 rows staling because `docs/goals/README.md` moved and 48 rows staling because the sentence they quote moved. **Price: a fingerprint has to be stored and cleared, and `records/README.md` already carries the discipline for the clearing half in its rule that a state change without a fresh `checked:` date is a guess.** **The stable anchor is the third, and the records tier already has one.** `records/README.md` designs the row heading so "the heading anchor never moves", which is `esid`; the prose documents have no such id, and the tree's 9,307 `path:NN` citations are `es6id`, deprecated at its own source for the same reason. **Price: every normative sentence a document wants cited has to be given a name.** **Doorstop's corpus does not transfer.** Its items are YAML files and this tree's documents are written for a person to read, which `docs/decisions/decision-ai-tier.md` makes the point of the doc tier. **The one-artifact family does not transfer for this rule.** The executable consequence is already single-sourced in `check_ae`; the part that drifted is the reasoning, and no surveyed mechanism executes reasoning. **What the generated-section pattern does not reach, stated against cog rather than against this tree's own tool**: COGRUN:207-208 makes a generated region refuse a hand edit, so an explanation that a person needs to extend in place cannot live in one.

  **13. WHAT THE SOURCES DO NOT SETTLE, AND WHAT WAS NOT REACHED.** **No surveyed mechanism catches an uncited paraphrase, and none of the fifteen claims to.** The three that fire all require a declared key, so the honest statement of the finding is that single-sourcing a rule is enforced by making a second copy impossible or by making every carrier declare itself, and neither is detection. **No source prices the retrofit.** Doorstop assumes items authored as YAML from the start and says nothing about converting an existing prose corpus; test262's own migration off section numbers is described as unfinished at TEST262CON:44 with no cost attached. **No source measures the false-alarm rate of a suspect link**, and Doorstop's own wording at DOORSTOPITEM:222-224 calls it a convenience to the writer, which is advisory language. That is the cost that would fall hardest here, because 9,307 positional citations fingerprinted at line granularity would flag on every insertion above them. **The constitutional half is thin and this row does not lean on it.** RFC 2026 and the MADR template are the only pinned amendment sources, neither states a prohibition on restatement, and no legal code was pinned, so this row makes no claim about what legal drafting forbids. **The prose-linter family was looked at and not pinned.** Vale's README was fetched at `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/errata-ai/vale/v3/README.md` and carries marketing copy with no statement of what a rule can match; its rule reference lives on a docs site this run did not retrieve, so this row makes no claim about what a term lint can express. **Nothing here speaks to the formal-specification half**, which is the same limit FD-27 recorded: the TLA+ and Alloy specification-to-implementation drift literature is in PDFs and this sandbox has no `pdftotext`. **One local number is softer than it looks.** The 5,124 wikilink occurrences and 9,307 `path:NN` spans are regex counts over tracked markdown and include spans inside code fences, which `prose-lint` strips and these counts do not.
