Live tree spans, measured 2026-09-13 at `254d1d0`: `docs/decisions/decision-primitive-with-consumer.md:125-128`, `docs/goals/README.md:27` and `:32`, `records/lenses/unspoken.md` (48 occurrences of the amended sentence), `records/lenses/problems.md:1203-1205` (PRB-85), `records/README.md` (row format, heading anchor, `checked:` rule), `tools/ledger-lint/ledger-lint.py:315-364` (check F), `:374-382` (`PATHSPAN`), `:413-421` (check G), `:477-485` (check R), `:2117-2135` (check AI). Commands re-runnable from the root: `git grep -l 'belongs to exactly one arc'` returns 5 paths; `git grep -l -F 'goals/README.md:27'` returns 4; `git grep -l 'exactly one arc'` returns 7; `git grep -l -F '[[goals/README]]' -- '*.md'` returns 7; `git grep -c -F 'An element belongs to at least one arc' records/lenses/unspoken.md` returns 48; `git grep -o -E '\[\[[a-zA-Z0-9/_-]+\]\]' -- '*.md' | wc -l` returns 5124 and the same piped through `sort -u` returns 501; `git grep -o -E '[a-zA-Z0-9/_.-]+\.(md|py|chiral|sh):[0-9]+' -- '*.md' | wc -l` returns 9307 over 399 files. `ledger-lint` measured before and after this row at 145 violations, 99 element homes owed, 27 author calls owed, 173 lens rulings owed, 2 checks that checked nothing.
- checked:  2026-09-13
- element:  `none`. This row surveys and prices; it designs nothing. It leaves four things in the author's hands. Whether a corrected rule keeps its citation key or takes a new one, which is the single decision that separated this tree's repair from every surveyed amendment pattern. Whether the `[[link]]` graph check F already parses gains a reverse direction, which is the cheapest surveyed catch and the only one that finds Case 1's carrier without a new format. Whether a citation stores a fingerprint of what it cites, and at what granularity, given that check AI is keyed on a file and check R is keyed on a code symbol while Doorstop keys on the item. Whether a body of records keeps quoting the rule it measures against, given that test262 discourages the quote outright and the premise of the 48 rows is already computed by `check_ae`.

## Keeping one register of what a human still owes

### FD-29 no surveyed project runs one register, all eight reconcile several by a shared id, the only family that reaches a ruling given out of band is a procedural MUST with no check behind it, and this tree's own register file holds three settled rulings its own check cannot see

- state:    OPEN
- claim:    The question: how does a project keep one register of decisions owed by a human, so that a question needing an answer never lives only where no tool looks, and so that an answer once given reaches every carrier that was waiting on it? **Eight mechanisms were read at their own sources. Fourteen pins added, every one raw, four earlier pins reused.** **No surveyed project keeps one register.** Every one runs several at different granularities and binds them with a shared identifier plus a validated or generated view, so the cost of collapsing registers is a price no surveyed project pays. **The families are eight.** **CLOSED TOKEN VALIDATED BY A TOOL**: Kubernetes `kep.yaml` `status:` against a Go enum, PEP `Status` against `ALL_STATUSES`, MADR's status field, and this tree's check AK. **KEYED PROSE MARK**: ruff's flake8-todos, where a `TODO` must carry an author and an issue link, beside Kubernetes' `<<[UNRESOLVED]>>` delimiter, which ships the marker with no checker behind it. **CONFIRM AN OUT-OF-BAND DECISION IN THE DURABLE MEDIUM**: RFC 2418's MUST, and Rust's meeting summary posted back to the pull request. **PERMALINK INTO THE EPHEMERAL MEDIUM**: PEP's `Resolution:` header, a URL at the post that made the decision, form-checked. **NAMED BLOCKING CONCERN**: rfcbot, where a concern is an object with a name, blocks the gate, and is resolvable only by whoever raised it. **WRITE BOTH DIRECTIONS AT THE MOMENT OF THE ANSWER**: adr-tools' `adr link` and `adr new -s`, and PEP's `Superseded-By`/`Replaces` pair. **FINGERPRINT**: Doorstop, reused from FD-28. **ERRATUM STATE MACHINE**: the IESG's three states, whose middle state records a ruling and defers its propagation deliberately. **Applied to the two local cases the split is one-sided.** **§UNSORTED, a question stated in a document's prose where the register's check could not see it, is caught by two of the eight.** The keyed prose mark fires when the mark is a directive the linter enumerates, and Doorstop's coverage rule fires on a normative item that declares no parent. Six miss. **The unrecorded in-session ruling is caught by no surveyed check and by one surveyed obligation.** RFC 2418 and the Rust RFC README both say a decision taken off the record MUST be brought back onto it; neither ships anything that fires when it is not, and neither states a consequence. **That asymmetry is the finding.** Discovery and propagation both reduce to the carrier emitting a key a tool indexes, which is FD-28's result unchanged, and the moment a human speaks emits no key at all, so every surveyed defence there is a rule addressed to a person. **The tiered-repository question has a surveyed answer, and this tree's premise for asking it is false.** The `.planning/` files holding dated author rulings are tracked, `docs/decisions/decision-ai-tier.md:28` heads a section reading `Both tiers are tracked`, and two arcs cite `.planning/DISPLAY-LAYER-GAP.md` already. **The measured defect is one register file carrying two row shapes.** `records/author-calls.md:401-405` records the three display directives of 2026-09-04 as a two-column table with no state cell, and the file says at `:396-399` that they are settled, that none of them holds a row in the table above, and that a directive kept only in a session transcript is lost; check AK's regex reads the token in the first cell, so it sees 40 of the file's 73 body rows and none of those three. **A ruling filed in the register, inside the register's own file, is invisible to the register's own check.** The surveyed answer to a tiered repository is PEP's: leave the ruling where it was made, require the tracked row to carry a form-checked pointer at a permanent copy, and make the pointer a required header. **The measured price of that answer sits inside the same pin.** PEP 1 requires `Resolution` for Standards Track PEPs, `check-peps.py` omits it from `REQUIRED_HEADERS`, and the validator it does run checks the pointer's shape and never its content.
- measured: **1. THE TWO LOCAL CASES, MEASURED TODAY AT `1f01e03`.** **§UNSORTED.** `docs/elements/catalog.md:578-592` has carried six `?` rows since `8d8ddb6` (2026-08-31), saying at `:582` that "Every one of these needs one word from the author". It reached `records/author-calls.md` on 2026-09-10 in `838c311`, whose own row at `:368` states the cause: they "lived only in that section, where `ledger-lint` check AK could not see them". Ten days. **The unrecorded ruling.** `records/homing-triage.md:257` asks whether a `superseded` element is exempt from homing. The author answered in session, the session did not write it down, and the next agent refused to act on an unrecorded ruling and reported 100 element homes owed; `records/lenses/problems.md:1176` carries both halves, the refusal and the amendment, and the register row now stands at `records/author-calls.md:370` with the count at 99. **The reverse direction.** `docs/goals/README.md:27` now reads "An element belongs to at least one arc, or the unspoken lens says nobody has", and `docs/decisions/decision-primitive-with-consumer.md:125-128` still leaves a question open on the ground that `[[goals/README]]` `:27` "makes an element belong to exactly one arc". The answer arrived and one carrier waiting on it never heard.

  **2. THE THREE REGISTERS AND THEIR SIZES, COUNTED WITH EACH TOOL'S OWN PARSER.** `records/author-calls.md` holds 40 rows check AK can read: 27 `unreviewed`, 10 `ruled`, 3 `dissolved`. The four lens files hold 183 rows: 173 `unreviewed`, 10 `ruled`. The ⚑ mark has no parser: 2,323 occurrences on 1,923 lines across 288 tracked markdown files, of which 227 lines name the author and 93 end in a question mark, and ⚑ in this tree marks corrections and amendments as well as owed questions, so the count bounds the third register from above and does not measure it. **Both register schemas are shaped like their own rows.** A reader's obvious grep over `author-calls.md` returns 43 tokens and the check returns 40, because the legend at `:26-28` spells the three tokens in a table; the same grep over `records/lenses/` returns 174 and the check returns 173, because `records/lenses/README.md:52` spells the axis as an example.

  **3. FAMILY CLOSED TOKEN, VALIDATED BY A TOOL: THE REGISTER IS EXACTLY AS WIDE AS THE FILE IT READS.** Kubernetes puts the state in machine-readable metadata beside the prose, KEPYAML:9 "status: provisional|implementable|implemented|deferred|rejected|withdrawn|replaced", and validates it against an enumerated Go type, KEPAPI:65 "ProvisionalStatus   Status =" through the seven constants, with KEPAPI:84 "func (s Status) IsValid() error {" refusing anything else at KEPAPI:90 "invalid status: %v". The same file makes two fields mandatory, KEPAPI:112 "Approvers         []string" and KEPAPI:116 "Status            Status", both tagged required. **The template ships the owed state as literal text**, KEPYAML:11 "reviewers:" and KEPYAML:14 "approvers:" each carrying a `TBD` entry. PEP does the same over an rst header block: CHECKPEPS:57 "REQUIRED_HEADERS = frozenset(" fixes six mandatory headers and a closed status set is checked against them. MADR carries the state in one field, MADRTPL:3 "superseded by ADR-0123". **MISSES §UNSORTED and MISSES the in-session ruling, both for one reason.** The check reads the register file; a question living in another document, or in nobody's file, emits no row. **Cost: the enumeration reaches only what was already filed, and the gap between the prose rule and the checked rule goes unreported.** PEP 1 requires the resolution pointer, PEP1:673 "The Resolution header is required for Standards Track PEPs" only, and `check-peps.py` leaves it out of CHECKPEPS:57, so an Accepted Standards Track PEP with no `Resolution` passes the register's own checker.

  **4. FAMILY KEYED PROSE MARK: THE MARK IS ALLOWED, AND IT MUST CARRY A KEY.** ruff requires an owner, RUFFTODO:49 "Checks that a TODO comment includes an author.", and a link into the tracker, RUFFTODO:78 "Checks that a TODO comment is associated with a link to a relevant issue" or ticket, with the reason stated as the register's reason, RUFFTODO:82 "Including an issue link near a TODO makes it easier for resolvers" to get context, and the diagnostic raised at RUFFTODO:319 "report_diagnostic_if_enabled(MissingTodoLink, directive.range)". **The reach is the directive set and the source says so**, RUFFTODO:274 "flake8-todos doesn't support HACK directives." Kubernetes ships the delimiter without the linter, KEPTPL:40 "Just because a KEP is merged does not mean it is complete or approved. Any KEP" marked provisional is a working document, KEPTPL:42 "denote sections that are under active debate as follows:", KEPTPL:45 "<<[UNRESOLVED optional short context or usernames ]>>" closed by KEPTPL:47 "<<[/UNRESOLVED]>>"; the pinned validator enumerates statuses and names no such marker. **FIRES on §UNSORTED, conditionally, and it is the cheapest thing here that does.** A TD003-shaped rule demands that a mark carry a link into the register, which converts "needs one word from the author" into a resolvable citation or a finding. **The condition is that the mark be a directive a tool enumerates.** This tree's ⚑ is a character, 2,323 of them, read by nothing. **MISSES the in-session ruling: a ruling never written emits no mark.** **Cost: every mark costs one register entry, and a question phrased as ordinary prose carries no directive and stays invisible.**

  **5. FAMILY CONFIRM OUT OF BAND, AND IT IS THE ONLY FAMILY THAT REACHES A VERBAL RULING.** The IETF makes it a MUST, RFC2418:625 "participants.  Decisions reached during a face-to-face meeting about" RFC2418:626 "topics or issues which have not been discussed on the mailing list," or significantly different from prior list consensus RFC2418:628 "consensus MUST be reviewed on the mailing list." The chair owns the durable copy, RFC2418:911 "The Chair should make sure that discussions on the list are" RFC2418:912 "summarized and that the outcome is well documented (to avoid" repetition). Rust states the same rule twice, RUSTRFC:127 "comment thread of the pull request itself. Offline discussion will be" RUSTRFC:128 "summarized on the pull request comment thread." and again for meetings, RUSTRFC:198 "case a summary from the meeting will be posted back to the RFC pull request." **FIRES on the in-session ruling as an obligation and MISSES it as a detector.** Both sources state the rule and neither carries a check, a consequence, or a measurement of compliance. **MISSES §UNSORTED.** **Cost: nothing to build, and nothing mechanical bought.** **This is the honest centre of the answer.** A moment when a person speaks emits no artifact, so no tool can index it, and every surveyed defence at that moment is a rule addressed to whoever was listening.

  **6. FAMILY PERMALINK: BIND THE TRACKED ROW TO A PERMANENT COPY OF THE RULING WHEREVER IT WAS MADE.** PEP 1 makes the update an obligation of the state change, PEP1:439 "When a PEP is Accepted, Rejected or Withdrawn, the PEP should be updated" accordingly, and PEP1:441 "the Resolution header should be added with a direct link" PEP1:442 "to the relevant post making a decision on the PEP." The checker validates the shape, CHECKPEPS:421 "must be a direct thread/message URL or a link with a date." The register also names the live discussion and the archive of past ones through `Discussions-To` and `Post-History`. **This is the surveyed answer to a decision recorded in a tier the tracked tier depends on: neither copy the ruling nor leave it, but require a form-checked pointer at an archived copy.** **FIRES on the in-session ruling only where the medium has an addressable archive.** A session transcript nobody can link falls outside it, which is exactly the gap PRB-84's amendment note records. **MISSES §UNSORTED.** **Cost, measured in the pin: the checker tests the pointer's shape. Nothing tests that the target exists or that it says what the row claims, and the register's own checker leaves out the header PEP 1 requires.**

  **7. FAMILY NAMED BLOCKING CONCERN: THE QUESTION BECOMES AN OBJECT WITH AN OWNER AND A GATE.** rfcbot registers the concern by command, RFCBOT:132 "To register blocking concerns on the FCP proposal, use" `@rfcbot concern NAME_OF_CONCERN`, which adds it to the list of concerns in the tracking comment; the canonical list is generated, RFCBOT:118 "rfcbot will create a tracking comment with a checklist of review requests" that reports when every request is satisfied and every concern resolved; and ownership of the answer is enforced, RFCBOT:134 "only the original author can mark their concern as resolved." The gate it blocks is a real one, RUSTRFC:135 "At some point, a member of the subteam will propose a" motion for final comment period, and RUSTRFC:150 "Before actually entering FCP, *all* members of the subteam must sign off;". **MISSES both local cases: a concern enters by a command and a question in prose issues none.** **Cost, and it is the inverse of this tree's failure**, RFCBOT:124 "This will delete all records of the FCP, including any concerns raised" while their comments remain. The prose survives and the register is discarded. **One more measured limit: a feedback request resolves on the fact of a reply**, RFCBOT:140 "Note that these feedback requests will not block start/end of an FCP." which detects that a person spoke and never that they answered.

  **8. FAMILY WRITE BOTH DIRECTIONS, WHICH IS THE WHOLE PROPAGATION ANSWER OUTSIDE DOORSTOP.** adr-tools edits the superseded record in the same command that writes the superseding one, ADRTOOLS:41 "This will create a new ADR file that is flagged as superceding" ADRTOOLS:42 "ADR 9, and changes the status of ADR 9 to indicate that it is" ADRTOOLS:43 "superceded by the new ADR." The general form takes both link texts as arguments, ADRLINK:7 "Creates a link between two ADRs, from SOURCE to TARGET new." ADRLINK:9 "LINK is the description of the link created in the SOURCE." ADRLINK:10 "REVERSE-LINK is the description of the link created in the TARGET", and the script body at ADRLINK:22-23 calls the same helper twice with the ends swapped. PEP requires the pair in both documents, PEP1:707 "PEPs may also have a Superseded-By header indicating that a PEP has" been rendered obsolete, PEP1:709 "the PEP that replaces the current document.  The newer PEP must have a" PEP1:710 "Replaces header containing the number of the PEP that it rendered" obsolete. **Every one of these is a write-time action with no check behind it.** adr-tools ships no verifier of a link in its pinned bytes, and `check-peps.py` tests only that a reference is well formed, CHECKPEPS:351 "must be 'NNN' PEP IDs", so a `Superseded-By` whose target carries no `Replaces` passes. **Applied to this tree's reverse failure, this family is the one that would have fixed it and it would have needed a person to aim it.** An `adr link`-shaped move at the moment the author overturned "exactly one arc" edits `decision-primitive-with-consumer.md` in the same commit; nothing finds the carrier, and the operator names it.

  **9. FAMILY FINGERPRINT, CARRIED OVER FROM FD-28 AND STILL THE ONLY AUTOMATIC PROPAGATION.** Doorstop flags every child when the parent's text changes, DOORSTOPITEM:222 "The link fingerprint is used by Doorstop to detect when a parent item is" changed, reported at validation, DOORSTOPVAL:83 "fingerprint of a parent item changes, the link is reported as suspect during" validation. **The coverage half reaches an undeclared carrier and is the second of the two mechanisms that catch §UNSORTED**, DOORSTOPVAL:43 "A normative, non-derived item in a child document has no links." A document stating a question and linking to no register is reported for the link's absence. **MISSES the in-session ruling.** **Cost is FD-28's and unchanged: every carrier becomes an item with a declared parent, a person clears each flag, and clearing corrects nothing.**

  **10. FAMILY ERRATUM STATE MACHINE: A THIRD STATE THIS TREE'S TWO REGISTERS DO NOT HAVE.** A question against a published document becomes an object with a closed state set, IESGERRATA:861 "The reviewer will classify the erratum as falling under one of the following states:" and the three are IESGERRATA:863 "The erratum is appropriate under the criteria below and should be available to implementers" for Verified, a Rejected state that pushes the change to a new document, IESGERRATA:864 "proposes a significant change to the RFC that should be done by publishing a new RFC that replaces or updates the current one", and the middle one, IESGERRATA:865 "The erratum is not a necessary update to the RFC." **Hold for Document Update records a ruling and defers its propagation on purpose, and names the deferral.** `ruled` in `author-calls.md` and `ruled <date>` on a lens row each say the answer exists and neither says whether the carriers were updated. `decision-primitive-with-consumer.md:125-128` is a ruling sitting in that state with nothing recording that it is. The escape hatch is the same amendment-by-new-identity move FD-28 measured, RFC2026:1128 "cases, the IESG or RFC Editor may be asked to republish the RFC (with" a new number. **Cost: the register grows and the document stays wrong, so a reader consults two places forever.**

  **11. THE MULTIPLE-REGISTER QUESTION, WHICH IS THE ONE THE SURVEY ANSWERS MOST CLEANLY.** **No surveyed project mandates one register, and none was found that collapsed several into one.** Kubernetes runs three at once, a prose README, a machine-readable `kep.yaml`, and a GitHub issue, and reconciles them by making the directory name the issue number, KEPPROC:64 "KEPs are now prefixed with their associated tracking issue number. This gives" KEPPROC:65 "both the KEP a unique identifier and provides an easy breadcrumb for people to" KEPPROC:66 "find the issue where the current state of the KEP is being updated.", with the issue mandated first, KEPTPL:16 "Create an issue in kubernetes/enhancements". The stated purpose is the discoverability half of this question, KEPPROC:41 "This will lead to more structured decisions that stick as there is a discoverable record around the decisions." PEP does the same with the PEP number spanning the document, the discussion thread and the resolution post. Rust splits its questions by deadline rather than by subject, RUSTRFCTPL:77 "## Unresolved questions" whose own prompts are RUSTRFCTPL:80 "What parts of the design do you expect to resolve through the RFC process before this gets merged?" and RUSTRFCTPL:81 "What parts of the design do you expect to resolve through the implementation of this feature before stabilization?", with the after-merge half moving to a tracking issue and a note added to the original document for anything larger, RUSTRFC:187 "should be new RFCs, with a note added to the original RFC. Exactly what counts" as minor being left to the subteam. **That split is the one this tree already has**: `author-calls.md` holds forks blocking a pass now, and the lens `author:` axis holds standing forks on recorded defects. **The honest price of the shared-id route is stated by Kubernetes against itself**, KEPPROC:58 "Presubmit checks for KEPs around metadata format and markdown validity." appears in the list of things still wanted before the process graduates out of beta, so the key is cheap and checking it is the part not yet done. **The generated-view half already exists here**: check AN names `python3 tools/lens/lens.py author` in its own message at `tools/ledger-lint/ledger-lint.py:2552`, which is rfcbot's tracking comment computed on demand instead of posted.

  **12. WHAT IS TRANSFERABLE, WITH ITS PRICE, AND ONE PIECE COSTS A REGEX.** **The cheapest is making the register's own file legible to its own check.** `records/author-calls.md:401-405` holds three settled rulings in a shape check AK cannot parse, in the file check AK reads, and the file states the intent at `:399`: a directive kept only in a session transcript is lost. **Price: one more row shape, or one more token.** **The second is the keyed mark**, ruff's rule applied to ⚑: a mark that claims the author owes something carries a citation into a register, and a mark that carries none is a finding. **Price: 2,323 marks get triaged once, and only marks that use the character are ever seen.** **The third is the propagation state.** The IESG's middle state separates "the answer exists" from "the carriers were updated", which is the distinction `ruled` currently hides and the distinction `decision-primitive-with-consumer.md` fell through. **Price: a `ruled` row gains a second axis and somebody closes it.** **The fourth is the permalink, and it is the answer to the tier question.** A ruling given in session gets a tracked row whose pointer names a file and a span, form-checked the way `check-peps.py` checks a thread URL. **Price: the checker validates the shape and never the content, which the pinned tool demonstrates by requiring neither.** **What does not transfer is the same thing FD-28 found: nothing detects an unfiled question or an unspoken ruling, and the three families that fire all require a declared key.**

  **13. WHAT THE SOURCES DO NOT SETTLE, AND WHAT WAS NOT REACHED.** **No surveyed source measures compliance with the confirm-out-of-band rule**, which is the one family that reaches the verbal-ruling case, so the mechanism this tree most needs is unmeasured everywhere it was found. **No source prices a retrofit** onto a corpus of prose documents, the same limit FD-28 recorded for Doorstop. **The requirements-tooling half is thin and this row leans on none of it.** DOORS and Jama are proprietary and their documentation sits behind product sites this run did not retrieve; RAID logs and decision logs have no normative source that was found; Doorstop is the only requirements tool pinned, and its pins come from FD-28. **The IETF erratum state machine is pinned from an IESG statement page rather than from an RFC.** `https://www.rfc-editor.org/errata-definitions.php` returned HTTP 404 on 2026-09-13, so the states are quoted from the stream's processing statement and this row makes no claim about the other streams. **No W3C source was pinned**, so this row says nothing about formal objections, wide review, or W3C errata management. **TC39 was fetched and not pinned.** `tc39/how-we-work/main/champion.md` describes a stage tracking issue as something that "can be useful", which is advisory, and `normative-pr.md` returned HTTP 404 at that path, so no claim is made about TC39. **One local number is soft.** The 2,323 ⚑ count is a regex over tracked markdown including code fences, and the mark carries corrections and amendments alongside owed questions, so 227 author-naming lines and 93 question-mark lines are filters and not a count. **One premise in this run's own prompt was measured false and is corrected above**: the `.planning/` files carrying dated author rulings are tracked, and `docs/arcs/display-calculus-arc.md` and `docs/arcs/terminal-arc.md` cite `.planning/DISPLAY-LAYER-GAP.md` today.
- evidence: **Fourteen pins added in `.planning/sources/`, every one origin `raw`. `xlat pin` stamps a UTC clock, so each `.meta` reads `pinned: 2026-09-14` while the tree spans below were measured on 2026-09-13 local.** `ADRTOOLS` (`npryce/adr-tools/master/README.md`, 55 lines), `ADRLINK` (`npryce/adr-tools/master/src/adr-link`, 23), `RUSTRFC` (`rust-lang/rfcs/master/README.md`, 274), `RUSTRFCTPL` (`rust-lang/rfcs/master/0000-template.md`, 103), `RFCBOT` (`rust-lang/rfcbot-rs/master/README.md`, 146), `PEP1` (`python/peps/main/peps/pep-0001.rst`, 902), `CHECKPEPS` (`python/peps/main/check-peps.py`, 609), `RFC2418` (`rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc2418.txt`, 1,459), `KEPPROC` (`kubernetes/enhancements/master/keps/README.md`, 72), `KEPTPL` (`keps/NNNN-kep-template/README.md`, 830), `KEPYAML` (`keps/NNNN-kep-template/kep.yaml`, 51), `KEPAPI` (`kubernetes/enhancements/master/api/proposal.go`, 256), `RUFFTODO` (`astral-sh/ruff/main/crates/ruff_linter/src/rules/flake8_todos/rules/todos.rs`, 406), `IESGERRATA` (`ietf.org/about/groups/iesg/statements/processing-errata-ietf-stream/`, 948, served as HTML and pinned as the bytes served). **Four pins reused from FD-27 and FD-28**: `MADRTPL`, `DOORSTOPITEM`, `DOORSTOPVAL` and `RFC2026`. Two fetches came back 404 and are named in item 13. Live tree spans, measured 2026-09-13 at `1f01e03`: `records/author-calls.md:19-28` (the legend), `:50` (the table header), `:368` (the §UNSORTED row), `:370` (the superseded-homing ruling), `:394-405` (the display directives, stated at `:396-399` and tabled at `:401-405` with no state cell), `docs/elements/catalog.md:578-592`, `records/homing-triage.md:257`, `records/lenses/problems.md:1176` (PRB-84's refusal and its amendment), `records/lenses/README.md:52` and `:93-109` (the author axis), `docs/goals/README.md:27`, `docs/decisions/decision-primitive-with-consumer.md:125-128`, `docs/decisions/decision-ai-tier.md:28`, `tools/ledger-lint/ledger-lint.py:2289` (`_AUTHOR_CALL`), `:2293-2332` (check AK), `:2490-2552` (check AN). Commands re-runnable from the root: `git grep -o '⚑' -- '*.md' | wc -l` returns 2323, `git grep -c '⚑' -- '*.md'` reports 288 files whose per-file line counts sum to 1923, and `git grep -n '⚑' -- '*.md' | grep -ci author` returns 227; the token table in `author-calls.md` parses to 27 `unreviewed`, 10 `ruled` and 3 `dissolved` under `_AUTHOR_CALL`, against 43 for `grep -oE '^\| *`(unreviewed|ruled|dissolved)`' records/author-calls.md | wc -l`; `_lens_rows` over the four lens files returns 86, 24, 22 and 51 rows with 173 `unreviewed`, against 174 for `grep -hoE '^- author: +unreviewed' records/lenses/*.md | wc -l`, which also reads `records/lenses/README.md:52`; `git grep -l -F 'DISPLAY-LAYER-GAP' -- docs records` returns six paths including two arcs. `ledger-lint` measured before and after this row at 145 violations, 99 element homes owed, 27 author calls owed, 173 lens rulings owed, 2 checks that checked nothing.
- checked:  2026-09-13
- element:  `none`. This row surveys and prices; it designs nothing. It leaves four things in the author's hands. Whether `records/author-calls.md`'s settled-directive tables gain a token, given that three rulings sit in the register's own file where the register's own check cannot read them. Whether ⚑ becomes a keyed mark that must cite a register row, which is the only surveyed mechanism that reaches a question stated in prose and which prices 2,323 existing marks. Whether `ruled` splits into an answer axis and a propagation axis, which is the IESG's middle state and the gap `decision-primitive-with-consumer.md:125-128` fell through. Whether a ruling given in session gets a required, form-checked pointer at a tracked copy, which is PEP's `Resolution` and the only surveyed answer to a decision made where no tool was looking.

## Where an editor's model stops and its presentation begins

### FD-30 one surveyed editor serves a terminal and a graphical surface from a single model, and it does so by carrying pixel fields that collapse to unit values on the cell lane

- state:    OPEN
- claim:    The question, aimed at the cut the author is choosing in `prog/scriba/`: in editors that drive both a terminal and a graphical surface, where does the boundary between the editor model and the presentation layer fall, what value crosses it, and does one model genuinely serve both surface kinds? **Ten systems were read at their own sources: GNU Emacs, sam, Acme, xi-editor, Kakoune, Neovim, Vim, Helix, vis, Project Oberon, with Zed's GPUI read for its platform list. The answer has three parts. (a) Exactly one surveyed system serves a text terminal and a graphical window from one model, and that is GNU Emacs; its seam is a glyph matrix, and the same `struct glyph` carries `pixel_width`, `ascent`, `descent` and `voffset` on both lanes, with the row's ascent forced to zero and its height to 1 on a terminal frame. (b) Every other seam takes one of two shapes. A byte-offset edit stream carrying zero presentation property (sam, Acme), or a grid or line list carrying a closed face vocabulary in cell units (Vim, Kakoune, Neovim, vis, xi-editor). (c) The two systems that declared a model-to-presentation boundary in order to serve structurally different surfaces both recorded its failure in their own documents: xi-editor's author wrote that the front-end/core process separation was a mistake, and Helix's architecture doc says the view layer meant to be frontend-agnostic is tied to the terminal UI.**
- measured: **1. THE TWO AXES, NAMED PER SYSTEM.** A client/server split multiplexes one editing session across several attached clients. A model/presentation split serves surfaces of different kinds. Kakoune is client/server only, and its design document says so in the multiplexing terms: KAKDESIGN:37 "multiple windows by supporting many clients on the same editing session," with the surrounding sentence naming the graphical environment it wants to exploit KAKDESIGN:36 "of that without hindering text mode support." Neovim is both axes at once: the UI protocol admits many simultaneous UIs and resolves capabilities across all of them, NVIMUI:35 "			  supported by all connected UIs (including |TUI|)." sam is both: one host part with one terminal part over a stream, and the stream exists to cross a machine boundary. Emacs is model/presentation with a multiplexing side effect, since one process owns many terminals. xi-editor, Helix, vis and Oberon are model/presentation only. Acme inverts the axis: the editor is the display program and the split faces outward at its clients. **A thin terminal client answers the multiplexing question and answers nothing about a pixel surface.** **2. GNU EMACS: one model, two seams, one glyph vocabulary.** Model side: buffers, windows, and the display iterator that produces glyphs. Presentation side: a per-terminal hook table. The terminal object is typed by output method, EMACSTERMH:55 "/* Output method of a terminal (and frames on this terminal, respectively).  */", and the graphical seam hangs off it as a function-pointer record that the terminal lane does not receive at all, EMACSTERMH:556 "  /* Window-based redisplay interface for this device (0 for tty". The tty lane instead gets frame-level hooks on the same terminal object, EMACSTERMH:574 "  void (*write_glyphs_hook) (struct frame *f, struct glyph *s, int n);", and the window lane gets the metric producer, EMACSDISPX:3075 "  void (*produce_glyphs) (struct it *it);". So Emacs has two seams of different shape over one model. **The value crossing both is the same glyph matrix**, and the matrices are distinguished by capability rather than by vocabulary, EMACSDISPX:773 "   1. Frame glyph matrices.  These are used for terminal frames whose" and EMACSDISPX:785 "   3. Free-standing window glyph matrices managing their own glyph" whose purpose is stated as EMACSDISPX:786 "   storage.  This form is used in window-based redisplay which" variable width and height fonts. **That one vocabulary is a pixel vocabulary, and the cell lane degenerates its fields.** A glyph carries EMACSDISPX:502 "  /* Width in pixels.  */", EMACSDISPX:505 "  /* Ascent and descent in pixels.  */" and a sub-cell vertical displacement, EMACSDISPX:508 "  /* Vertical offset.  If < 0, the glyph is displayed raised, if > 0". The degeneration is written into the row struct, EMACSDISPX:958 "     and height is 1 on terminal frames.  */". Emacs displays both kinds at once in one session, EMACSFRAME:54 "There are two classes of terminals" and EMACSFRAME:72 "both, graphical and text terminals, simultaneously." **The lesson is directional: Emacs generalized the cell lane up into pixels, and paid by making every cell-lane glyph carry three fields whose only legal value there is a constant.** **3. SAM: the seam carries byte offsets and nothing else.** Model side: the file as a string, the command language, the transaction log. Presentation side: proportional-font layout, tiled boxes, a bitmap. The split is two programs over a stream, SAMPAPER:163 "is implemented as two processes connected by a low-bandwidth stream," and it exists for bandwidth, SAMPAPER:2665 "The low bandwidth of an RS-232 link". **The protocol is typed as data edits at character positions**, SAMPAPER:2707 "popping up menus and interpreting the responses, the messages are about" data and SAMPAPER:2709 "For example, the host knows nothing about what is displayed on the screen," with the example given as an insert of one byte at location 123 in file 7. The wire type is a two-enum message set with one length-prefixed header, SAMMESG:17 "	Trequest,	/* request data to fill a hole */" and SAMMESG:49 "	Hgrow,		/* insert space in rasp */", and its only size constant is a text block bound, SAMMESG:6 "#define	TBLOCKSIZE 512		  /* largest piece of text sent to terminal */". **sam does not serve two surface kinds from one presentation.** Its terminal answer is to drop the display program and use the command language alone, SAMPAPER:2640 "part&rsquo;s command language to edit text on an ordinary terminal." All layout lives on the graphical side, where text is tiled as boxes over a bitmap, SAMPAPER:3142 "The update algorithms always use the" bitmap image. Pike priced the split in the same paper, SAMPAPER:2669 "a program in two parts is much harder to write and to debug". **4. ACME: the seam is a file namespace and the editor is the display program.** Presentation, input and text all live in one program that exports files, ACMEPAPER:162 "Acme is in part a file server that exports device-like files that may be" manipulated. What crosses is a character position and the bytes at it, ACMEPAPER:789 "file is written to set a character position within the body; the" data file is then read or written. Six files per window: `addr`, `body`, `ctl`, `data`, `event`, `tag`. **Acme has no terminal surface and no presentation vocabulary on its seam.** It answers the plugin question and leaves the surface question untouched. **5. XI-EDITOR: the seam was a line list with style spans, and its author reversed the decision.** Model side: a rope, CRDT edits, syntax highlighting in a third process. Presentation side: a native GUI per platform. The value crossing is a cached array of lines, XIPROTO:448 "considered an array of lines. Each line consists of the *text* (a string), a set" of cursor locations and style information, with styles interned by id, XIPROTO:479 "(It's not hard to imagine more style properties such as typeface, size, OpenType" features. **The viewport crossed in pixels**, XIPROTO:154 "in px units / points" rather than device pixels. **The core could not compute line breaks from that, so the protocol grew a synchronous reverse call into the presentation layer**, XIPROTO:618 "Asks the frontend to measure the display widths" of a group of strings, and XIPROTO:620 "These widths are used to determine how to" calculate line breaks. **That is the cell-to-pixel question answered by giving up: font metrics stayed on the presentation side and the model had to ask.** The retrospective names word wrap as the hardest consequence, XIRETRO:93 "live-resizing the window causes text to rewrap continuously" and, on the same line, "tearing-style artifacts" as what the async seam made hard to avoid. Scrolling took months where a shared data structure would have been direct, XIRETRO:95 "in-process data structure for the UI to query, it would have been quite straightforward". The verdict is explicit, XIRETRO:99 "I now firmly believe that the process separation between front-end and core" was a mistake, and the plugin split is kept as supportable in the same sentence. The motivation for the split collapsed too, XIRETRO:123 "There is no such thing as native GUI". One further cost was social, XIRETRO:157 "monolithic architectures, perhaps ironically, are *better* for community". **6. KAKOUNE: a typed presentation interface in cell units, terminal lane only in tree.** The seam is one abstract class whose draw call takes a computed display buffer, KAKUI:68 "  virtual void draw(const DisplayBuffer& display_buffer," with the width argument typed in columns, KAKUI:72 "                      ColumnCount widget_columns) = 0;" and the size query returning a cell coordinate, KAKUI:81 "  virtual DisplayCoord dimensions() = 0;". A `DisplayBuffer` is lines of atoms each carrying a `Face` of foreground, background, underline colour and a thirteen-bit attribute set. **The unit is the column and it is in the type**, so a pixel client inherits a monospace advance assumption from the interface itself. **7. NEOVIM: the seam is a cell grid, declared as such, and the pixel knowledge is pushed to the client as opaque strings.** NVIMUI:15 "The default UI model is a terminal-like grid with a single," NVIMUI:16 "monospace font. The UI can opt-in to have windows drawn on separate grids, and" have elements externalized. The redraw event is a run-length encoded cell span, NVIMUI:366 "	Redraw a continuous part of a `row` on a `grid`, starting at the column" given as NVIMUI:368 "	`[text(, hl_id, repeat)]` . `text` is the UTF-8 text that should be put in" a cell, against a highlight table defined by id. **Two entries in that closed face vocabulary have no cell-lane meaning and shipped anyway.** NVIMUI:327 "	`altfont`:		alternative font." and NVIMUI:328 "	`blend`:		blend level (0-100). Could be used by UIs to" support blending floating windows. The permission is advisory, so a terminal client ignores them and a pixel client honours them. **Font choice and cell metrics never enter the grid vocabulary at all**, and travel instead as option strings the model does not interpret, NVIMUI:206 "	- 'guifont'" alongside `guifontwide` and `linespace`. Per-window grids were added so a client could vary metrics, NVIMUI:591 "  occupies on the global layout. So the UI could use a different font size" per window. **The price of one vocabulary across clients of unequal capability is a downgrade to the intersection**, NVIMUI:35 "			  supported by all connected UIs (including |TUI|)." **8. VIM: the split was refused, and the graphical surface is modelled as a terminal type.** One global cell grid backs both lanes, VIMGLOBALS:32 " * The characters that are currently on the screen are kept in ScreenLines[]." with attributes in a parallel array and dimensions in rows and columns, VIMGLOBALS:29 "EXTERN long	Columns INIT(= 80);	// nr of columns in the screen". The GUI announces itself by naming a fake terminal, VIMGUIDOC:44 "- The 'term' option is set to " a pseudo-terminal named builtin_gui, VIMGUIDOC:44 " and terminal options are reset to" their GUI defaults. **Vim's answer to the graphical surface is to make the graphical surface pretend to be a terminal.** It ships, and the cell grid is the whole model of the screen. **9. HELIX AND VIS: a declared boundary that did not hold, and a boundary typed in cells from the start.** Helix names the intent and the outcome in consecutive lines, HELIXARCH:52 "The `view` layer was supposed to be a frontend-agnostic imperative library that" HELIXARCH:53 "would build on top of `core` to provide the common editor logic. Currently it's" tied to the terminal UI. **A frontend-agnostic layer with one frontend drifts into that frontend.** vis draws the boundary at a cell buffer and says so in the field comment, VISUI:141 "	VisCellBuffer cell_buffer; /* 2D grid of cells, at least as large as current terminal size */", where a cell is an eight-byte record with a width in terminal columns and a per-cell style, VISUI:98 "typedef alignas(16) struct {" and VISUI:108 "	VisCellStyle style;". vis has one surface kind. **10. PROJECT OBERON: presentation properties live inside the text value, and there is no cell lane.** The typed text is a piece list, and each piece carries a font, a colour and a vertical offset, OBTEXTS:23 "      fnt: Fonts.Font;" with the on-disk encoding naming the same three, OBTEXTS:13 "      run = fnt [name] col voff len. *)". **A minimal-by-construction system put font, colour and a sub-cell vertical displacement in the model.** The presentation side is a frame tree with a single dispatching procedure variable over extensible message records, OBDISPLAY:10 "    Handler* = PROCEDURE (F: Frame; VAR M: FrameMsg);" over a memory-mapped bitmap, OBDISPLAY:6 "    base = 0E7F00H;  (*adr of 1024 x 768 pixel, monocolor display frame*)". Layout advances per glyph, OBTEXTFRM:99 "  PROCEDURE Width (VAR R: Texts.Reader; len: LONGINT): INTEGER;" summing per-character advances, OBTEXTFRM:104 "      ox := ox + dx; INC(pos); Texts.Read(R, nextCh)". **Oberon's seam type is an open message sum extended by record subtyping, and its coordinates are pixels throughout.** **11. ZED AND GPUI: the terminal surface was declined.** GPUIREADME:3 "GPUI is a hybrid immediate and retained mode, GPU accelerated, UI framework" for Rust. Its platform feature list names Metal, Wayland, X11, Win32 and DirectWrite, with glyph rasterization gated on a font backend. **No terminal backend appears in that list.** Reading the absence as a deliberate refusal is inference from the platform list rather than a statement by the project. **12. WHAT THE CELL VOCABULARY COST THE SYSTEMS THAT FACED IT.** Four of them carried a cell-shaped value into a pixel surface and each paid differently. Emacs paid by making the vocabulary pixel-shaped and pinning three fields to constants on the cell lane. Vim paid by declaring the GUI a terminal type. Neovim paid by shipping two attributes with no cell meaning under an advisory rule, by moving font identity off the seam entirely, and by intersecting capabilities down to the weakest attached client. xi paid by discovering that line breaking cannot be computed on the model side and adding a synchronous callback to the presentation layer for string widths. **Nobody carried a cell vocabulary into pixels and kept one meaning for every property.** The two systems that refused the shared vocabulary (sam, Acme) put byte offsets on the seam and accepted that the terminal surface is a different program with a different interaction model. **13. THE LINEAGE CANDIDATES FOR A CUT AT `RendererFn`.** The live plug is `lib/protocol/render.chiral:34-35`, `(-> Str (Pair I64 I64) I64 Rendering)`, and `Rendering` at `:7-31` is a nine-constructor tree with a `Face` of foreground, background and attribute bits at `:37`. Five named lineages, with the match stated: (i) **Emacs**, one model and two seams of different shape, where the shared value is pixel-typed and the cell lane uses constants. This is the only lineage that has actually shipped both surfaces. (ii) **Kakoune and xi**, a per-line list of faced atoms measured in columns. **This is the closest match to `Rendering` as written**, because `rnd-cols` is declared `(-> Rendering I64)` at `lib/protocol/render.chiral:91` and its contract is stated in cell units at `:458` as the number of terminal columns a node advances the cursor by, with the standing precondition at `:465` that nothing in `Rendering` reflows. (iii) **Neovim**, a cell grid on the wire with font identity off-seam and advisory attributes. The tree's `(Pair I64 I64)` dimension argument is this shape, and `Rendering` has a different shape. (iv) **sam and Acme**, byte offsets on the seam and no presentation property at all. (v) **Oberon**, presentation properties inside the text value and an open message sum at the frame boundary. **The measured obstruction to (ii) is one type.** `rnd-cols` returning a single `I64` is a monospace-advance assumption in the value vocabulary; xi's `measure_width` is the same function under a proportional font and it returns a float per string per style and runs on the presentation side. The tree's own comment at `:469` already says that a wrapping constructor makes this signature wrong rather than imprecise. **14. WHETHER THE RECORD SUPPORTS A FULLY ALTERNATE GRAPHICAL VERSION.** The record splits. **It supports the author's position on the seam value and undercuts it on the codebase.** Supporting: no surveyed system carries one property vocabulary across both surface kinds without degeneration or advisory opt-out, which is the tree's standing ruling holding in four independent codebases; sam and Acme treat the terminal surface as a different interaction model reached by a different program; xi's retrospective and Helix's architecture doc are two recorded failures of a declared model-to-presentation boundary. Undercutting: **the one system that actually ships a terminal and a graphical surface kept one codebase and one model**, and its cost was three degenerate fields on a struct rather than a fork; xi's failure was a process and async boundary, which is a different decision from a second renderer in one program, and Levien's reversal was toward one process; Helix's drift argues that a second surface built outside the shared layer pulls the shared layer with it. **The supported reading of the survey is one model with two presentation seams of different shape and two property vocabularies, which is Emacs's shape, and the unsupported reading is a second copy of the model.** The 3,487 lines already free of `Rendering`, `ansi-` and `read-key` are the model side by this reading. **15. WHAT THE SOURCES DO NOT SETTLE.** **No source prices a second renderer registered against an existing typed plug.** Every surveyed boundary is either a process boundary or a vtable over one surface family, so the tree's actual question, a third registration against `RendererFn` that emits pixels, has no occupant in the survey. **No surveyed system states a rule for which property belongs to which lane.** Neovim's `blend` and `altfont` are the closest thing to one and they are permissions rather than a rule. **Neither the Emacs internals manual nor `xdisp.c` carries a pin here.** The redisplay seam here is quoted from `dispextern.h` and `termhooks.h` only, so this row makes no claim about the ordering of the two redisplay phases. **Kakoune's and vis's GUI story is absent rather than negative.** Only in-tree sources were read, the in-tree `UserInterface` implementation is the terminal one, and no source was pinned that says a pixel client exists or cannot. **Zed's refusal of a terminal frontend is inference.** **Project Oberon's book carries no pin**, because `PO.System.pdf` is a PDF and a line-addressed pin over it would carry a transcriber's reading; the module sources were pinned instead, so this row quotes Oberon's code and none of Wirth's prose. **Two fetches failed and are named**: `neovim/neovim/master/runtime/doc/ui.txt` returned HTTP 404 on 2026-09-15 and the protocol is pinned from the `release-0.11` branch at that path; `projectoberon.net/wirth/ProjectOberon/Sources/*.Mod.txt` returned HTTP 404 and the ETH mirror at `people.inf.ethz.ch` served the same files.
- evidence: **Nineteen pins added 2026-09-15 in `.planning/sources/`, every one origin `raw`.** `EMACSDISPX` (`git.savannah.gnu.org/cgit/emacs.git/plain/src/dispextern.h`, 4,023 lines), `EMACSTERMH` (same tree, `src/termhooks.h`, 993), `EMACSFRAME` (`gnu.org/software/emacs/manual/html_node/elisp/Frames.html`, 226, served as HTML and pinned as the bytes served), `SAMPAPER` (`doc.cat-v.org/plan_9/4th_edition/papers/sam/`, 3,829, Pike's 1987 Software-Practice and Experience paper as mirrored, served as HTML), `SAMMESG` (`9fans/plan9port/master/src/cmd/sam/mesg.h`, 131), `ACMEPAPER` (`doc.cat-v.org/plan_9/4th_edition/papers/acme/`, 1,590, served as HTML), `XIRETRO` (`raphlinus/raphlinus.github.io/master/_posts/2020-06-21-xi-retrospective.md`, 198, the authored Markdown behind the published post; the rendered HTML at `raphlinus.github.io` was fetched first and rejected as a pin because three metadata tags repeat the whole article and every span in it resolves four times), `XIPROTO` (`xi-editor/xi-editor/master/docs/docs/frontend-protocol.md`, 797), `KAKDESIGN` (`mawww/kakoune/master/doc/design.asciidoc`, 152), `KAKUI` (`mawww/kakoune/master/src/user_interface.hh`, 94), `NVIMUI` (`neovim/neovim/release-0.11/runtime/doc/ui.txt`, 865), `HELIXARCH` (`helix-editor/helix/master/docs/architecture.md`, 128), `VISUI` (`martanne/vis/master/ui.h`, 202), `VIMGUIDOC` (`vim/vim/master/runtime/doc/gui.txt`, 1,298), `VIMGLOBALS` (`vim/vim/master/src/globals.h`, 2,191), `OBTEXTS` (`people.inf.ethz.ch/wirth/ProjectOberon/Sources/Texts.Mod.txt`, 537), `OBDISPLAY` (same directory, `Display.Mod.txt`, 190), `OBTEXTFRM` (same directory, `TextFrames.Mod.txt`, 856), `GPUIREADME` (`zed-industries/zed/main/crates/gpui/README.md`, 98). `xlat pin` stamps a UTC clock, so each `.meta` reads `pinned: 2026-09-15`. Live tree spans, measured 2026-09-15 at `7e0eb24`: `lib/protocol/render.chiral:7-31` (the nine `Rendering` constructors), `:34-35` (`RendererFn`), `:37` (`Face`), `:41-42` (`Mode`), `:91` (the `rnd-cols` declaration), `:451-475` (its contract, with the cell-unit statement at `:458` and the no-reflow precondition at `:465-470`). The per-file presentation-coupling counts over `prog/scriba/` were supplied to this run and this run re-measured none of them. `ledger-lint` measured after this row at the figure in the report.
- checked:  2026-09-15
- element:  `none`. This row surveys and prices; it designs nothing and opens no roster row. It leaves two things in the author's hands. Whether the cut is one model with two presentation seams of different shape, which is the only surveyed shape that shipped both surfaces, or a second copy of the model, which no surveyed system chose and which xi's and Helix's records argue against for different reasons. Whether `rnd-cols`, declared `(-> Rendering I64)` in cell units, is amended to a metric the presentation side supplies, which is what xi's `measure_width` became and what the standing property-vocabulary ruling predicts.

### FD-31 no surveyed system carries a span's identity and its role in one single-valued field, the two whose identity rides on rendered output at zero width each give it its own channel, and the one specification that states a rule forbids deriving any meaning from an identifier's value

- state:    OPEN
- claim:    The question, aimed at `Doc`'s `d-tag` and at D10: how do systems attach a stable identity to a span of rendered text such that the identity survives an edit, and do they carry that identity on the same channel as the span's semantic or visual role or on a separate one? **Fifteen mechanisms were read at their own sources across eleven systems: GNU Emacs (text properties, overlays, markers), Peritext, Yjs, Automerge, CodeMirror 6, ProseMirror, tree-sitter, Unison, LSP semantic tokens, OSC 8 terminal hyperlinks, org-mode, and the W3C Web Annotation Data Model. The answer has four parts. (a) Every surveyed system that carries both an identity and a role for a span carries them in two fields of one record or in two separate mechanisms, and none carries them in one single-valued slot. (b) The two systems whose identity rides on the rendered artifact at zero rendered width, HTML and OSC 8, each put that identity in a channel the role does not use: the `id` attribute beside `class`, and the `id=` parameter beside SGR. (c) The HTML standard states the rule directly: an identifier is an opaque string and no particular meaning may be derived from its value. A face-registry key is a meaning derived from the value. (d) No surveyed system gives a span an identity that survives an arbitrary edit to the span's own content. Every intrinsic scheme gives identity to the atoms and derives the span from two anchored endpoints, and GNU Emacs states the impossibility result for span identity as its own design reason.**
- measured: **1. THE THREE STRATEGIES, ASSIGNED.** *Mapping*: CodeMirror 6, ProseMirror, tree-sitter, LSP. *Intrinsic identity*: Emacs markers and overlays, Peritext, Yjs, Automerge. *Content addressing*: Unison, the W3C annotation model's quote selectors, and this tree's own `block-id`. The third has the weakness the question names, and the record confirms it below at §14. **2. GNU EMACS: THREE MECHANISMS, AND A STATED IMPOSSIBILITY RESULT FOR SPAN IDENTITY.** A text property is per character position and the keyspace is an open plist, EMACSTEXTP:2976 "property list}, much like the property list of a symbol (@pxref{Property" lists, where the face is one key among many, EMACSTEXTP:2986 "property @code{face} specifies the faces for displaying the character", and the openness is stated as a licence, EMACSTEXTP:3557 "have no standard meaning, and you can use them as you like." **A property span has no identity at all, and the manual devotes a node to why.** Other editors let a user name intervals; Emacs refused, EMACSTEXTP:4592 "deliberately provided a different sort of interface in Emacs Lisp to" avoid paradoxical behaviour under text modification. The reason is an edit: kill part of a one-interval buffer and yank it back and you hold two intervals with the same properties, coalesce them and two adjacent equal intervals become one, EMACSTEXTP:4604 "Thus, editing does not preserve the distinction" between one interval and two. The consequence is a refusal to answer the question at all, EMACSTEXTP:4622 "we have not implemented asking questions" about where intervals start or end. **The identity-bearing mechanism was then shipped separately and the manual says so in that same node**, EMACSTEXTP:4630 "Emacs also provides explicit intervals as a presentation feature; see" Overlays. An overlay is an object with two positions the editing primitives adjust, EMACSDISPLAY:1594 "Editing the text of the buffer adjusts the beginning and end of each" overlay so that it stays with the text, and it carries its own property plist including a face. **The channel split is stated as a difference of kind**, EMACSDISPLAY:1821 "  Text properties are considered a part of the text; overlays and" EMACSDISPLAY:1822 "their properties are specifically considered not to be part of the" text, with the measurable consequence that EMACSDISPLAY:1824 "preserves text properties, but does not try to preserve overlays." A marker is the one-position case of the same intrinsic scheme, EMACSMARKER:12 "beginning of the buffer automatically whenever text is inserted or" deleted, with exactly three fields, EMACSMARKER:38 "  A marker has three attributes: the marker position, the marker" buffer, and the insertion type, and the boundary rule is the third of them, EMACSMARKER:342 "@var{type}.  If @var{type} is @code{t}, @var{marker} will advance when" text is inserted at its position. **So Emacs answers the channel question three times and never once by sharing a field.** **3. PERITEXT: IDENTITY ON EVERY ATOM, ROLE ON A SEPARATE OPERATION, JOINED BY AN ANCHOR.** A mark is defined as a span property, PERITEXT:452 "is any property that is applied to a contiguous substring of the text", and every operation carries its own immutable id, PERITEXT:454 "Every operation that modifies the state of the document is given a unique, immutable identifier called" `opId`. Identity belongs to the character, PERITEXT:461 "The key idea of most text CRDTs is to represent the text as a sequence of characters, and to give each character a unique identifier." **A formatting operation is a separate record whose identity field and role field are two distinct keys**, PERITEXT:501 "  opId: &quot;18@A&quot;," beside a `markType` of bold, and per-role metadata rides a third key, PERITEXT:524 "  markType: &quot;link&quot;," beside a `url`. The span itself holds no id: it is two anchors, each a character opId plus a before-or-after side, and that side is the boundary rule Emacs calls insertion type. **Peritext rejects the in-band alternative by name and prices it.** The rejected shape is formatting carried inside the content sequence, PERITEXT:353 "insert special hidden control characters into the plain text sequence to denote the beginning and end of formatting spans", which the article attributes to Yjs and then shows losing author intent under a concurrent edit. The price of intrinsic identity is stated twice, PERITEXT:775 "we store each character as a separate object (which uses a lot of memory)" for the naive form, and PERITEXT:777 "at a cost of about one byte per operation" for Automerge's compressed form. **4. YJS AND AUTOMERGE: THE IDENTITY TOKEN IS A DIFFERENT API FROM THE FORMATTING API.** Yjs states the rot this tree measured, YJSRELPOS:7 "Normal index-positions (expressed as integers) are not convenient to use because the index-range is invalidated as soon as a remote change manipulates the document." Its answer is intrinsic, YJSRELPOS:9 "A _relative position_ is fixated to an element in the shared document and is not affected by remote changes." Formatting arrives through a different call on a different object, YJSTEXT:49 "    Assign formatting attributes to a range of text." Automerge is the same split in two exported functions, AMIMPL:1780 "export function getCursor<T>(" returning an opaque token whose portability is the stated point, AMIMPL:1755 " * The string representation is shareable, and so you can use this both" to edit and to share positions, against AMIMPL:1820 "export function mark<T>(" taking a range, a name and a value. **Neither library offers one call that assigns an identity and a role together.** **5. CODEMIRROR 6 AND PROSEMIRROR: POSITIONS ARE MAPPED, AND IDENTITY WHERE IT EXISTS IS A SECOND FIELD.** CodeMirror transforms the position through the change, CMCHANGE:120 "  mapPos(pos: number, assoc?: number): number", with the same boundary rule under a third name, CMCHANGE:108 "  /// `assoc` indicates which side the position should be associated" with. A decorated range carries mapping metadata and no id, CMRANGESET:19 "  /// The mode with which the location of the range should be mapped" when its ends coincide, and equality falls back to object identity, CMRANGESET:7 "  /// rangesets. The default implementation compares by identity." A mark decoration is style only, CMDECOR:224 "  /// Create a mark decoration, which influences the styling of the" content in its range, and its equality is structural over the style fields, CMDECOR:295 "    return this == other || other instanceof MarkDecoration && this.tagName == other.tagName && attrsEq(this.attrs, other.attrs)". **The one place CodeMirror admits an identity is a free slot beside the style fields**, CMDECOR:212 "    /// include additional properties in there to store metadata about" your decoration. ProseMirror maps the same way, PMMAP:4 "  /// Map a position through this object. When given, `assoc` (should" be -1 or 1. **Its decoration carries the two channels as two named fields of one object.** Role is `attrs`, PMDECOR:247 "export type DecorationAttrs = {" holding `nodeName`, `class`, PMDECOR:256 "  /// A string of CSS to be _added_ to the node's existing `style` property." and PMDECOR:259 "  /// Any other properties are treated as regular DOM attributes." Identity is an optional key in `spec`, consulted only by equality, PMDECOR:42 "       (this.spec.key && this.spec.key == other.spec.key ||". **ProseMirror is the closest surveyed shape to a `d-tag` that needs both, and it holds them in two slots of one record.** **6. TREE-SITTER: TWO FIELDS ON THE NODE, AND THE ID IS NOT WHAT SURVIVES.** The node struct carries an identity pointer and a tree pointer, TSAPI:134 "  uint32_t context[4];" preceding the `id` and `tree` fields, and equality is exposed, TSAPI:743 " * Check if two nodes are identical." The role is a separate accessor over the grammar symbol, TSAPI:503 " * Get the node's type as a null-terminated string." **Across an edit the strategy is mapping, and the identity does not carry the edit.** A held node is transformed by the edit, TSAPI:729 " * Edit the node to keep it in-sync with source code that has been edited.", and the normal path is that the tree does it for you, TSAPI:733 " * afterward will already reflect the edit. You only need to use [`ts_node_edit`]" for a node you keep. **What is compared across a reparse is structure**, TSAPI:476 " * The returned ranges indicate areas where the hierarchical structure of syntax" nodes has changed. So tree-sitter answers `which bytes changed shape` and never `is this the same node`. **7. UNISON: IDENTITY IS THE CONTENT HASH, AND CONTINUITY ACROSS AN EDIT LIVES IN TWO SEPARATE MUTABLE RECORDS.** The identity claim is the project's own headline, UNISONRD:18 "in which function are identified by a hash of their implementation rather than by name, and code is stored as its AST in a database". Hashes are the only key, UNISONV2:16 "Hashes are not stored anywhere else in the database." **An edit therefore yields a different definition, and the thing that says `the same definition, edited` is a patch.** The codebase design document states the operation, UNISONBR:277 "moves names from old hash to new hash, transitively, to the type-preserving frontier". The repository format states its asymmetry, UNISONV2:223 "the old version of a definition doesn't have to be available to use the patch". **So Unison holds an immutable content address and a mutable name-to-reference relation, and the second is what survives the edit.** **8. LSP SEMANTIC TOKENS: THE NEGATIVE CASE, WITH THE MITIGATION AIMED ELSEWHERE.** A token is five integers, LSPSEMTOK:104 "Each token is represented using 5 integers." with positions encoded against the previous token because most tokens hold still relative to each other, LSPSEMTOK:104 "There are two different ways how the position of a token can be expressed in a file". Role is two of the five, resolved against a negotiated table, LSPSEMTOK:109 "- at index `5*i+3` - `tokenType`: will be looked up in `SemanticTokensLegend.tokenTypes`." whose contents the server declares, LSPSEMTOK:90 "	 * The token types a server uses." **A token has no identity field.** The protocol's `resultId` identifies a whole response, LSPSEMTOK:380 "	 * An optional result ID. If provided and clients support delta updating," the client may ask for a delta, and the delta is edits over the integer array, LSPSEMTOK:426 "	 * The result ID of a previous response. The result ID can either point to" a full or a delta response. **So LSP's mitigation for position rot is to make the transmission cheaper and never to make a span nameable.** **9. OSC 8: THE ONE SURVEYED IDENTITY THAT RIDES ON RENDERED TERMINAL OUTPUT AT ZERO COLUMNS, AND IT IS ITS OWN PARAMETER.** The sequence carries an optional parameter list and exactly one key is defined, OSC8SPEC:73 "Currently only the `id` key is defined, see below." **The parameter exists because the render loses the grouping**, OSC8SPEC:103 "There's a nontrivial question though: Which cells to underline on hover?" with the diagnosis that a terminal lacks the information about which cells belong together. The remedy is named as a connector, OSC8SPEC:107 "In order to be able to do this, the lowercase `id` parameter was introduced which connects the cells together." and its semantics are exact, OSC8SPEC:109 "Character cells that have the same target URI and the same nonempty `id` are always underlined together on mouseover." **Colour and attributes travel on SGR, a different escape sequence entirely, so the identity channel and the style channel share only the byte stream.** **The spec's own suggestion for what to put in an `id` is the rotting thing**, OSC8SPEC:121 "Such an `id` might perhaps be the file offset, or the (row, column) tuple where the hyperlink starts." **10. HTML: THREE ATTRIBUTE CHANNELS, AND THE CONFORMANCE RULE THAT SETTLES D10.** The role channel is an open token set with no restrictions, HTMLDOM:2000 "There are no additional restrictions on the tokens authors can use in the" class attribute, and the standard's advice on how to key it is the first half of this row's answer, HTMLDOM:2001 "the nature of the content, rather than values that describe the desired presentation of the" content. The identity channel is a different attribute with a uniqueness requirement, HTMLDOM:2007 "value must be unique amongst all the" IDs in the element's tree. **The rule that forbids the dual use is stated in the standard's own words**, HTMLDOM:2028 "Identifiers are opaque strings. Particular meanings should not be derived from the value of the" id attribute. A third channel was added for everything else, HTMLDOM:2650 "are intended to store custom" data, state, annotations and similar. **So the most deployed answer in existence is three separate attributes, and the only one of the three that is an open role keyspace carries spec-level advice against putting presentation in it.** **11. ORG-MODE: THE BY-HAND PRECEDENT, AND WHERE THE UNIQUENESS INVARIANT SITS.** A link resolves to a property, ORGMANUAL:3242 "specifically targets the entry with the =CUSTOM_ID= property set to" the named value, where the property lives in a drawer beside the heading and never in the heading text. **The invariant is on the author and the enforcement is a linter**, ORGMANUAL:3270 "Note that you must make sure custom IDs, dedicated targets, and names" ORGMANUAL:3271 "are unique throughout the document.  Org provides a linter to assist" you in the process. That is the same arrangement `records/README:80` already runs by hand in this tree. **12. W3C WEB ANNOTATION: CONTENT-DERIVED ANCHORING, AND THE SPEC'S OWN CONCESSION.** A quote selector is three content fields, W3CANNO:2824 "have exactly 1 <code>exact</code> property" required, with W3CANNO:2830 "have exactly 1 <code>prefix</code> property" and a suffix each recommended. The model permits several alternative selectors per target, W3CANNO:2493 "relationships associated with a Specific Resource." with multiple selectors expected to select the same content at differing precision. **The concession is in the spec**, W3CANNO:3108 "Selections made by users may be extensive and/or cross over internal boundaries in the representation" making a single robust selector difficult. **A standard that ships several fallback anchors is a standard that did not find one address that holds.** **13. WHETHER ANY SURVEYED SYSTEM PUT IDENTITY AND STYLING ON ONE OPEN KEYSPACE, AND WHAT IT COST.** **Zero of fifteen mechanisms put them in one single-valued field.** Two of them share one open namespace by occupying distinct keys in it. Emacs text properties are a plist where `face` is one name and any other name is available, which costs nothing because a plist holds many keys at once; the thing Emacs refused is narrower and is the exact thing shard G wants, namely an identity for the span. HTML's `class` is the nearest real instance of dual use, since one open token set drives CSS selectors, `getElementsByClassName` and, in the standard's words, other such features. **What that cost is visible in the standard as two additions beside it**: a separate `id` attribute carrying a uniqueness requirement and a no-derived-meaning rule, and a separate `data-*` family for custom data. The remaining thirteen carry the two in two fields of one record (Peritext `opId` beside `markType`, ProseMirror `spec.key` beside `attrs`, tree-sitter `id` beside `type`, HTML `id` beside `class`) or in two mechanisms (Emacs overlay object beside property plist, Automerge `getCursor` beside `mark`, OSC 8 `id=` beside SGR). **14. WHETHER A CONTENT ADDRESS CAN SERVE AS AN IDENTITY THAT SURVIVES AN EDIT.** **On this record it cannot, and the system that leaned hardest on content addressing pays for the gap with a second mutable structure.** Unison's identity is the hash of the implementation (UNISONRD:18), so an edit is a different definition by construction, and what carries the edited definition forward is a name relation plus a patch that moves names from the old hash to the new one (UNISONBR:277), with the old version not required to be present (UNISONV2:223). The annotation model's quote selector is the fuzzy form of the same idea and the spec concedes its robustness (W3CANNO:3108). **This tree's own `block-id` is the strict form and is already labelled as such in the code**, `lib/protocol/apc.chiral:8`, verbatim: `"Every addressable node (r-section / r-hole) carries a content-hash `block-id`"`, computed at `:141` and re-verified by the decoder at `:227`. So `block-id` is an integrity check over one version of a node, and it is a different object from shard G. **15. ZERO WIDTH, PER SCHEME.** Achievable and invisible in the render: Emacs text properties (attributes on a character, contributing no columns), Emacs overlays and markers (outside the text entirely), the CRDT opIds and cursors (model-side), ProseMirror and CodeMirror decorations (declared not to be part of the document), tree-sitter node ids (pointers). **Achievable and carried in the rendered artifact at zero rendered width: exactly two, HTML's `id` and OSC 8's `id=`.** Both are in-band on the output stream and consume no columns, which is the shape `lib/prelude/doc.chiral:83` already has, verbatim: `"(d-tag   (name Str) (body Doc))) ; a semantic role: zero width, zero text."` **One scheme fails the constraint and is named by name**: formatting carried as hidden control characters inside the content sequence (PERITEXT:353), which occupies positions in the sequence and is the shape whose merge anomaly the article demonstrates. Org's `<<dedicated target>>` also fails it, since the marker is literal text in the file. **16. THE DIRECT ANSWER TO D10.** **The record supports a separate channel for the address and does not support folding the address into the role keyspace.** Three pieces of evidence, in descending strength. (i) The only specification in the survey that states a rule about it forbids the fold, HTMLDOM:2028, and a face-registry key is precisely a particular meaning derived from an identifier's value. (ii) Both in-band zero-width precedents give the address a channel the role does not use: `id` beside `class`, `id=` beside SGR. (iii) No surveyed mechanism carries both in one single-valued slot, and the two records that come closest, Peritext's mark operation and ProseMirror's `Decoration`, each hold them as two named fields of one object. **The reading this supports for C1 is that `Role` may close into a sum, provided the address moves to its own carrier.** The two carriers the record witnesses are a second field on the same node, which is ProseMirror's and Peritext's shape and would make `d-tag` `(d-tag (role Role) (addr ...) (body Doc))`, and a separate object beside the text, which is Emacs's overlay shape and would put shard G outside `Doc` altogether. **The reading the record does not support is an address-bearing arm of `Role` whose payload a renderer resolves through the same lookup as a face.** No surveyed system resolves identity and style through one table, and `lib/prelude/doc.chiral:84-86` says the current `Str` is resolved through exactly one such table, verbatim: "`Str` here is a FACE-REGISTRY KEY -- the open set protocol/render's `lookup-face` already indexes". **17. WHAT THE SOURCES DO NOT SETTLE.** **No surveyed system gives a span an identity that survives an arbitrary edit to that span's own content.** Every intrinsic scheme hands identity to the atoms and derives the span from two anchored endpoints, and Emacs states the reason as a result rather than an omission (EMACSTEXTP:4604, :4622). This tree's P4 wants the thing no source in this survey delivers. **No source prices a zero-width in-band address inside a typed document tree.** HTML and OSC 8 are the two in-band precedents and both are byte-stream formats read by a parser. **No source offers a rot-resistant scheme for what to put in an in-band id.** OSC 8's own suggestion is a file offset or a row-column tuple (OSC8SPEC:121), which is the rot `docs/arcs/text-tools-arc.md:127-131` already measured as BA-20 and BA-21. **No source sizes the mutable side of the Unison arrangement**, so this row makes no claim about what a name-to-address relation costs or what happens when the name is gone. **Four coverage gaps are named.** Roam and org-roam block references were not reached; org-mode's `CUSTOM_ID` is the pinned instance of that family and org-roam carries no pin here. Automerge's cursor was read from the JavaScript wrapper alone, so no claim is made about its byte encoding. The Peritext pin is the published web article served as HTML, and the ACM paper was not pinned. `docs/branchless.md` is a design document inside the Unison repository, and its statements are reported as that project's own design note. **This run measured nothing in this tree's own code beyond the four spans it cites**, and it did not survey whether `d-tag`'s open `Str` has ever been used for an address anywhere in `lib/` or `prog/`.
- evidence: **Twenty-one pins added 2026-09-15 in `.planning/sources/`, every one origin `raw`.** `EMACSTEXTP` (`git.savannah.gnu.org/cgit/emacs.git/plain/doc/lispref/text.texi`, 6,686 lines), `EMACSMARKER` (same tree, `doc/lispref/markers.texi`, 742), `EMACSDISPLAY` (same tree, `doc/lispref/display.texi`, 9,843), `PERITEXT` (`inkandswitch.com/peritext/`, 790, the published article served as HTML and pinned as the bytes served), `YJSRELPOS` (`yjs/docs/main/api/relative-positions.md`, 60), `YJSTEXT` (`yjs/docs/main/api/shared-types/y.text.md`, 126), `AMIMPL` (`codemirror/view/main/src/decoration.ts`, 418), `PMMAP` (`ProseMirror/prosemirror-transform/master/src/map.ts`, 284), `PMDECOR` (`ProseMirror/prosemirror-view/master/src/decoration.ts`, 793), `TSAPI` (`tree-sitter/tree-sitter/master/lib/include/tree_sitter/api.h`, 1,525), `UNISONRD` (`unisonweb/unison/trunk/README.md`, 102), `UNISONV2` (same repo, `docs/repoformats/v2.markdown`, 334), `UNISONBR` (same repo, `docs/branchless.md`, 709), `LSPSEMTOK` (`microsoft/language-server-protocol/gh-pages/_specifications/lsp/3.18/language/semanticTokens.md`, 558), `OSC8SPEC` (egmontkob's statement), `:141` (`block-id`'s definition), `:227` (the decoder's re-verification), `docs/banks/text.md:53` (shard G, owed, render half built), `:75-77` (the open-keyspace statement D10 quotes),
